[
31
]
we use a coarser grid
0=t_{i_{1}}<t_{i_{2}}<\ldots<t_{i_{K}}=t_{L}=\tau_{(1)}-\tau_{(L)}
to construct the coupling. More precisely, let us introduce auxiliary processes
\bar{Y}^{(k)}
as follows: for
k\leq K

\begin{cases}d\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t}=\left[\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t}+2s(\tau_{(1)}-t,\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t})\right]dt+\sqrt{2}dB_{t}^{\prime},&t\in[0,t_{i_{k}}],\,k>1\\
d\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t}=\left[\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t}+2\bar{s}(\tau_{(1)}-t,\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t}|\tau_{(L)}-t_{i},\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{1}:t_{i}})\right]dt+\sqrt{2}dB_{t}^{\prime},&t\in(t_{i},t_{i+1}]\text{ for }i\geq i_{k},\\
\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{0}\sim p_{\tau_{(1)}}.\end{cases}

(6.13)

In other words, the process
Y^{(k)}
first coincides with the true backward process
Y
given by (
2.4
) on the interval
[0,t_{i_{k}}]
, and for the rest of time
(t_{i_{k}},\tau_{(1)}]
it follows the approximate dynamic (
6.12
).

We bound the Wasserstein distance between the target distribution
X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle dist.}}{{=}}Y^{(1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}
and its approximation
\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}}
as

W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}},X_{0})\leq W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{\tau_{(1)}})+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}})+W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(K)}_{\tau_{(1)}},X_{0}).

In what follows, we explain the structure of the argument and defer the technical details to Appendix E. The proof is based on the above interpolation between the ideal reverse process and the discretized process. The interpolation isolates three distinct sources of error.

First, the discretized process is initialized from a standard Gaussian, whereas the ideal reverse process should be initialized from
p_{\tau_{(1)}}
. Since
\tau_{(1)}\geq\overline{T}-1
, this error by
[
7
]
is exponentially small in
\overline{T}
:

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}\left(\bar{Y}_{t_{L}},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{t_{L}}\right)\lesssim\sqrt{D}e^{-\overline{T}}.

Second, even if the process follows the exact reverse dynamics until the last grid point, it still has to replace the final transition from
X_{\tau_{(L)}}
to
X_{0}
by the denoiser. Since
\tau_{(L)}\leq 2\underline{T}
, the high-probability control from Assumption
6
gives

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}\left(\bar{Y}^{(K)}_{t_{L}},X_{0}\right)\lesssim\sqrt{\underline{T}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(L\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\delta^{-1})}

Finally, the remaining terms
W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}})
are only impacted by the discretization over the interval
[T_{K-k},T_{K-k+1}]
. The self-consistency modification of the denoiser ensures that the endpoints of the bridge remain localized, while Girsanov’s theorem reduces the comparison of the two path laws to the accumulated score error on that block. Averaging over the randomized mesh then allows the discrete score error to be controlled by the continuous denoising loss. This gives, for every dyadic block
k
,

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}\left(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{L}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t_{L}}\right)\leq 4R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right)\TV\left(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{[0,t_{L}]},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{[0,t_{L}]}\right)\\
\lesssim\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(L\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\delta^{-1})}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}^{2}+\delta+\kappa d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+\overline{T})}.

Summing the above bounds over the dyadic blocks gives that

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}},X_{0})\lesssim\sqrt{D}e^{-\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\underline{T}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(L\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\delta^{-1})}\\
+K\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(L\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\delta^{-1})}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}^{2}+\delta+\kappa(d\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{W})(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+\overline{T})}.

By Proposition
6.2
,
L=O\left(\kappa^{-1}(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+\overline{T})\right)
and
K=O\left(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+\log\overline{T}\right)
.

Let
\hat{\mu}_{u}=\textrm{Law}(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}}|u)
, and
\hat{\mu}=\mathbb{E}_{u}\hat{\mu}_{u}
be the law of
\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}}
. Then, substituting
\kappa=\delta=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}^{2}
into the previous display and applying convexity of Wasserstein distance we get

\displaystyle W_{1}(\mu,\hat{\mu})\leq\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}(\mu,\hat{\mu}_{u})\lesssim\sqrt{D}e^{-\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\underline{T}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(\varepsilon^{-1}_{\mathrm{denoiser}}\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\overline{T})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}\log(\overline{T}\cdot\underline{T}^{-1})\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(\varepsilon^{-1}_{\mathrm{denoiser}}\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\overline{T})}\sqrt{(d\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{W})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})}

7
Alternative Approach: Kernel-Based Score Function Approximation

An alternative approach to building estimator
\hat{s}
develops the idea that we used in case
d\leq 2
, and based on the kernel-density estimator of
\mu
constructed by
[
15
]
.

We assume usual assumptions,
\mu
is a measure satisfying Assumptions
2.2
–
2.2
supported on a
d
-dimensional manifold
M
, and we observed
n
i.i.d. samples
\mathcal{Y}=\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\}
from
\mu
.

In Theorem 3.7,
Divol [15]
constructs the kernel-density estimator
\hat{\mu}
in two stages. As a first step, applying results of
[
2
]
that we discussed in Section
5.1
they solve optimization problem (
5.1
) and construct an approximating surface
M^{*}=\cup M^{*}_{i}
. Then, using smooth partitioning functions
\chi_{i}
, they construct a smooth approximation
\widehat{\Vol}(dy^{*})
to uniform measure
dy
as
\widehat{\Vol}(dy^{*})=\sum\chi_{i}(y^{*})dy^{*}
, i.e. for
\phi\in C^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{D})

\int_{M^{*}}\phi(y^{*})\widehat{\Vol}(dy^{*})=\sum\int_{M^{*}_{i}}\phi(y^{*})\chi_{i}(y^{*})dy^{*}.

As a second step, they construct a smoothing kernel
K_{n}(x)
on
\mathbb{R}^{D}
as a rescaling of a special kernel
K
, see
[
15
, Section 3]
for precise description. Denote as

\nu_{n}=\nu_{n}(\mathcal{Y}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{y_{i}\in\mathcal{Y}}\delta_{y_{i}}

the empirical density function. The estimator
\hat{\mu}_{n}
is defined as

\hat{\mu}_{n}=K_{n}*\left(\frac{\nu_{n}}{K_{n}*\hat{\Vol}M}\right).

Theorem 7.1
.

[
15
, Theorem 3.7]

For
d\geq 3
, and any
1\leq p\leq\infty

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{Y}\sim\mu^{\otimes n}}W_{p}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n},\mu\right)\lesssim n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}.

We define
\hat{s}_{n}
as the score function of
\hat{\mu}_{n}
, then applying Proposition
5.6

4\sigma^{2}_{T_{k}}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\hat{s}_{n}(t,x)-s(t,x)\|^{2}p_{t}(x)dxdt\leq W^{2}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\mu}_{n}).

Since
T_{k+1}=2T_{k}
we have
\sigma_{t}\leq 2\sigma_{T_{k}}
for all
t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
. So, taking expectation and summing over all
k\leq K=\log(n)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{Y}\sim\mu^{\otimes n}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\sigma^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\hat{s}_{n}(t,x)-s(t,x)\right\rVert^{2}p(t,x)dxdt\lesssim n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\log n.

At first glance, this gives an alternative and very short proof of Theorem
3.1
. However, in this case, diffusion models are merely used to sample from the already known distribution
\hat{\mu}_{n}
, while in the results we presented, we indeed learn the distribution by solving the empirical risk minimization problem, which is much closer to what happens in practice.

8
Conclusion and Future work

We have presented a novel approach for analyzing diffusion models under the manifold hypothesis in a high-dimensional setting. This approach allows us to show that: (i) neural networks can approximate the normalized score function
\sigma_{t}s(t,x)
with the ambient-dimension-free convergence rate
n^{-2(\alpha+1)/(2\alpha+d)}\polylog(n)
w.r.t. the score matching loss; and (ii) diffusion models achieve the optimal (up to
\polylog
) convergence rate
n^{-(\alpha+1)/(2\alpha+d)}
in the
W_{1}
metric.

Our results can be extended in several directions. Firstly, it is unclear if Assumption
3
is necessary. We believe that similarly to
[
15
]
inequality
\beta\geq\alpha+1
should be enough.

Another interesting direction is the study of which assumptions might be relaxed. The assumption that density
p(y)>p_{\min}
is lower bounded is quite strong, however, the bounds we got are polynomial on
C_{\log}
, which itself is only logarithmic in
p_{\min}
, so any smoothing, e.g. by the heat kernel could help.
Also, it is interesting to study the case when the observed samples are corrupted by a bounded ambient noise. Finally in a recent line of works
[
16
,
37
]
have obtained generalization without early stopping without the manifold hypothesis, notably because the score has the required smoothness uniformly over
t
. It is unclear if this can be achieved in the context of degenerate distributions over a smooth manifold.

Finally note that our approach can be applied to other diffusion models, for instance in the form
X_{t}=X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{d}
with
\sigma_{t}
going to infinity, since this diffusion model parametrization differs only by rescaling and time reparameterization. Rescaling can be accommodated by changing the fixed first and last layers, while time reparameterization by a small neural network of
\polylog
size. This argument also covers stochastic interpolants parametrization
[
5
]
.

A
we provide auxiliary geometric statements that will be used further in the proofs. In Appendix
B
we prove the results of Section 4. Appendix
C
contains the proofs of the results in Section
5
. Appendix
D
contains omitted details of Section
3
. Also in Appendix
D.5
, we correct
[
31
]
, Theorem C.4 that plays an important role in the proof of our main result Theorem
3.1
. In Appendix
F
we recall lemmas on neural networks approximation capabilities from
[
31
]
. Finally, in Appendix
G
we prove the bounds related to the projection of a normal random variable on a tangent space that play an important role in the case
T_{k}\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
.

Appendix A
Geometric Results

Proposition A.1
.

Let
M
be a manifold satisfying Assumption A, and
y\in M
. Let
\Phi_{y}:T_{y}M\mapsto M
be a local inverse of projection
\pr_{y}
on
T_{y}M
and
r_{0}=\min\left(\tau,L^{-1}_{M}\right)/8
, where
L_{M}\geq 1
.

(i)

The function
\Phi_{y}
is well defined on
B_{T_{y}M}(0,\tau/4)
. For any
z\in B_{T_{y}M}(0,\tau/4)
we have

\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Phi_{y}(z)-y\right\rVert\leq 2\left\lVert z\right\rVert.

(ii)

\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{y}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{y}(0)\right\rVert_{op}\leq L_{M}\left\lVert z\right\rVert.

(iii)

\nabla\Phi_{y}(0)\nabla^{T}\Phi_{y}(0)=\Id_{d}
, and for
\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq r_{0}
we have
\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{y}(z)\nabla^{T}\Phi_{y}(z)-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}\leq 1/2
.

Proof.

(i)

See
[
15
]
, Lemma A.1(iv).

(ii)

By the mean value theorem

\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{y}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{y}(0)\right\rVert_{op}=\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert d_{u}\left(\Phi_{y}(z)-\Phi_{y}(0)\right)\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert z\right\rVert\sup_{z^{\prime}\in[0,z],\left\lVert u\right\rVert,\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert d_{u}d_{v}\Phi_{y}(z^{\prime})\right\rVert\leq L_{M}\left\lVert z\right\rVert.

(iii)

The first part holds by the definition of
\Phi_{y}
as an inverse projection on
T_{y}M
. Next, we prove the second part.

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{y}\nabla^{T}\Phi_{y}-\Id\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{y}(z)\nabla^{T}\Phi_{y}(z)v-v\right\rVert=\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert,\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}\left\langle\nabla\Phi_{y}(z)(d_{v}\Phi_{y}(z))-v,u\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert,\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}[d_{v}\Phi_{y}(z)]^{T}d_{u}\Phi_{y}(z)-v^{T}u

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert,\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}[d_{v}\Phi_{y}(z)]^{T}d_{u}\Phi_{y}(z)-[d_{v}\Phi_{y}(0)]^{T}d_{u}\Phi_{y}(0)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert,\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}(d_{v}\Phi_{y}(z))^{T}d_{u}\left(\Phi_{y}(z)-\Phi_{y}(0)\right)+d_{v}\left(\Phi_{y}(z)-\Phi_{y}(0)\right)^{T}u

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}(\left\lVert d^{T}_{v}\Phi_{y}(z)\right\rVert+1)\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert d_{u}\left(\Phi_{y}(z)-\Phi_{y}(0)\right)\right\rVert.

Applying part (ii) we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert d^{T}_{v}\Phi_{y}(z)\right\rVert\leq\sup_{\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}(\left\lVert d^{T}_{v}\Phi_{y}(z)-d^{T}_{v}\Phi_{y}(0)\right\rVert+\left\lVert d^{T}_{v}\Phi_{y}(0)\right\rVert\leq L_{M}\left\lVert z\right\rVert+1,

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert d_{u}\left(\Phi_{y}(z)-\Phi_{y}(0)\right)\right\rVert\leq L_{M}\left\lVert z\right\rVert.

So since
\left\lVert z\right\rVert<L_{M}^{-1}/8

\left\lVert\nabla\Phi\nabla^{T}\Phi-\Id\right\rVert_{op}\leq\sup_{\left\lVert v\right\rVert=1}(\left\lVert d^{T}_{v}\Phi_{y}(z)\right\rVert+1)\sup_{\left\lVert u\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert d_{u}\left(\Phi_{y}(z)-\Phi_{y}(0)\right)\right\rVert\\
\leq L_{M}\left\lVert z\right\rVert(L_{M}\left\lVert z\right\rVert+2)\leq 3/8\leq 1/2.

∎

Proposition A.2
.

Let
M
be a manifold satisfying Assumption A. Fix
\varepsilon<r_{0}
and denote as
C_{d}
the volume of the unit
d
-dimensional ball.

(i)

Let
M_{y}(\varepsilon)=\Phi_{y}\left(B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon\right)\right)
then

C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}\leq\Vol M_{y}(\varepsilon)\leq 2^{d}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d},

where
C_{d}
is volume of a
d
-dimensional unit ball.

(ii)

Recall that for
y\in M
we defined
B_{M}(y,\varepsilon)=M\cap B(y,\varepsilon)
then

2^{-d}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}\leq\Vol B_{M}(y,\varepsilon)\leq 2^{d}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}.

Proof.

(i)

Since projection
\pr_{T_{y}M}
is contraction, and
\Phi_{y}
is the inverse map

\Vol M_{y}(\varepsilon)\geq\Vol\left(\pr_{TyM}M_{y}(\varepsilon)\right)=\Vol B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon\right)=\Vol B_{d}\left(0,1\right)\varepsilon^{d}=C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}.

On the other hand, applying Proposition
A.1

\Vol M_{y}(\varepsilon)=\int_{M_{y}(\varepsilon)}dy=\int_{B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon\right)}\left|\nabla\Phi_{y}(z)\nabla^{T}\Phi_{y}(z)\right|^{-1/2}dz\leq 2^{d}\Vol B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon\right)\leq 2^{d}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}.

(ii)

Since
\Phi_{y}(z)
is
2
-Lipschitz
\Phi_{y}\left(B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon/2\right)\right)\subset B_{M}(y,\varepsilon)

\Vol B_{M}(y,\varepsilon)\geq\Vol\Phi_{y}\left(B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon/2\right)\right)\geq 2^{-d}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}.

Finally, since
\pr_{T_{y}M}
is contraction

\Vol B_{M}(y,\varepsilon)\leq\Vol M_{y}(\varepsilon)\leq 2^{d}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}.

∎

Appendix B
Concentration of the Score Function

B.1
Correlation with Gaussian Noise

We start the section by establishing a technical proposition that will be used in the proofs.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
f:U\subset B_{d}(0,r)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{D}
be
L
-Lipschitz such that
f(0)=0
.
Let
Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Id_{D})
be a
D
-dimensional standard normal vector and define the centred Gaussian field
F(z):=\left\langle Z_{D},f(z)\right\rangle
on
U
.
Then for any
\delta>0

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in U}F(z)\leq Lr\sqrt{d}+Lr\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

Proof.

The main idea of the proof is to find another process
F^{\prime}
with a larger variance of pairwise differences and with easy-to-control supremum. Then, the first condition guarantees a higher spread in values, and, therefore, a larger expected maximum, while the second helps us to control this supremum exactly. The difference between values in two points is controlled by the Lipschitz constant allowing us to choose
F^{\prime}(z)=L\left\langle z,Z_{d}\right\rangle
– a process defined intrinsically in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Formally, by the Lipschitz property for any
z,z^{\prime}\in B(y,r)

\mathbb{E}\left|F(z)-F(z^{\prime})\right|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f(z)-f(z^{\prime})\right\rangle\right|^{2}=\left\lVert f(z)-f(z^{\prime})\right\rVert^{2}\leq L^{2}\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}.

Let
Z_{d}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{d}\right)
, consider
G(z)=L\left\langle Z_{d},z\right\rangle
an auxiliary Gaussian field on
B(y,r)
. Both
F(z)
and
G(z)
are continuous zero-mean Gaussian fields, and by construction
\mathbb{E}\left|F(z)-F(z^{\prime})\right|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left|G(z)-G(z^{\prime})\right|^{2}
, so by the Sudakov-Fernique inequality
[
4
, Theorem 2.8]

\mathbb{E}\sup_{z\in U}F(z)\leq\mathbb{E}\sup_{z\in U}G(z)=\mathbb{E}\sup_{z\in B_{d}(0,r)}L\left\langle Z_{d},z\right\rangle=Lr\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z_{d}\right\rVert\leq Lr\sqrt{d}.

Moreover,

\sup_{z\in U}\mathbb{E}\left|{F(z)}\right|^{2}=\sup_{z\in U}\left\lVert f(z)\right\rVert^{2}\leq(Lr)^{2}.

Finally, we note that
F(z)
is continuous, so
\sup\left|F(z)\right|<\infty
a.s., and by the Borell-TIS inequality
[
4
]
, Theorem 2.1,

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in U}F(z)\leq Lr\sqrt{d}+Lr\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

∎

Now we are ready to prove the main result of this section

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

The main idea is to first prove the inequality locally and then generalize it to arbitrary
y,y^{\prime}\in M
using a chaining argument by taking an
\varepsilon
-dense set on
M
.

Fix a small enough constant
\varepsilon>0
and an arbitrary point
G\in M
. By Assumption
2.2
the manifold
M
can be locally represented as a graph of a function
\Phi_{G}
. By choice of
\varepsilon
, the function
\Phi_{G}(z)
is
2
-Lipschitz by Proposition
A.1
and by the definition
\Phi_{G}(0)=G
, so applying Proposition
B.1
we conclude

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B_{T_{G}M}(G,\varepsilon)}\left\langle Z_{D},\Phi_{G}(z)-G\right\rangle\leq 2\varepsilon\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\right)\geq 1-\delta

that implies a local version of the desired result

\rP\left(\sup_{y\in M\cap B(G,\varepsilon)}\left\langle Z_{D},y-G\right\rangle\leq 2\varepsilon\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\right)\geq 1-\delta.

(B.1)

To make it global, we take a
\varepsilon
-dense set
G_{1},\ldots,G_{N}
, where by Proposition
2.1
we can guarantee
N\leq(\varepsilon/2)^{-d}\Vol M
. For any pair
G_{i},G_{j}
we note that
\left\langle Z_{D},G_{i}-G_{j}\right\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\left\lVert G_{i}-G_{j}\right\rVert^{2}\right)
and therefore

\rP\left(\left\langle Z_{D},G_{i}-G_{j}\right\rangle\leq\left\lVert G_{i}-G_{j}\right\rVert\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

(B.2)

We estimate from above with
\delta N(N+1)/2
the probability that simultaneously for any
G_{i}
event (
B.1
) holds, and for any pair
G_{i}\neq G_{j}
event (
B.2
) holds too.

Representing
\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle=\left\langle Z_{D},y-G\right\rangle+\left\langle Z_{D},G-G^{\prime}\right\rangle+\left\langle Z_{D},G^{\prime}-y^{\prime}\right\rangle
, where
G,G^{\prime}
are the nearest points in the
\varepsilon
-net to
y,y^{\prime}
respectively, and applying the bounds above we get that

\rP\left[\forall y,y^{\prime}\in M:\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\leq 4\varepsilon\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)+\left(\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+2\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{2\log\delta^{-1}}\right]\\
\geq 1-\delta N(N+1)/2,

where we additionally used the triangle inequality

\left\lVert G-G^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+\left\lVert y-G\right\rVert+\left\lVert y^{\prime}-G^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+2\varepsilon.

Next, we reorder and perform a change of variable

\rP\left(\forall y,y^{\prime}\in M:\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\leq 4\varepsilon\sqrt{d}+\left(\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+6\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{2\log N(N+1)/2+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\\
\geq 1-\delta.

Since
N(N+1)/2\leq N^{2}\leq(\varepsilon/2)^{-2d}(\Vol M)^{2}

we get

\rP\Big[\forall y,y^{\prime}\in M:\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\leq 4\varepsilon\sqrt{d}+\\
\left(\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+6\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{4d\log 2\varepsilon^{-1}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log\delta^{-1}}\Big]\geq 1-\delta.

Finally, noting that the same bound holds for
-\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle dist.}}{{=}}\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle
we obtain the bound on
\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\right|

\rP\Big[\forall y,y^{\prime}\in M:\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\right|\leq 4\varepsilon\sqrt{d}+\\
\left(\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+6\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{4d\log 2\varepsilon^{-1}+4\log\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}}\Big]\geq 1-\delta.

(B.3)

∎

B.2
Bounds on the Score Function

Proof of Proposition
4.3
.

Recall that
p\left(y|t,X_{t}\right)\propto e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p(y)
, so

\log p\left(y|t,X_{t}\right)=-\log\left(\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p(y)dy\right)-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}+\log p(y).

(B.4)

We start our proof with an analysis of term
-\left\lVert X_{t}-y\right\rVert^{2}
. By definition of
X_{t}

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert c_{t}X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}=\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}.

Since
X_{0}\in M
, by Proposition
4.1
, for
\varepsilon<r_{0}
with probability
1-\delta
for all
y\in M

\left|\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle\right|\leq 4\varepsilon\sqrt{d}+\left(\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert+6\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{4d\log 2\varepsilon^{-1}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}}.

Taking
\varepsilon=\min\left(r_{0},\frac{1}{4}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\right)
we get

\left|\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{4}{4}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d}\\
+\left(\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert+\frac{6}{4}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\right)\sqrt{4d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{-1}+4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}}.

Since
2<\sqrt{4\log 8}
we have
\frac{4}{4}\sqrt{d}\leq\frac{2}{4}\sqrt{4d\log 8}
and therefore

2\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle\right|\leq 2\sigma^{2}_{t}\sqrt{4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}}\\
+2\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\sqrt{4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}},

(B.5)

while the last term can be bounded by Young’s inequality as

2\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\sqrt{4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}+2\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}\right).

Substituting, since
\sqrt{x}\leq x
for
x\geq 1
, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
that for all
y\in M

2\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}\\
+4\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\delta^{-1}\right).

Finally, by definition of
C_{\log}
we have
4d\log 8r^{-1}_{0}+4\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log 2\leq C_{\log}\left(12d+2\right)
whence

\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}-4\sigma^{2}_{t}C_{0}=\\
-4\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log\delta^{-1}\right)+\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\\
\leq\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}-\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\\
3c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/2+4\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log\delta^{-1}\right)\\
=\frac{3}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}+4\sigma^{2}_{t}C_{0},

(B.6)

where, for brevity, we introduced

C_{0}:=\left(4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log\delta^{-1}\right).

Next, we bound the normalizing constant

\log\left(\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p(y)dy\right).

Using the inequality (
B.6
) and a local representation of the manifold as
\Phi_{X_{0}}\left(B_{T_{X_{0}}M}(X_{0},r_{0})\right)
, where
\Phi_{X_{0}}
is
2
-Lipschitz, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}p(y)dy

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-2C_{0}}\int_{\Phi_{X_{0}}\left(B_{T_{X_{0}}M}(X_{0},r_{0})\right)}e^{-3c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/4\sigma_{t}^{2}}p(y)dy

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-d}p_{\min}e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-2C_{0}}\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq r_{0}}e^{-3c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}/\sigma_{t}^{2}}dz

(B.7)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{d}}{(2d)^{d+1}}e^{-2}p_{\min}e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-2C_{0}}(r_{0}\wedge(\sigma_{t}/c_{t}))^{d}.

The last inequality follows from integration in polar coordinates, standard bounds on
\Gamma
-function
[
29
]
, Theorem 1.5 and inequality on the incomplete gamma function
\gamma(a,x)
from
[
30
]
, Theorem 4.1. More precisely, we applied that for
a,b>0
and
c:=\min(a,b)
holds

\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq a}e^{-3\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}/b^{2}}dz\geq\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq c}e^{-3\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}/c^{2}}dz=\int_{0}^{c}e^{-3r^{2}/c^{2}}r^{d-1}dr\\
=\frac{2\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}\frac{c^{d}}{6^{d/2}}\gamma\left(\frac{d}{2},3\right)\geq\frac{2\cdot\pi^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{1/2}(d/2e)^{d}}\frac{c^{d}}{6^{d/2}}e^{-\frac{3d}{2d+1}}\frac{3^{d/2}}{(d/2)}\geq c^{d}\frac{e^{d}}{d^{d+1}}\frac{e^{-2}}{2}.

Taking logarithms in (
B.7
) we conclude

-\log\left(\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}p(y)dy\right)\\
\leq\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2+2C_{0}+d\log 2d+2+\log_{+}p^{-1}_{\min}+d\log r^{-1}_{0}+d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{-1}\\
\leq\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2+2C_{0}+d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+C_{\log}\left(3d+1\right).

In a similar way, we can get an upper-bound, since
\int_{M}p(y)dy=1

\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}p(y)dy\leq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{2C_{0}}\int_{M}e^{-c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/4\sigma_{t}^{2}}p(y)dy\leq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{2C_{0}}.

So,

-\log\left(\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}p(y)dy\right)\geq-2C_{0}+\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2.

Finally, recalling the representation (
B.4
) of
\log p\left(y|t,X_{t}\right)
and that
\left|\log p(y)\right|\leq dC_{\log}

\displaystyle-16d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})-(49d+8)C_{\log}-8\log\delta^{-1}-\frac{3}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=-4C_{0}-dC_{\log}-\frac{3}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\log p\left(y|t,X_{t}\right)=-\log\left(\int_{M}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p(y)dy\right)-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}+\log p(y)

\displaystyle\leq 4C_{0}+d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+C_{\log}\left(4d+1\right)-\frac{1}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=17d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+(52d+9)C_{\log}+8\log\delta^{-1}-\frac{1}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2},

that implies

-19d\left(\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)-8\log\delta^{-1}-\frac{3}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}\\
\leq\log p(y|t,X_{t})\leq\\
19d\left(\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}-\frac{1}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}.

(B.8)

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4
.

We prove the 3 items of the Theorem.

1.

Fix
r>0
. Applying (
B.8
)

\displaystyle\int_{\left\lVert y-X_{0}\right\rVert\geq r}p(y|t,X_{t})dy

\displaystyle\leq e^{19d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}}\int_{\left\lVert y-X_{0}\right\rVert\geq r}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}}dy

\displaystyle\leq e^{19d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}}[e^{-\frac{1}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}r^{2}}\Vol M]

\displaystyle\leq e^{20d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}r^{2}}\leq\eta,

where we additionally used that
\Vol M<e^{dC_{\log}}
and
the last inequality holds if we take

r=r(t,\delta,\eta):=2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}+\log\eta^{-1}}.

2.

First we express the LHS as

\sigma_{t}s(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}=\int_{M}\frac{c_{t}y-c_{t}X_{0}-\sigma_{t}Z_{D}}{\sigma_{t}}p(y|t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\\
=\int_{M}\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\left(y-X_{0}\right)p(y|t,X_{t}).

(B.9)

Since
\diam M\leq 1
,
\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\leq 1
a.s., so splitting integral by event
\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\leq r(t,\delta,\eta)
and applying (
4.3
) we have that with probability
1-\delta

\left\lVert\sigma_{t}s(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})\left(r(t,\delta,\eta)+\eta\right).

Taking
\eta=\min\left(1,(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\right)
with probability
1-\delta

\left\lVert\sigma_{t}s(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 2\sqrt{20d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}+\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})}+1\\
\leq 4\sqrt{20d\left(2\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}}.

(B.10)

3.

Similarly, by (
B.9
), we have that a.s.
\left\lVert\sigma_{t}s(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq c_{t}/\sigma_{t}
, so applying the first inequality of (
B.10
) and
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\sigma_{t}s(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\\
\leq 8\left(20d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+8\log\delta^{-1}+\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\right)+2+(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})^{2}\delta.

Taking
\delta=\min\left(1,(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\right)

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\sigma_{t}s(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\\
\leq 8\left(20d\left(\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)+16\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+2\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\right)+3\\
\leq 8\left(20d\left(2\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4C_{\log}\right)\right)+3.

∎

B.3
Point Comparison

This section proves key statements used to build an efficient approximation of the score function.
The following lemma is a full version of Lemma
4.5
.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{N}\}\subset M
be a set of points in
M
. Denote the nearest neighbor of
X_{t}
in
\mathcal{P}
with
m_{\min}(t)=m_{\min}(X_{t}):=\arg\min_{i}\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert
, then with probability at least
1-\delta
:

(i)

m_{\min}(t)
is close to
m_{\min}(0)

\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\leq 3\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}+64(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\left(d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{-1}+4dC_{\log}+\log\delta^{-1}\right);

(B.11)

(ii)

for any
m_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\geq\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{2}\left(\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-9\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}\right)\\
-128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\left(d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{-1}+4dC_{\log}+\log\delta^{-1}\right);

(B.12)

(iii)

if
\mathcal{P}
is an
\varepsilon
-dense set, for all
m_{i}

\frac{2}{3}c^{-2}_{t}\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\right)-128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\left(d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{-1}+4dC_{\log}+\log\delta^{-1}\right)\\
\leq\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\\
9\varepsilon^{2}+2c^{-2}_{t}\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\right)+128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\left(d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{-1}+4dC_{\log}+\log\delta^{-1}\right).

(B.13)

Proof.

(i)

The result follows from inequality (
B.6
) that expresses
\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}
in terms of
\left\lVert X_{t}-y\right\rVert^{2}
; more precisely it states that for
\delta<1
with probability
1-\delta
for all
y\in M

\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}-4\sigma^{2}_{t}C_{0}\leq\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}-\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}+4\sigma^{2}_{t}C_{0},

where
C_{0}=\left(4d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log\delta^{-1}\right)\leq 4\left(d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4dC_{\log}+\log\delta^{-1}\right)
.

On the one hand by choice of
m_{\min}(t)
and
m_{\min}(0)
and the second part of (
B.6
)

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}+4C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+3c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}/2.

On the other hand, applying the first part of the same inequality (
B.6
)

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\geq\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}-4C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}/2.

Combining, we prove the first part of the statement

c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\leq 16C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+3c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}\\
\leq 3c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}+64\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+4dC_{\log}+\log\delta^{-1}\right).

(ii)

To prove the second part we first express both distances to
X_{t}
through distances to
X_{0}
using (
B.6
) and then substitute (
B.11
) to get the statement

\displaystyle\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}-\sigma^{2}_{t}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\right)-\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\sigma^{2}_{t}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{2}\left(3\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 32C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{2}\left(9\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\right).

(iii)

Since
\mathcal{P}
is a
\varepsilon
-dense set
\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert=\min_{m_{i}\in\mathcal{P}}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}
rearranging terms in (
B.12
) we get RHS

\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 9\varepsilon^{2}+2c^{-2}_{t}\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\right)+32(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}C^{\prime}_{0}.

To get LHS we need to bound
\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}
from below.
Applying (
B.6
)

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}+4C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+3c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}/2,

and

\displaystyle\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}-4C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}/2

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}-4C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-c_{t}m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}/2.

Subtracting we get

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\leq 8C_{0}\sigma^{2}_{t}+\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{2}\left(3\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert^{2}\right),

rearranging terms and noting that
\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{\min}(0)\right\rVert\geq 0

\frac{2}{3}c^{-2}_{t}\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m_{\min}(t)\right\rVert^{2}\right)-16C_{0}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\leq\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert^{2}

we get the LHS.

∎

Appendix C
Manifold Approximation

C.1
Proofs of Section 5.1 on the properties of
\Phi_{i}^{*}

This section contains the proofs of the results in Section
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

To simplify notation, we fix index
i
and drop it for the rest of the proof.
Recall that
P=\pr P^{*}((P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*})^{-1/2}
.

First, we show that this operator is well-defined, it is enough to show that
(P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*}
is symmetric and positive-definite. Since
\pr^{*}
is a projection on
\operatorname{Im}P^{*}
the image of embedding
P^{*}
we have
\pr^{*}P^{*}=P^{*}
and
(P^{*})^{T}P^{*}=\Id_{d}
, so

(P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*}=\Id_{d}+(P^{*})^{T}(\pr-\pr^{*})P^{*},

note that
(P^{*})^{T}(\pr-\pr^{*})P^{*}
is symmetric and
\left\lVert(P^{*})^{T}(\pr-\pr^{*})P^{*}\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}<1/2
giving us that
P^{*}
is well defined.

Moreover, applying
(1+x)^{-1/2}=1-x/2+O(x^{2})
we bound

\left\lVert\Id_{d}-\left((P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*}\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\Id_{d}-\left(\Id_{d}+(P^{*})^{T}(\pr-\pr^{*})P^{*}\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert(P^{*})^{T}(\pr-\pr^{*})P^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\beta-1}_{N}

We are now ready to bound
\left\lVert P^{*}_{i}-P_{i}\right\rVert
. Noting that
\pr^{*}P^{*}=P^{*}
,
\pr^{2}=\pr=\pr^{T}
,
(\pr^{*})^{2}=\pr^{*}=(\pr^{*})^{T}
, and
\left\lVert P^{*}\right\rVert_{op}=\left\lVert\pr^{*}\right\rVert_{op}=\left\lVert\pr\right\rVert_{op}=1

\displaystyle\left\lVert P^{*}-P\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\pr^{*}P^{*}-\pr P^{*}((P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*})^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(\pr-\pr^{*})P^{*}\right\rVert_{op}+\left\lVert\pr P^{*}\left(\Id_{d}-((P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*})^{-1/2}\right)\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\pr-\pr^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert P^{*}\right\rVert_{op}+\left\lVert\pr P^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\left(\Id_{d}-((P^{*})^{T}\pr P^{*})^{-1/2}\right)\right\rVert_{op}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\beta-1}_{N}.

Finally, the last statement to prove is that

P_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow T_{G_{i}}M-G_{i}
is an isometric embedding. By construction
\operatorname{Im}P_{i}\subseteq\operatorname{Im}\pr=T_{G_{i}}M-G_{i}
, and for any
v,u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\left\langle P_{i}v,P_{i}u\right\rangle=\left(\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\left(\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\right)^{-1/2}v\right)^{T}\left(\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\left(\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\right)^{-1/2}u\right)=\\
=v^{T}\left(\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\right)^{-1/2}(P^{*}_{i})^{T}\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\left(\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}\pr_{i}P^{*}_{i}\right)^{-1/2}u=v^{T}u,

where we again used that
\pr_{i}\pr_{i}=\pr_{i}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

We fix the index
i
and drop it to simplify notation.
Let
\overline{\Phi}:\operatorname{Im}P\mapsto M,\overline{\Phi}^{*}:\operatorname{Im}P^{*}\mapsto M^{*}
be defined as
\overline{\Phi}(Pz)=\Phi(z),\overline{\Phi}^{*}(P^{*}z)=\Phi^{*}(z)
. Introducing
V_{j}=d^{j}\overline{\Phi}(0),V^{*}_{j}=d^{j}\overline{\Phi}^{*}(0)
where
2\leq j<\beta-1
we represent

\displaystyle\overline{\Phi}(v)=y_{i}+v+\sum V_{j}\left(v^{\otimes k}\right)+R(v),

\displaystyle v\in\operatorname{Im}P,

\displaystyle\overline{\Phi}^{*}(v)=y_{i}+v+\sum V^{*}_{j}\left(v^{\otimes k}\right),

\displaystyle v\in\operatorname{Im}P^{*},

where
R(v)
is the residual of order
\beta
. We first control
\nabla^{k}\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\nabla^{k}\Phi^{*}(z)
and then
\nabla^{k}\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\nabla^{k}\Phi(z)
.

Substituting
P^{*}z
and
PP^{T}P^{*}z=\pr P^{*}z

\displaystyle\overline{\Phi}(PP^{T}P^{*}z)=y_{i}+PP^{T}P^{*}z+\sum V_{j}\left(\left(PP^{T}P^{*}z\right)^{\otimes k}\right)+R(PP^{T}P^{*}z),

\displaystyle\overline{\Phi}^{*}(P^{*}z)=y_{i}+P^{*}z+\sum V^{*}_{j}\left(\left(P^{*}z\right)^{\otimes k}\right).

[
15
]
, Lemma A.2 (iii) states that
\left\lVert V^{*}_{j}\circ\pr^{*}-V_{j}\circ\pr\right\rVert_{op}\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-j}
for
j\geq 2
, note that taking
V_{1}=V^{*}_{1}=\Id
this also holds for
j=1
. So for all
\left\lVert z\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}
,

\left\lVert\Phi^{*}(z)-\Phi(P^{T}P^{*}z)\right\rVert=\left\lVert\overline{\Phi}^{*}(P^{*}z)-\overline{\Phi}(PP^{T}P^{*}z)\right\rVert\\
\ \lesssim\left\lVert\pr-\pr^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert P^{*}z\right\rVert+\sum_{j}\left\lVert V^{*}_{j}\circ\pr^{*}-V_{j}\circ\pr\right\rVert\left\lVert P^{*}z\right\rVert^{k}+\left\lVert R(PP^{T}P^{*}z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta},

since
\pr^{*}P^{*}z=P^{*}z,\left\lVert z^{*}\right\rVert=\left\lVert P^{*}z\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}
and
\left\lVert PP^{T}P^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\leq 1
.

To prove the result for
k\geq 1
we note that if we take
1\leq k<\beta-1
derivatives along the directions
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\lVert v_{j}\right\rVert=1
, since
\pr^{*}P^{*}z=P^{*}z

\displaystyle d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\overline{\Phi_{i}}\circ\pr\circ P^{*}\right)(z)=\sum_{j=k}^{\beta-1}\frac{j!}{(j-k)!}V_{j}\left(\left(\pr P^{*}z\right)^{\otimes(j-k)}\otimes\left(\pr P^{*}v_{1}\right)\otimes\ldots\otimes\left(\pr P^{*}v_{k}\right)\right)+R^{k}(z),

\displaystyle d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\overline{\Phi_{i}}^{*}\circ P^{*}\right)(z)=\sum_{j=k}^{\beta-1}\frac{j!}{(j-k)!}V^{*}_{j}\left(\left(\pr^{*}P^{*}z\right)^{\otimes(j-k)}\otimes\left(\pr^{*}P^{*}v_{1}\right)\otimes\ldots\otimes\left(\pr^{*}P^{*}v_{k}\right)\right),

where
\left\lVert R^{k}(z)\right\rVert=O\left(\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}\right)
. Thus

\displaystyle\left|d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\Phi^{*}(z)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\overline{\Phi_{i}}\circ\pr\circ P^{*}\right)(z)-d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\overline{\Phi_{i}}^{*}\circ P^{*}\right)(z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}\right)+\sum_{j=k}^{\beta-1}\left\lVert V^{*}_{j}\circ\pr^{*}-V_{j}\circ\pr\right\rVert\left\lVert P^{*}z\right\rVert^{j-k}\prod\left\lVert P^{*}v_{k}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}+\sum_{j=k}^{\beta-1}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-j}\varepsilon_{N}^{j-k}\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}.

This proves that
\left\lVert\nabla^{k}\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\nabla^{k}\Phi^{*}(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}
. Therefore, to prove the statement it is enough to show that
\left\lVert\nabla^{k}\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\nabla^{k}\Phi(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}
and
\left\lVert\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\Phi(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}
.

Denote by
A=P^{T}P^{*}
, then since
P
is an isometry

\left\lVert A-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}=\left\lVert P^{T}P^{*}-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}=\left\lVert PP^{T}P^{*}-P\right\rVert_{op}=\left\lVert\pr\left(P^{*}-P\right)\right\rVert_{op}\leq\left\lVert P^{*}-P\right\rVert_{op}\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-1}.

and
\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{op}\leq\left\lVert P^{T}\right\rVert\left\lVert P^{*}\right\rVert=1
. We start with the case
k=0

\left\lVert\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\Phi(z)\right\rVert\lesssim L_{M}\left\lVert P^{T}P^{*}z-z\right\rVert\leq L_{M}\left\lVert P^{T}P^{*}-\Id_{d}\right\rVert\left\lVert z\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}.

Let
k\geq 1
. Taking derivatives along the directions
v_{1},\ldots,v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\lVert v_{j}\right\rVert=1
and then applying the chain rule and chaining argument

\displaystyle\left\lVert d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\Phi\circ A\right)(z)-d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\Phi(z)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\left(\Phi_{i}\circ A\right)(z)-d_{v_{1}}\ldots d_{v_{k}}\Phi(z)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert d^{k}\Phi(Az)\left(A{v_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes Av_{k}\right)-d^{k}\Phi(z)\left(v_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes v_{k}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert d^{k}\Phi(Az)\left({v_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes v_{k}\right)-d^{k}\Phi(z)\left(v_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes v_{k}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\lVert d^{k}\Phi(Az)\left(v_{1}\otimes\ldots v_{j-1}\otimes(Av_{j}-v_{j})\otimes Av_{j+1}\ldots\otimes Av_{k}\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{z^{\prime}\in[z,Az]}\left\lVert d^{k+1}\Phi(z^{\prime})\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert A-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert z\right\rVert+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\lVert d^{k}\Phi(Ax)\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert A\right\rVert^{k-j}_{op}\left\lVert A-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle\leq(k+\varepsilon_{N})L_{M}\left(\left\lVert A-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-1},

by the mean value theorem. So
\left\lVert\nabla^{k}\left(\Phi\circ P^{T}P^{*}\right)(z)-\nabla^{k}\Phi(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-1}\leq\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-k}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.4
.

For simplicity, we drop the index
i
and the star notation and consider the minimization problem

P,\{a_{S}\}_{2\leq\left|S\right|<\beta}\in\argmin_{P,a_{S}\leq\varepsilon_{N}^{-1}}\sum_{v_{j}\in\mathcal{V}}\left\lVert v_{j}-PP^{T}v_{j}-\sum_{S}a_{S}(P^{T}v_{j})^{S}\right\rVert^{2},

where
\argmin
is taken over all linear isometric embeddings
P:\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{D}
and all vectors
a_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
that satisfy
\left\lVert a_{S}\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon_{N}^{-1}
.

As a preliminary step, let us show that a solution always satisfies
a_{S}\perp\operatorname{Im}P
. Note that
PP^{T}
is a projection on
\operatorname{Im}P
, so
v-P^{T}Pv\perp\operatorname{Im}P
, and therefore

\left\lVert v_{j}-PP^{T}v_{j}-\sum_{S}a_{S}(P^{T}v_{j})^{S}\right\rVert^{2}\\
=\left\lVert(\Id_{D}-PP^{T})v_{j}-\sum_{S}(\Id_{D}-PP^{T})a_{S}(P^{T}v_{j})^{S}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert PP^{T}a_{S}(P^{T}v_{j})^{S}\right\rVert^{2},

so the
\argmin
should satisfy
P^{T}Pa_{S}=0
, or equivalently
a_{S}\perp\operatorname{Im}P
.

Note that
P^{T}P
is a projection on
\operatorname{Im}P
.
First, we prove that there is
P
satisfying
\operatorname{Im}P\subseteq\spn\mathcal{V}
. Assume the opposite and take
P
such that
\dim(\operatorname{Im}P\cap\spn\mathcal{V})
is the largest. Then, by the assumption
\dim(\operatorname{Im}P\cap\spn\mathcal{V})<\dim\operatorname{Im}P
, since
\dim\spn\mathcal{V}\geq d=\dim\operatorname{Im}P
there is a vector
u_{P}\in\operatorname{Im}P
such that
u_{P}\perp\spn\mathcal{V}
,
\left\lVert u_{P}\right\rVert=1
and similarly a vector
u_{\mathcal{V}}\in\spn\mathcal{V}
such that
u_{\mathcal{V}}\perp\operatorname{Im}P
,
\left\lVert u_{\mathcal{V}}\right\rVert=1
.

Let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
be column vectors in
P
, then
\left\langle e_{i},e_{j}\right\rangle=\delta_{ij}
and

P^{T}v=\left(\left\langle v,e_{1}\right\rangle,\ldots,\left\langle v,e_{d}\right\rangle\right).

We introduce vectors
f_{i}=e_{i}+\left(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P}\right)\left\langle u_{P},e_{i}\right\rangle
and note that
u_{\mathcal{V}}\perp e_{i}
for all
i
, so

\left\langle f_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle=\left\langle e_{i}+\left(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P}\right)\left\langle u_{P},e_{i}\right\rangle,e_{j}+\left(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P}\right)\left\langle u_{P},e_{j}\right\rangle\right\rangle=\delta_{ij}.

We introduce a new operator

Q=\left(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\right)^{T},

and note that by construction
u_{P}\perp\operatorname{Im}Q
and
u_{\mathcal{V}}\in\operatorname{Im}Q
, since

\sum_{i}\left\langle e_{i},u_{P}\right\rangle f_{i}=\left\langle e_{i},u_{P}\right\rangle\left(e_{i}+\left(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P}\right)\left\langle u_{P},e_{i}\right\rangle\right)=\underbrace{\left(\sum\left\langle e_{i},u_{P}\right\rangle^{2}\right)}_{=\left\lVert u_{P}\right\rVert=1}u_{\mathcal{V}},

so

\dim\left(\operatorname{Im}Q\cap\spn\mathcal{V}\right)=\dim\left(\operatorname{Im}P\cap\spn\mathcal{V}\right)+1.

(C.1)

Then for any
v_{i}\in\mathcal{V}
, since
u_{P}\perp\spn\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\left\langle e_{j},v_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle f_{j},v_{i}\right\rangle-\left\langle u_{P},e_{j}\right\rangle\left\langle u_{\mathcal{V}},v_{i}\right\rangle=\left\langle f_{j},v_{i}\right\rangle-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\left\langle f_{k},v_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle e_{k},u_{P}\right\rangle\left\langle u_{P},e_{j}\right\rangle,

so expanding the brackets we represent

\displaystyle\sum_{2\leq\left|S\right|<\beta}a_{S}\left(P^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|S\right|<\beta}a_{S}\left(\left\langle e_{1},v_{i}\right\rangle,\ldots,\left\langle e_{d},v_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{S}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|S\right|<\beta}b_{S}\left(\left\langle f_{1},v_{i}\right\rangle,\ldots,\left\langle f_{d},v_{i}\right\rangle\right)^{S}=\sum_{2\leq\left|S\right|<\beta}b_{S}\left(Q^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S},

(C.2)

for some vector coefficients
b_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
.

We are finally ready to present an estimator that has a smaller or equal error w.r.t.
l_{\mathcal{V}}
,

\Phi^{\prime}(z)=Qz+\sum_{2\leq\left|S\right|<\beta}\underbrace{\left(b_{S}-\left\langle b_{S},u_{P}\right\rangle u_{P}-\left\langle b_{S},u_{\mathcal{V}}\right\rangle u_{\mathcal{V}}\right)}_{=c_{S}}z^{S}

and the reconstruction error is equal to

\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{V}\right|}\sum_{v_{i}\in\mathcal{V}}\left\lVert v_{i}-QQ^{T}v_{i}-\sum c_{S}\left(Q^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right\rVert^{2}.

We decompose
\mathbb{R}^{D}
into two subspaces
L_{1}=\spn\{u_{\mathcal{V}},u_{P}\}
and
L_{2}=L_{1}^{\perp}
– the orthogonal complement of
L_{1}
, and denote as
\pr_{1}
,
\pr_{2}
corresponding projections. By construction for
v_{i}\in\mathcal{V}
, since
c_{S}\perp u_{P},u_{\mathcal{V}}
and
v_{i}\perp u_{P}
, and
Q^{T}Q
is a projector on
\operatorname{Im}Q\ni u_{\mathcal{V}}

\pr_{1}\left(v_{i}-QQ^{T}v_{i}-\sum c_{S}\left(Q^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right)=\pr_{1}\left(v_{i}-Q^{T}v_{i}\right)=\left\langle v_{i}-QQ^{T}v_{i},u_{\mathcal{V}}\right\rangle u_{\mathcal{V}}=0.

At the same time since by construction
c_{S}=\pr_{2}b_{S}
, applying (
C.2
)

\pr_{2}\left(v_{i}-QQ^{T}v_{i}-\sum c_{S}\left(Q^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right)=\pr_{2}\left(v_{i}-PP^{T}v_{i}-\sum a_{S}\left(P^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right),

where we additionally used that
\pr_{2}QQ^{T}v_{i}=\pr_{2}PP^{T}v_{i}
following from

QQ^{T}v_{i}=\sum\left\langle f_{j},v_{i}\right\rangle f_{j}=\sum\left\langle e_{j}+(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P})\left\langle e_{j},u_{P}\right\rangle,v_{i}\right\rangle\left(e_{j}+(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P})\left\langle e_{j},u_{P}\right\rangle\right)\\
=\sum_{j}\left\langle e_{j},v_{i}\right\rangle e_{j}+\left\langle e_{j}+(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P})\left\langle e_{j},u_{P}\right\rangle,v_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle e_{j},u_{P}\right\rangle\left(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P}\right)+\left\langle u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P},v_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle e_{j},u_{P}\right\rangle e_{j}\\
=P^{T}Pv_{i}+\left[\sum_{j}\left\langle e_{j}+(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P})\left\langle e_{i},u_{P}\right\rangle,v_{i}\right\rangle\left\langle e_{i},u_{P}\right\rangle\right]\left(u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P}\right)+\left\langle u_{\mathcal{V}}-u_{P},v_{i}\right\rangle u_{P}.

And we finally conclude that for any
v_{i}\in\mathcal{V}

\left\lVert v_{i}-PP^{T}v_{i}-\sum a_{S}\left(P^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right\rVert^{2}\\
=\left\lVert\pr_{1}\left(v_{i}-PP^{T}v_{i}-\sum a_{S}\left(P^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right)\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\pr_{2}\left(v_{i}-PP^{T}v_{i}-\sum a_{S}\left(P^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right)\right\rVert^{2}\\
\geq\left\lVert v_{i}-QQ^{T}v_{i}-\sum b_{S}\left(Q^{T}v_{i}\right)^{S}\right\rVert^{2}.

Averaging over all
v_{i}\in\mathcal{V}
and using (
C.1
) we get the contradiction, so there is a
P
satisfying
\operatorname{Im}P\subseteq\spn\mathcal{V}
.

Finally, choosing
\operatorname{Im}P\subset\mathcal{V}
for all
v\in\mathcal{V}
by construction
v-PP^{T}v\in\mathcal{V}\cap\Ker P
, so

\left\lVert v_{j}-PP^{T}v_{j}-\sum_{S}a_{S}(P^{T}v_{j})^{S}\right\rVert^{2}\geq\left\lVert v_{j}-PP^{T}v_{j}-\sum_{S}\pr_{3}a_{S}(P^{T}v_{j})^{S}\right\rVert^{2},

where
\pr_{3}
is the projection on
\spn\mathcal{V}\cap\Ker P
showing that there is a solution satisfying
a_{S}\in\spn\mathcal{V}\cap\Ker P
for all
S
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

Recall that
\varepsilon_{N}=\left(C_{d,\beta}\frac{p^{2}_{\max}}{p^{3}_{\min}}\frac{\log N}{N-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{d}}\simeq\left(\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{d}}
, where a constant
C_{d,\beta}
depends only on
d
and
\beta
. Let us take
\mathcal{F}=\{F_{1},\ldots F_{K}\}
– a minimal in size
\varepsilon_{N}
-dense set on
M
. For a point
y\in M
there is a point
F_{j}
, such that
\left\lVert y-F_{j}\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon_{N}
, so
B_{M}(y,\varepsilon_{N})\subset B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})
and therefore
\left|\mathcal{V}_{y}\right|\leq\sup_{j}\left|\mathcal{G}\cap B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})\right|
. So, it is enough to bound quantity
\sup_{j}\left|\mathcal{G}\cap B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})\right|
.

Since points
G_{i}
are i.i.d. samples from measure
\mu
with density
p_{\min}\leq p(y)\leq p_{\max}
the probability of the event
\rP\left(G_{i}\in B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})\right)=\mu\left(B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})\right)\simeq\varepsilon_{N}^{d}\simeq\frac{\log N}{N}
. So by Chernoff’s multiplicative bound, for a large enough constant
C

\rP\left(\left|\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})\right|\geq Cu\log N\right)\leq 2e^{-u^{2}\log N},

and taking
u=\sqrt{\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{\log N}}

\rP\left(\left|\mathcal{G}\cap B_{M}(F_{j},2\varepsilon_{N})\right|\geq C\sqrt{\log 2\delta^{-1}\log N}\right)\leq\delta.

Taking
\delta=N^{-\beta/d}/K
and summing over all
F_{j}
since
K\leq(\varepsilon_{N})^{-d}\Vol M\simeq\frac{N}{\log N}
we conclude that there is a constant
C_{\dim}
s.t.

\rP\left(\left|\mathcal{G}\cap B(F_{j},2\varepsilon)\right|\geq C_{\dim}\log N\right)\leq N^{-\frac{\beta}{d}}.

∎

C.2
Effect of the Manifold Approximation on the Score Function

Recall that the Kullback-Leibler divergence between two distributions
P
and
Q
on
\mathbb{R}^{D}
with densities
p
and
q
respectively is defined as

D_{KL}(P\|Q)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\log\frac{p(x)}{q(x)}p(x)dx.

We start with the following auxiliary statement.

Proposition C.1

(De Bruijn identity)
.

In the same notation as in Proposition
5.6
, if
P_{t}
and
Q_{t}
are the laws of
X_{t}
and
Y_{t}

\frac{d}{dt}D_{KL}(P_{t}\|Q_{t})=-2\mathbb{E}{\|\nabla\log p(t,X_{t})-\nabla\log q(t,X_{t})\|^{2}}.

Proof.

See
[
17
]
, Theorem 7.1.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.6
.

Since
W_{2}(P,Q)\leq\varepsilon
, there exists a coupling
\Gamma
between
P
and
Q
such that

\int|v-w|^{2}\Gamma(dv,dw)\leq\varepsilon^{2}.

Letting
P_{t}
and
Q_{t}
denote the laws of
X_{t}
and
Y_{t}
respectively, by
Proposition

C.1

\frac{d}{dt}D_{KL}(P_{t}\|Q_{t})=-2\mathbb{E}{\|\nabla\log p(t,X_{t})-\nabla\log q(t,X_{t})\|^{2}}.

Integrating over
t
we rewrite

\displaystyle\int_{t_{\min}}^{t_{\max}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\|\nabla\log p(s,x)-\nabla\log q(s,x)\|^{2}p_{s}(x)dxds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{KL}(P_{t_{\min}}\|Q_{t_{\min}})-D_{KL}(P_{t_{\max}}\|Q_{t_{\max}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}D_{KL}(P_{t_{\min}}\|Q_{t_{\min}}).

It remains to show that

\displaystyle D_{KL}(P_{t}\|Q_{t})

\displaystyle:=C_{\sigma_{t}}\int\log\frac{\int e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}P(dv)}{\int e^{-|x-c_{t}w|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}Q(dw)}\int e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}P(dv)dx

\displaystyle\leq W_{2}^{2}(P_{t},Q_{t})\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}.

Using the fact that
\Gamma
has marginals
P,Q
we rewrite

D_{KL}(P_{t}\|Q_{t})=C_{\sigma_{t}}\int\log\frac{\iint e^{-|x-c_{t}w|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)}{\iint e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)}{\iint}e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)dx.

Next, using the log-sum inequality for a fixed
x
we get

\displaystyle\log\frac{\iint e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)}{\iint e^{-|x-c_{t}w|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)}{\textstyle\iint}e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)

\displaystyle\leq\iint e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\log\frac{e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}}{e^{-|x-c_{t}w|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}}\Gamma(dv,dw)

\displaystyle\leq\iint\frac{|x-c_{t}w|^{2}-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)

\displaystyle=\iint\frac{2c_{t}\left\langle v-w,x\right\rangle+c_{t}^{2}\left(|v|^{2}-|w|^{2}\right)}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw).

Integrating over
x
we get

D_{KL}(P_{t}\|Q_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}C_{\sigma_{t}}\int\int\int\left(2c_{t}\left\langle v-w,x\right\rangle+c_{t}^{2}\left(|v|^{2}-|w|^{2}\right)\right)e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\Gamma(dv,dw)dx\\
=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\iint 2c_{t}\left\langle\left(C_{\sigma_{t}}\int(xe^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}dx\right),v-w\right\rangle\Gamma(dv,dw)\\
+\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\iint\left(C_{\sigma_{t}}\int e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}dx\right)c_{t}^{2}\left(|w|^{2}-|v|^{2}\right)\Gamma(dv,dw).

Since
C_{\sigma_{t}}e^{-|x-c_{t}v|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}
is the density of
\mathcal{N}(c_{t}v,\sigma^{2}_{t})
we get

\displaystyle D_{KL}(P_{t}\|Q_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\iint 2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle v,v-w\right\rangle\Gamma(dv,dw)+\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\iint c_{t}^{2}\left(|w|^{2}-|v|^{2}\right)\Gamma(dv,dw)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{t}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\iint|v-w|^{2}\Gamma(dv,dw)\leq W^{2}_{2}(P,Q)\frac{c_{t}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}},

where the last equality follows from the definition of
\Gamma
.
∎

Our next goal is to prove
Proposition

C.4
an analog of Theorem
4.4
for approximation
M^{*}
.
We start with a simple proposition that bounds the length of the projection
Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{D}\right)
on subspaces
\mathcal{H}_{i}=\spn\mathcal{V}_{i}
constructed in
5.1
.

Proposition C.2
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
for all
i\leq N
simultaneously

\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{(C_{\dim}+2)\log N+2\log 2\delta^{-1}},

where
C_{\dim}
is given in Proposition
5.5
.

Proof.

First note that

\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}\right)
, so using standard bounds
[
25
]
on the tails of the
\chi
-distribution

\rP\left(\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}+2\log\left(2\delta^{-1}N\right)}\right)\geq 1-\delta/N.

Since
\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}\leq C_{\dim}\log N
, we get

\rP\left(\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{C_{\dim}\log N+2\log\left(2\delta^{-1}N\right)}\right)\geq 1-\delta/N.

Combining for all
i\leq N
we get the statement.

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.7
.

We follow the same strategy as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
First, we prove that the statement holds on a submanifold
M_{i}=\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})\right)
, and then we spread it on the whole manifold.

Take
y\in M_{i}
and represent it as
y=\Phi_{i}(z)
, where
z\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})
, and define
{y^{*}=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)}
. Then
y-y^{*}=\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)
. By construction
\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{i}(z)-\nabla\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\right\rVert\leq L^{*}|z|^{\beta-1}
, so on
B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})
the function
\Phi_{i}-\Phi^{*}_{i}
is
L^{*}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-1}
Lipschitz, and
\Phi_{i}(0)=\Phi^{*}_{i}(0)=G_{i}
. Therefore by
Proposition

B.1
for all
\delta<1

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon_{N})}\left\langle Z_{D},\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\right\rangle\leq L^{*}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log{(\delta/N)^{-1}}}\right)\right)\geq 1-\delta/N.

So, the probability that such an event holds for all
M_{i}
is bounded from below as

\rP\left(\forall i:\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon_{N})}\left\langle Z_{D},\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\right\rangle\leq L^{*}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log N+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\right)\geq 1-\delta.

Applying the same bound to
-\left\langle Z_{D},\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\right\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle dist.}}{{=}}\left\langle Z_{D},\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\right\rangle
and using
\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}\leq\sqrt{2(a+b)}
we get the statement.
∎

Next, we prove an analog of Proposition
4.3
for
M^{*}
.

Proposition C.3
.

Let
t\geq\varepsilon^{2\beta}_{N}
,
X_{0}\sim\mu,Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{D}\right)
and
X_{t}=c_{t}X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}
. For any
\delta>0
a.s. in
X_{0}
and with probability at least
1-\delta
in
Z_{D}
for all
i\leq N
,
y\in M_{i}
and
y^{*}=\Phi_{i}^{*}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{i}(y)

\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}-16\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(4d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+2d\log\varepsilon^{-1}_{N}+(12d+2)C_{\log}+(L^{*})^{2}+2\log\delta^{-1}\right)\\
\leq\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}-\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\\
\frac{3}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}+8\sigma^{2}_{t}\left(4d\log_{+}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})+2d\log\varepsilon^{-1}_{N}+(12d+2)C_{\log}+(L^{*})^{2}+2\log\delta^{-1}\right).

(C.3)

Proof.

First, we represent

\displaystyle\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle+2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle X_{0}-y,y-y^{*}\right\rangle+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}_{t}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle X_{0}-y,y-y^{*}\right\rangle+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}.

We recall (
B.5
) stating that if

C^{2}_{0}:=4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log\varepsilon^{-1}_{N}+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log 2\delta^{-1}\geq 4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log 2\delta^{-1}

then with probability at least
1-\delta

2\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle\right|\leq 2\sigma^{2}_{t}C_{0}+2\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert C_{0}\leq c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/4+6\sigma^{2}_{t}C^{2}_{0}.

By Proposition
5.7
since
\sigma_{t}\geq\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}
with probability at least
1-\delta

2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle\right|\leq 2c_{t}\sigma_{t}L^{*}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2d\log\varepsilon_{N}^{-1}+2\log 2\delta^{-1}}\right)\leq 2\sigma^{2}_{t}\left((L^{*})^{2}+C^{2}_{0}\right).

Finally,

\displaystyle\left|2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle X_{0}-y,y-y^{*}\right\rangle+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2c^{2}_{t}L^{*}\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert+c^{2}_{t}(L^{*})^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}_{N}

\displaystyle\leq 2c^{2}_{t}L^{*}\sigma_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert+(L^{*})^{2}c^{2}_{t}\sigma^{2}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/4+5(L^{*})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}.

Summing up, we get that with probability at least
1-\delta

\left|2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},X_{0}-y\right\rangle+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle+2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle X_{0}-y,y-y^{*}\right\rangle+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right|\\
\leq c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/2+8\sigma^{2}_{t}C^{2}_{0}+7(L^{*})^{2}\sigma^{2}_{t},

and the desired inequality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}-8\sigma^{2}_{t}(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}-\sigma_{t}^{2}\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+8\sigma^{2}_{t}(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2}).

∎

Finally, we are ready to present the main result of this section. Consider a measure
\mu
supported on
M
. Let
\mu^{*}
be the measure on
M^{*}
constructed in Section
5
corresponding to
\varepsilon_{N}>0
.
As in Section
4
we consider a conditional measure
\mu^{*}(y|t,x)\propto e^{-\left\lVert x-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\mu^{*}(dy^{*})
.

Proposition C.4
.

Let a measure
\mu
satisfy Assumption
2.2
, and measure
\mu^{*}
be as above. Fix
t\geq\varepsilon_{N}^{2\beta}
, non-negative
\delta,\eta<1
, and the radius

r(t,\delta,\eta)=20(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d(\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+\log\varepsilon^{-1}_{N}+3C_{\log})+(L^{*})^{2}+\log 2\delta^{-1}+\log\eta^{-1}}

then with probability at least
1-\delta

in
Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{D}\right)
and a.s. in
X_{0}\sim\mu
for a point
X_{t}=c_{t}X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}
the following bound holds

\int_{y^{*}\in M:\left\lVert y^{*}-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq r(t,\delta,\eta)}p(y^{*}|t,X_{t})dy^{*}\geq 1-\eta.

Proof.

As in
Proposition

C.3
we introduce
C^{2}_{0}=4d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4d\log\varepsilon^{-1}_{N}+(12d+2)C_{\log}+2\log 2\delta^{-1}
.

Since
\mu^{*}(y^{*}|t,X_{t})\propto e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu(dy^{*})
, first, we control the normalization constant. Applying the change of variable formula,
Proposition

C.3
, and then (
B.7
) with probability at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\int_{M^{*}}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu(dy^{*})=\sum_{i}\int_{M^{*}_{i}}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\int_{M_{i}}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}\circ\Phi_{i}^{-1}(y)\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu_{i}(dy)

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-4(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})}\sum_{i}\int_{M_{i}}e^{-3\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu_{i}(dy)

\displaystyle=e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-4(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})}\int_{M}e^{-3c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu(dy)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{d}}{(2d)^{d+1}}e^{-2}p_{\min}e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-4(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})}(r_{0}\wedge(\sigma_{t}/c_{t}))^{d}

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{-C_{\log}(3d+1)-4(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})-d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})}.

By the same change of variables formula and
Proposition

C.3
with probability at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=\int_{y^{*}\in M^{*}:\left\lVert X_{0}-y^{*}\right\rVert\geq r(t,\delta,\eta)}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu^{*}(dy^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\int_{y^{*}\in M^{*}_{i}:\left\lVert X_{0}-y^{*}\right\rVert\geq r(t,\delta,\eta)}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\int_{y\in M_{i}:\left\lVert X_{0}-\Phi^{*}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{i}(y)\right\rVert\geq r(t,\delta,\eta)}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{i}(y)\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu_{i}(dy)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}\int_{y\in M_{i}:\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\geq r(t,\delta,\eta)-L^{*}\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}}e^{-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{i}(y)\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu_{i}(dy)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{4(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})}\int_{y:\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\geq r(t,\delta,\eta)-L^{*}\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}}e^{-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert^{2}/4\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mu(dy)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}/2}e^{4(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})-c^{2}_{t}\left(r(t,\delta,\eta)-L^{*}\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\right)^{2}/4\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

Thus with probability at least
1-\delta

\mu^{*}\left(B_{M^{*}}(X_{0},r(t,\delta,\eta)|t,X_{t}\right)=1-\frac{A}{B}\\
\geq 1-e^{C_{\log}(3d+1)+8(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})+d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})-c^{2}_{t}\left(r(t,\delta,\eta)-L^{*}\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\right)^{2}/4\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

So, for

r(t,\delta,\eta)\geq L^{*}\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}+2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log\eta^{-1}+C_{\log}(3d+1)+d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+8(C^{2}_{0}+(L^{*})^{2})},

with probability at least
1-\delta

\mu^{*}\left(B_{M^{*}}(X_{0},r(t,\delta,\eta)|t,X_{t}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

The statement follows if we let
t\geq\varepsilon^{2\beta}_{N}
, note that
(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\geq\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}
, and substitute the definition of
C_{0}
.
∎

Appendix D
Score Approximation by Neural Networks

In this section, we prove Theorem
3.3
for
d\geq 3
on the score approximation by a neural network.

Recall that
\underline{T}\geq\left(\log n\right)^{\gamma}n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}
and
\overline{T}\lesssim\log n
.
Throughout the appendix, we harmlessly enlarge the polylogarithmic exponent
by replacing
\gamma
with
4\gamma
when convenient.

We divide the interval
[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
into segments
\underline{T}=T_{1}<T_{2}<\ldots<T_{K}=\overline{T}
, where
T_{k+1}/T_{k}=2
and build estimator
\hat{s}(t,x)
as a union of separate estimators
\hat{s}_{k}(t,x)
, where
k
-th estimator approximates
s(t,x)
on
[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
. We find each
\hat{s}_{k}(t,x)
as a solution of
\hat{s}_{k}(t,x)=\argmin_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\phi)
, where the class
\mathcal{S}_{k}
was defined in (
3.5
). The first step is to construct
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
approximating the score; and in
Section

D.1
we prove that for
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\leq\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}\left(\log n\right)^{4\gamma\beta}n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}},

while in
Section

D.2
we prove that for
T_{k}<n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\leq\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}\left(\log n\right)^{6\gamma\beta}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha+d}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}

In
Section

D.4
we first bound the covering number of classes
\mathcal{S}_{k}
, then we bound the variance of the empirical risk estimator via the covering number of
\mathcal{S}_{k}
, thus obtaining Theorem
3.3
.
Finally, in
section

D.5
, we provide correction of
[
31
]
, Theorem C.4 that we use in
Section

D.4
.

In both cases (
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
and

T_{k}<n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
), we first fix
\left(\log n\right)^{2\gamma d}n^{\frac{d}{2\alpha+d}}\geq N_{k}\geq n^{\frac{d-2}{2\alpha+d}}
, and by suppressing
k
, write

N=N_{k}
and
\varepsilon_{N}:=\left(C_{d,\beta}\frac{p^{2}_{\max}}{p^{3}_{\min}}\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{1/d}.
Throughout the proof, we assume that
n
is large enough, so
\varepsilon_{N}<r_{0}
.

The construction of
\phi
starts from a set

\mathcal{G}:=\{G_{1},\ldots,G_{N}\}\subset\mathcal{Y}=\{y_{1},\cdots,y_{n}\}
,
for instance
\mathcal{G}=\{y_{1},\cdots,y_{N}\}
so that

\mathcal{G}\sim\mu^{\otimes N}
.
Following
Section
5.1
, for each
G_{i}
we consider

\mathcal{V}_{i}:=\{G_{j}-G_{i}\big|\left\lVert G_{j}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon_{N}\}
and the polynomial functions

\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)=G_{i}+P^{*}_{i}z+\sum a^{*}_{i,S}z^{S},

where
P^{*}_{i},a^{*}_{i,S}
solution of (
5.1
) satisfying
\operatorname{Im}P^{*}_{i}\subset\spn\mathcal{V}_{i},a^{*}_{i,S}\in\spn\mathcal{V}_{i}
. The functions
\Phi_{i}^{*}
are polynomial approximations of the local maps
\Phi_{i}
defined as the local inverse to projections on
T_{G_{i}}M
over the balls
B_{d}(0,r_{0})
.

We introduce an event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}
, depending only on the training sample
\mathcal{Y}
, under which the following hold:

\mathcal{G}\text{ is }\varepsilon_{N}/2\text{-dense in }M,\qquad\sup_{i,z\in B_{d}(0,8\varepsilon_{N})}\left\lVert\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta},

\max_{i\leq N}|\mathcal{V}_{i}|\leq C_{\dim}\log n,\qquad\sup_{i,z\in B_{d}(0,8\varepsilon_{N}),\,m<\beta}\left\lVert\nabla^{m}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\right\rVert\leq 2L_{M},

and

\sup_{i,z\in B_{d}(0,8\varepsilon_{N})}\left\lVert\nabla\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\nabla^{T}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)-\Id_{d}\right\rVert\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Note that the last equation guarantees that
\Phi^{*}
is Lipschitz on
B_{d}(0,8\varepsilon_{N})
.

By Proposition
5.1
,
Lemma
5.3
,
Proposition
5.5
, and

Proposition

A.1
, this event has probability at least
1-O(N^{-\beta/d})\geq 1-O(n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}})
.

Throughout the appendix, high-probability statements are proved
conditionally on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}
, with probability then taken only
over the fresh pair
(X_{0},Z_{D})
.

Also, recall that

\displaystyle\rho(u)=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }0\leq u\leq 1/2,\\
2-2u&\text{if }u\in[1/2,1],\\
0&\text{ if }u\geq 1\end{cases},

(D.1)

and the localization functions are defined as

\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})=\rho\left(\frac{\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{\min}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\right)}{2C(T_{k},n)}\right)

(D.2)

where

\begin{split}C(T_{k},n)&=\sigma_{T_{k}}^{2}\log n\log\log n\quad\text{if }T_{k}\geq n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}\\
C(T_{k},n)&=6\varepsilon_{N}^{2}\quad\text{if }T_{k}<n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}\end{split}

(D.3)

and where

i_{\min}(x)\in\argmin_{i\leq N}\|x-c_{t}G_{i}\|,\qquad G_{\min}(x):=G_{i_{\min}(x)}.

Thus
\rho_{i}
is equal to
0
when
G_{i}
is far from
x/c_{t}
compared to
the closest center
G_{\min}(x)
. Finally recall that
s(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}e(t,x)-x/\sigma_{t}^{2}
where

e(t,x)=\frac{\int_{M}ye^{-\frac{\left\lVert x-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}}\mu(dy)}{\int_{M}e^{-\frac{\left\lVert x-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}}\mu(dy)}.

We first consider the (simpler) case
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
.

D.1
Case
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}

Choose

N=N_{k}:=\left\lceil\max\Big((\log n)^{2\gamma d}\,(\sigma_{T_{k}}/c_{T_{k}})^{-d},\ \ n\cdot n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}\Big)\right\rceil

By construction (for
n
large) we have
N\leq\ 4^{d}(\log n)^{2\gamma d}\,n^{\frac{d}{2\alpha+d}}
and

(\log n)^{1/d}\,n^{-\frac{d-2}{d}\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}\ \geq\ \varepsilon_{N}\geq\ (\log n)^{-2\gamma}\,n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}},\qquad(\log n)^{1/d-2\gamma}(\sigma_{T_{k}}/c_{T_{k}})\geq\varepsilon_{N}

(D.4)

By Assumption
3
we have
\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\lesssim(\log n)^{\beta/d}n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}
. Let
M_{i}:=\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})\right)
and define
M^{*}_{i}=\Phi_{i}^{*}\left(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})\right)

so that the surface
M^{*}_{i}
is a polynomial approximation of
M_{i}
and define
M^{*}=\cup_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}^{*}
. Since
\diam M\leq 1
by properties of
\Phi_{i}^{*}
,
\diam M^{*}\leq 2
.

We build the partition of unity on
M
corresponding to the set
\mathcal{G}_{i}
as

\chi_{i}(y)=\frac{\rho(\left\lVert y-G_{i}\right\rVert/\varepsilon_{N})}{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\rho(\left\lVert y-G_{j}\right\rVert/\varepsilon_{N})},

where
\rho
is defined in (
D.1
).
Since
\mathcal{G}
is
\varepsilon_{N}/2
dense on
M
,
\sum\chi_{i}(y)=1
for all
y\in M
. We consider the measure
\mu_{i}
with density
\chi_{i}(y)p(y)
supported on
M\cap B_{D}(G_{i},\varepsilon_{N})\subset M_{i}
and denote by
\mu^{*}_{i}
its pushforward on
M^{*}_{i}
by
\Phi^{*}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{i}
. By construction
\mu^{*}:=\sum\mu^{*}_{i}
is a probability measure satisfying
W_{2}(\mu,\mu^{*})\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}
Therefore, by Proposition
5.6
the score function
s^{*}
corresponding to measure
\mu^{*}
satisfies

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\|s(t,X_{t})-s^{*}(t,X_{t})\|^{2}dt\lesssim\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}\varepsilon_{N}^{2\beta}\leq\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{\frac{2\beta}{d}}.

(D.5)

and since
\sigma^{2}_{t}\leq 2\sigma_{T_{k}}
for
t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}]

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\sigma^{2}_{t}\mathbb{E}\|s(t,X_{t})-s^{*}(t,X_{t})\|^{2}dt\lesssim n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{\frac{2\beta}{d}}.

(D.6)

This achieves our first intermediate goal of replacing the original manifold
M
with its approximation
M^{*}
. We start by studying
s^{*}
more closely.

As we discussed in Section
3
introducing
\mathcal{A}_{i}=G_{i}+\spn\mathcal{V}_{i}
,
\mathcal{H}_{i}=\spn\mathcal{V}_{i}
and
x_{i,t}=\pr_{c_{t}\mathcal{A}_{i}}x
we can represent the score function
s^{*}(t,x)
as

s^{*}(t,x)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{*}(i|t,x)s^{*}_{i}(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{*}(i|t,x)e^{*}_{i}(t,x)-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}

(D.7)

where
\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}e^{*}_{i}(t,x):=s^{*}_{i}(t,x)-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}
and
s^{*}_{i}(t,x)
is the score function corresponding to the measure
\mu^{*}_{i}
, and

\displaystyle p^{*}(i|t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{M^{*}_{i}}e^{-{\left\lVert x-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}/{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})}{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{M^{*}_{j}}e^{-{\left\lVert x-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}/{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}}\mu^{*}_{j}(dy^{*})},

\displaystyle s^{*}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\frac{\int_{M^{*}_{i}}(c_{t}y-x)e^{-\left\lVert x-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})}{\int_{M^{*}_{i}}e^{-\left\lVert x-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})}=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}e_{i}^{*}(t,x)-\frac{x}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

As a next step, we show that terms corresponding to
\rho_{i}(t,x)<1
, where
\rho_{i}
is defined by
(
D.2
)
, are negligible in (
D.7
). So we can approximate
s^{*}
by the truncated version denoted
s^{*}_{tr}
defined by:

s^{*}_{tr}(t,x):=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\frac{\sum\rho_{i}(t,x)p^{*}(i|t,x)e^{*}_{i}(t,x)}{\sum\rho_{i}(t,x)p^{*}(i|t,x)}-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}},

(D.8)

We introduce

I_{1}(t):=\left\{i\leq N:\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq\frac{1}{3}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}\right\},

and

I_{2}(t):=\left\{i\leq N:\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 3(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}\right\}.

and in Proposition
D.1
show that with probability at least
1-n^{-d}

I_{1}(t)\subseteq\{i:\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})=1\},\qquad\{i:\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0\}\subseteq I_{2}(t).

Then by Proposition
C.4
there is an absolute constant
C>0
such that

r_{t}\leq C(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+dC_{\log}+d\log n}

with probability at least
1-n^{-d}
satisfy

\int_{B_{M^{*}}(X_{0},r_{t})}p(y^{*}|t,X_{t})dy^{*}\geq 1-n^{-d}.

Since
\diam M^{*}_{i}\leq 4\varepsilon_{N}
by representing representing
M^{*}=\bigcup M^{*}_{i}
we get

B_{M^{*}}(X_{0},r_{t})\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}\left(B(X_{0},r_{t})\cap M^{*}_{i}\right)\subset\bigcup_{\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq r_{t}+4\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}M^{*}_{i}

for sufficiently large
n
we have

r_{t}+4\varepsilon_{N}\leq\frac{1}{3}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n},

so

\sum_{i\notin I_{1}(t)}p^{*}(i\mid t,X_{t})\leq n^{-d}\leq n^{-2}.

Therefore, since
\|e^{*}_{i}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\|\leq 2c_{t}/\sigma_{t}^{2}
a.s., with probability
1-n^{-2}

\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert e^{*}_{tr}\left(t,X_{t}\right)-e^{*}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\right\rVert\leq\frac{2c_{t}n^{-2}}{(1-n^{-2})\sigma^{2}_{t}}.

At the same time, a.s.

\left\lVert e^{*}_{tr}\left(t,X_{t}\right)-e^{*}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert e^{*}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\right\rVert+\left\lVert e^{*}_{tr}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\right\rVert\leq 3,

so

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s^{*}_{tr}\left(t,X_{t}\right)-s^{*}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{\sigma^{4}_{t}}\left(16n^{-4}+18n^{-2}\right)

and since
T_{k}\leq\overline{T}=O(\log n)
and
d\geq 3

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\sigma_{t}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s^{*}_{tr}\left(t,X_{t}\right)-s^{*}\left(t,X_{t}\right)\right\rVert^{2}dt\lesssim n^{-2}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\frac{1}{t}dt\lesssim n^{-2}.

(D.9)

D.1.1
Polynomial Approximation of
s^{*}_{tr}

In this section, we introduce the main regularity bounds that allow us to construct an efficient neural network approximation of
e^{*}_{tr}
. We show that for some functions
h_{i},f_{i},g_{i}
we can represent it as

e^{*}_{tr}(t,x)=\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)f_{i}(t,x)}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)g_{i}(t,x)},

(D.10)

where
\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)
are easy-to-calculate weight functions,
f_{i}(t,x),g_{i}(t,x)
are functions that can be efficiently approximated by neural networks. This decomposition corresponds to (
3.4
) presented in Section
3.1
. Recall that

x_{i,t}=\pr_{c_{t}\mathcal{A}_{i}}x
and since
M^{*}_{i}\subset\mathcal{A}_{i}
we may represent
