\left\lVert x-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert x-x_{i,t}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}
so that

p^{*}(i|t,x)=e^{-\frac{\left\lVert x-x_{i,t}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}\int_{M^{*}_{i}}e^{\frac{\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})

Using a similar argument, we write

e^{*}_{i}(t,x)=\frac{\int_{M^{*}_{i}}y^{*}e^{(\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2})/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})}{\int_{M^{*}_{i}}e^{(\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2})/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})}.

We can thus write
e^{*}_{i}=f_{i}/g_{i}
with

\displaystyle g_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{M^{*}_{i}}\exp\left(\frac{\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*}),

\displaystyle f_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{M^{*}_{i}}y^{*}\exp\left(\frac{\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*}),

and take

h_{i}(t,x):=\exp\left(\frac{\left\lVert x-c_{t}G_{\min}(x)\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)

to get representation (
D.10
).

We establish regularity bounds to show that
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
can be efficiently approximated by neural networks. Noting that
\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}>>\sqrt{(d+C_{\dim}+5)\log n+4dC_{\log}+(L^{*})^{2}}
, we have:

•

By Proposition
D.1
, with probability at least
1-n^{-d}
every active index

\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
belongs to
I_{2}
, i.e.

\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 3\frac{\sigma_{t}}{c_{t}}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

(D.11)

•

Applying Proposition
5.7
with
\delta=n^{-2}
, since
\varepsilon_{N}>n^{-1}
, we have that with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
y_{i}\in M_{i},y^{*}_{i}=\Phi^{*}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{i}(y_{i})

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y_{i}-y^{*}_{i}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y_{i}-y_{i}^{*}\right\rangle\right|\leq L^{*}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\sqrt{(2d+5)\log n}.

•

Applying Proposition
4.1
with
\delta=n^{-2}
and
\varepsilon=n^{-2}
for all
y,y^{\prime}\in M

\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{\prime}\right\rangle\right|\leq 4n^{-2}\sqrt{d}+\left(\left\lVert y-y^{\prime}\right\rVert+6n^{-2}\right)\sqrt{(4d+2)\log 2n^{2}+4dC_{\log}}.

•

As a corollary of the two previous statements, since
\beta\geq 3
, for all
{y\in M,y^{*}\in M^{*}}
s.t.
\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert\lesssim 4\varepsilon_{N}
we have

\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle\right|\leq 5\varepsilon_{N}\sqrt{(8d+4)\log n+(4d+2)\log 2+4dC_{\log}}\leq\varepsilon_{N}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

(D.12)

We first bound the term in the exponent
\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}
where
X_{i,t}=\pr_{c_{t}\mathcal{A}_{i}}X_{t}
and
y^{*}\in M^{*}_{i}
.

\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\\
=-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y^{*}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}+2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle y^{*}-G_{i},X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rangle+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle y^{*}-G_{i},Z_{D}\right\rangle.

Combining (
D.11
) and (
D.12
), since for all
y^{*}\in M^{*}_{i}

\left\lVert y^{*}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 2\varepsilon_{N}<(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})(\log n)^{-\gamma}
,
with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}
and
y^{*}\in M^{*}_{i}
we have

\left|\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right|\\
\leq 4c^{2}_{t}\varepsilon^{2}_{N}+2c^{2}_{t}\cdot 2\varepsilon_{N}\cdot 3(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\cdot\varepsilon_{N}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}\\
\leq\sigma^{2}_{t}(\log n)^{-2\gamma}+7\sigma^{2}_{t}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}(\log n)^{-\gamma}

Therefore, assuming that
\gamma>2
and that
n
large enough, by using
\left|1-e^{x}\right|\leq 2\left|x\right|
for
x\leq 1
we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right|}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}(\log n)^{-\gamma+2},

\displaystyle\left|e^{-({\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}})/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq(\log n)^{-\gamma+2}.

(D.13)

Now we can bound
g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})
from above and below.
Proposition

A.2
states that
\Vol M_{i}\leq C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}_{N}
with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}
. We bound
g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})
from above by

\displaystyle g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\int_{M^{*}_{{i}}}e^{{\frac{\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{M^{*}_{i}}\left(1+(\log n)^{-\gamma+2}\right)\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})

\displaystyle=\left(1+(\log n)^{-\gamma+2}\right)\int_{M_{i}}\chi_{i}(y)p(dy)\leq 2^{d+1}p_{\max}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}_{N}.

At the same time, with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}
we bound from below
g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})
by

\displaystyle g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\int_{M^{*}_{i}}e^{{\frac{\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{M^{*}_{i}}\left(1-(\log n)^{-\gamma+2}\right)\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})=\left(1-(\log n)^{-\gamma+2}\right)\int_{M_{i}}\chi_{i}(y)p(dy).

By Proposition
5.5
,
\left|\mathcal{Y}\cap B(G_{i},4\varepsilon_{N})\right|\leq C_{\dim}\log N\leq C_{\dim}\log n
, so for
y\in B_{M}(G_{i},\varepsilon_{N}/2)

\chi_{i}(y)=\frac{\rho(\left\lVert y-G_{i}\right\rVert/\varepsilon_{N})}{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\rho(\left\lVert y-G_{j}\right\rVert/\varepsilon_{N})}\geq\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{Y}\cap B(y,\varepsilon_{N})\right|}\geq\frac{1}{C_{\dim}\log n},

substituting and applying
Proposition

A.2

g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})\geq\left(1-(\log n)^{-\gamma+2}\right)\int_{M_{i}}\chi_{i}(y)p(dy)\\
\geq\frac{p_{\min}}{2C_{\dim}\log n}\text{Vol}(B_{M}(G_{i},\varepsilon_{N}/2))\geq\varepsilon^{d}_{N}4^{-d}\frac{p_{\min}}{2C_{\dim}\log n}.

Since
\diam M^{*}\leq 2
, we bound
\left\lVert f_{i}\right\rVert
by

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{i}(y,X_{i,t})\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\int_{M^{*}_{i}}y^{*}e^{{\frac{\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}}\mu^{*}_{i}(dy^{*})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})\leq 2^{d+2}p_{\max}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}_{N}.

Since
(\log n)^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d}\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}\geq\varepsilon_{N}\geq(\log n)^{-\gamma+1}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}
,
for
n
large enough, with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\displaystyle g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon^{d}_{N}4^{-d}\frac{p_{\min}}{2C_{\dim}\log n}\gtrsim\frac{\varepsilon^{d}_{N}}{\log n}\geq(\log n)^{-\gamma d+d-1}n^{-\frac{d}{2\alpha+d}}

(D.14)

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{i}(t,X_{i,t})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d+2}p_{\max}C_{d}\varepsilon^{d}_{N}\lesssim\varepsilon^{d}_{N}<1.

(D.15)

Note that the constants depend only on
C_{dim},C_{\log}
and
d
.
We show below that if
\hat{g}_{i},\hat{f}_{i},\hat{e}_{i}
are such that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\displaystyle\left|\hat{g}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})-g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})\right|\leq n^{-2}(\log n)^{-\gamma d},\,\,\left\lVert\hat{e}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})-e_{i}(t,X_{i,t})\right\rVert\leq n^{-1},\,\,\left\lVert\hat{e}_{i}\right\rVert\leq 1,

(D.16)

where
\hat{e}_{i}=\hat{f}_{i}/\hat{g}_{i}
. Note that
\hat{g}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})\geq g_{i}(t,X_{i,t})/2
and
\left\lVert e_{i}\right\rVert\leq 1
. Then the approximation

\hat{e}^{*}(t,x):=\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)\hat{g}_{i}(t,x)\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)\hat{g}_{i}(t,x)}

verifies with probability greater than
1-n^{-2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert e^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})-\hat{e}^{*}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\quad=\left\lVert\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)\hat{g}_{i}(t,x)\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)\hat{g}_{i}(t,x)}-\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)g_{i}(t,x)e_{i}(t,x)}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,x)h_{i}(t,x)g_{i}(t,x)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\lVert\frac{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\left(\hat{g}_{i}\hat{e}_{i}-g_{i}e_{i}\right)}{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\hat{g_{i}}}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\frac{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}g_{i}e_{i}}{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}g_{i}}\left(\frac{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}g_{i}}{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\hat{g}_{i}}-1\right)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\hat{g}_{i}\left\lVert\hat{e}_{i}-e_{i}\right\rVert}{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\hat{g_{i}}}+\frac{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\left\lVert e_{i}\right\rVert\left|\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i}\right|}{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\hat{g_{i}}}+\left\lVert e^{*}_{tr}\right\rVert\frac{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\left|g_{i}-\hat{g}_{i}\right|}{\sum\rho_{i}h_{i}\hat{g}_{i}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{i\in I_{2}}\left\lVert\hat{e}_{i}-e_{i}\right\rVert+2\frac{\sup_{i\in I_{2}}\left|\hat{g}_{i}-g_{i}\right|}{\inf_{i\in I_{2}}\hat{g_{i}}}\leq n^{-1}+2\frac{n^{-2}}{n}\leq 3n^{-1}

Finally if we define
\hat{s}^{*}(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\hat{e}^{*}(t,x)-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}
and use that
\left\lVert e^{*}\right\rVert,\left\lVert\hat{e}^{*}\right\rVert\leq 1
a.s. we get

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{s}^{*}(t,X_{t})-s^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\sigma^{4}_{t}}\left(n^{-2}+n^{-2}\right)\leq\frac{8}{\sigma^{4}_{t}}n^{-2}.

After integration, this results in

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{s}^{*}(t,X_{t})-s^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\lesssim\sigma_{T_{k}}^{-2}n^{-2}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}dt\lesssim\sigma_{T_{k}}^{-2}n^{-2}\log n

(D.17)

D.1.2
Neural Networks Approximation

The goal of this section is to construct neural network functions
\hat{e}_{i}
and
\hat{g}_{i}
that satisfy (
D.16
) for all
i\in I_{2}
with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
. Since
\hat{e_{i}}
is an approximation of
e_{i}=f_{i}/g_{i}
, instead of directly constructing
\hat{e}_{i}
we focus on approximation of
f_{i}
with
\hat{f}_{i}
, by (
D.14
) it is enough to guarantee
\left\lVert\hat{f}_{i}-f_{i}\right\rVert\leq n^{-2}(\log n)^{-\gamma d}
.

We first construct polynomial functions in
X_{i,t}
and then we use
[
31
]
to approximate the polynomial functions by neural networks.
Since
M^{*}_{i}=\Phi^{*}_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon_{N}\right)\right)
, using the change of variable
y^{*}=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} g_{i}(t,x)&:=\int_{B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon_{N}\right)}e^{\frac{\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}p_{i}(z)dz,\\
f_{i}(t,x)&:=\int_{B_{d}\left(0,\varepsilon_{N}\right)}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)e^{\frac{\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}p_{i}(z)dz.\end{aligned}

Recall that
\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)=G_{i}+P^{*}_{i}z+R_{i}(z)
, where
R_{i}(z)=O(\left\lVert z\right\rVert)^{2}
and
R_{i}(z)\perp P^{*}_{i}z
, so

\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert x_{i,t}-c_{t}\left(G_{i}+P^{*}_{i}z+R_{i}(z)\right)\right\rVert^{2}\\
=2c_{t}\left\langle x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i},P^{*}_{i}z\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert P^{*}_{i}z\right\rVert^{2}-2c_{t}\left\langle x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i},R_{i}(z)\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}\\
=\underbrace{2c_{t}\left\langle(P^{*}_{i})^{T}(x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}),z\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}}_{=:F^{(1)}_{i}(x_{i,t},z)}-\underbrace{2c_{t}\left\langle x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i},R_{i}(z)\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}}_{=:F^{(2)}_{i}(x_{i,t},z)}=F^{(1)}_{i}(x_{i,t},z)+F^{(2)}_{i}(x_{i,t},z).

where to get the last line we used that
P^{*}_{i}
is an isometric embedding. We bound each of terms
F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)
and
F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)
separately

\left|F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|=\left|2c_{t}\left\langle(P^{*}_{i})^{T}(X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}),z\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}\right|\\
\leq 2c_{t}\left|\left\langle\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(c_{t}X_{0}-c_{t}G_{i}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}),P^{*}_{i}z\right\rangle\right|+c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}\\
\leq 2c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\left\lVert z\right\rVert+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\lVert(P^{*}_{i})^{T}Z_{D}\right\rVert\left\lVert z\right\rVert+c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}

Since
(P^{*}_{i})^{T}Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{d}\right)
applying Laurent–Massart inequality
[
25
]
we have

\rP\left(\forall i:\left\lVert(P^{*}_{i})^{T}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{d+2\log\delta^{-1}+2\log 2N}\right)\\
\geq 1-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rP\left(\left\lVert(P^{*}_{i})^{T}Z_{D}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d+2\log 2\delta^{-1}N}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

So substituting
\delta=n^{-2}
, combined with (
D.11
), and using that
\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon_{N}
we get that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

\displaystyle\left|F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma_{t}c_{t}\cdot\tilde{C}(n)\varepsilon_{N}+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{d+2\log\delta^{-1}+2\log 2N}\left\lVert z\right\rVert+c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sigma_{t}c_{t}\cdot\tilde{C}(n)\varepsilon_{N},

and substituting
2\varepsilon_{N}<(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})(\log n)^{-\gamma}
,
\tilde{C}(n)=\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}=o(\log n)
for
n
large enough with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

\left|F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|\leq\sigma^{2}_{t}(\log n)^{-\gamma+1}.

Similarly, we bound
F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)

\left|F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|=\left|2c_{t}\left\langle X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i},R_{i}(z)\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}\right|\\
\leq 2c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(X_{0}-G_{i})\right\rVert\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert+\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert+c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}

By construction
\left\lVert R_{i}(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}_{N}
, so combining with (
C.2
) and (
D.11
) with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
for all
i
s.t.
\rho_{i}(X_{t})>0

\left|F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|\lesssim c_{t}\sigma_{t}\tilde{C}(n)\varepsilon_{N}^{2}+\sigma_{t}c_{t}\varepsilon^{2}_{N}\sqrt{\log n}+c^{2}_{t}\varepsilon_{N}^{4}

Using that
\varepsilon_{N}<\min\{(\log n)^{1/d}\,n^{-\frac{d-2}{d}\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}},(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})(\log n)^{-\gamma}\}
,
(d-2)/d\geq 1/3
and
\tilde{C}(n)=o(\log n)
we get for
n
large enough

\displaystyle\left|F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|=o(\sigma^{2}_{t}n^{-\frac{1}{6}\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}})

Summarizing

\frac{\left|F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\leq(\log n)^{-\gamma+1},\text{ and }\quad\frac{\left|F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)\right|}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\leq n^{-\frac{1}{6}\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}

Therefore the Taylor approximation of
e^{u}
near 0 leads to

\exp\left((F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t})+F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t}))/2\sigma_{t}^{2}\right)=\\
\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\left\lceil\frac{5\log n}{2(\gamma-1)\log(\log n)}\right\rceil}\frac{\left(F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t})\right)^{k}}{k!2^{k}\sigma^{2k}_{t}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{16\cdot 6\cdot(2\alpha+d)}\frac{\left(F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t})\right)^{k}}{k!2^{k}\sigma^{2k}_{t}}\right)+O(n^{-4})

and since
\diam M^{*}\leq 2

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle:=\int_{B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\left\lceil\frac{5\log n}{2(\gamma-1)\log(\log n)}\right\rceil}\frac{\left(F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t})\right)^{k}}{k!2^{k}\sigma^{2k}_{t}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{16\cdot 6\cdot(2\alpha+d)}\frac{\left(F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t})\right)^{k}}{k!2^{k}\sigma^{2k}_{t}}\right)p_{i}(z)dz

\displaystyle=g_{i}\left(t,X_{i,t}\right)+O(n^{-4}),

(D.18)

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle:=\int_{B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N})}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\left\lceil\frac{5\log n}{2(\gamma-1)\log(\log n)}\right\rceil}\frac{\left(F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t})\right)^{k}}{k!2^{k}\sigma^{2k}_{t}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{16\cdot 6\cdot(2\alpha+d)}\frac{\left(F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t})\right)^{k}}{k!2^{k}\sigma^{2k}_{t}}\right)p_{i}(z)dz

(D.19)

\displaystyle=f_{i}\left(t,X_{i,t}\right)+O(n^{-4}).

\Phi^{*}_{i}
is polynomial

\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)=G_{i}+P^{*}_{i}z+\sum a^{*}_{i,S}z^{S}
,
where
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\operatorname{Im}P^{*}_{i}\subset\mathcal{H}_{i},a^{*}_{i,S}\in\mathcal{H}_{i}
, therefore introducing a linear isometry
P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}:\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{i}
that identifies
\mathcal{H}_{i}
with
\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
we represent

\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)=G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z),

where
\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z):\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
is a polynomial functions

\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z)=P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P^{*}_{i}z+{\sum P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}a^{*}_{i,S}z^{S}}

of degree
\beta-1
in
d
variables with coefficients belonging to
\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
and
\widetilde{R}_{i}(z):=\sum P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}a^{*}_{i,S}z^{S}
is corresponding second order residual.

So denoting
\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t)=P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\left(X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right)
we get

F^{(2)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)=2c_{t}\left\langle\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t),\widetilde{R}_{i}(z)\right\rangle-\left\lVert\widetilde{R}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t)\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t)-c_{t}\widetilde{R}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}:=\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}(\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t),z)

Similarly, introducing
\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i}(t)=(P^{*}_{i})^{T}(X_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

F^{(1)}_{i}(X_{i,t},z)={2c_{t}\left\langle\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i}(t),z\right\rangle-c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}}=\left\lVert\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i}(t)\right\rVert-\left\lVert\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i}(t)-c_{t}z\right\rVert:=\widetilde{F}^{(1)}_{i}(\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t),z).

Expanding brackets of (
D.18
) we represent
\hat{f}
and
\hat{g}
as polynomials in
c_{t},\sigma^{-2}_{t},\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i},\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}
with coefficients being integrals depending on unknown coefficients of
\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}
and density
p_{i}
that itself depends on samples
\mathcal{G}\subset\mathcal{Y}
used to construct the measure, and
\mu
– the density. More presisely

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|S^{(1)}\right|<s_{n}}\sum_{\left|S^{(2)}\right|<16\cdot 24\cdot(2\alpha+d)}\left(\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i}(t),\sigma^{-1}_{t},c_{t}\right)^{S^{(1)}}\left(\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t),\sigma^{-1}_{t},c_{t}\right)^{S^{(2)}}C_{S^{(1)},S^{(2)},g_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}(t,X_{i,t})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\left|S^{(1)}\right|<s_{n}}\sum_{\left|S^{(2)}\right|<16\cdot 24\cdot(2\alpha+d)}\left(\widetilde{X}^{(1)}_{i}(t),\sigma^{-1}_{t},c_{t}\right)^{S^{(1)}}\left(\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{i}(t),\sigma^{-1}_{t},c_{t}\right)^{S^{(2)}}C_{S^{(1)},S^{(2)},f_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right)

where
s_{n}=\left\lceil\frac{5\log n}{2(\gamma-1)\log(\log n)}\right\rceil
,
C_{S^{(1)},S^{(2)},g_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right)
are scalars and
C_{S^{(1)},S^{(2)},f_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
, while
S^{(1)}\in\mathbb{N}^{d+2}
and
S^{(2)}\in\mathbb{N}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}+2}

We treat the coefficients
C_{S^{(1)},S^{(2)},g_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right),C_{S^{(1)},S^{(2)},f_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right)
as unknown weights of the neural network that require optimization. Since
\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}\leq C_{\dim}\log n
and
s_{n}\leq\log n
, the number of monomials is bounded by

\binom{d+\log n}{\log n}\binom{C_{\dim}\log n+16\cdot 24\cdot(2\alpha+d)}{\log n}\leq\left((2C_{\dim}+1)\log n+d\right)^{16\cdot 24\cdot(2\alpha+d)+d}=\polylog n.

Therefore, the number of weights required to parameterize all the coefficients
C_{S,g_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right),C_{S,f_{i}}\left(\mathcal{Y},\mu\right)
is bounded by
\polylog n
.

Summarizing,
\tilde{g}_{i}
and
\tilde{f}_{i}
are both represented as polynomials in
O(\log n)
variables, with
\polylog n
non-zero coefficients, and coefficients belonging to
\polylog n
subspace. Therefore, using Lemmas
F.2
–
F.4
both
\tilde{g}_{i}(t,x_{i,t}),\tilde{f}_{i}(t,x)
can be approximated by a neural networks
\phi_{g_{i}},\phi_{f_{i}}\in\Psi(L,W,S,B)
, where
L,W,S=\polylog n
and
B=e^{\polylog n}
, with an error less than
n^{-4}
(actually less than
n^{-C}
for any
C>0
).
Finally, applying
Lemma

F.4
we approximate
\tilde{e}_{i}:=\tilde{f}_{i}(t,x)/\tilde{g}_{i}(t,x_{i,t})
with a neural network
\phi_{e_{i}}\in\Psi(L,W,S,B)
of the same type, with an error less than
2n^{-2}
.

Applying argument for all
i\leq N
and combining (
D.17
), (
D.9
) and (
D.6
) we obtain that for
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
there is a network
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
s.t.

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\sigma^{2}_{t}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\lesssim\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{\frac{2\beta}{d}}.

D.2
Case
\underline{T}\leq T_{k}\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}

D.2.1
Summary

Recall that

\underline{T}\geq{(\log n)^{\beta\gamma+\frac{\beta}{d}}}n^{-\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}
;
we choose
\gamma>3-1/d
and

N=(\log n)^{-\gamma d}n^{\frac{d}{2\alpha+d}}
.
Then

N^{-\frac{\beta}{d}}=n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{\beta\gamma}\leq r_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{N}\lesssim n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{(\gamma+1/d)}
, and

\forall t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}],\quad\sigma_{t}/c_{t}\leq 2\sqrt{t}\leq\varepsilon_{N}(\log n)^{-(\gamma+1/d)}\leq\varepsilon_{N}(\log n)^{-3},\quad\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\lesssim\underline{T}.

Similarly to the case
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
, as a first step, we localize the score function and construct its truncated version
s_{tr}
.
As before, we introduce
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})
defined in (
D.2
), with this time
C(T_{k},n)=6\varepsilon^{2}_{N}
.
In this section, we define

C(n)=\sqrt{d\left(\log n+4C_{\log}\right)+4\log n}\asymp\sqrt{\log n}

We show that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
if
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
then
i\in I_{2}
where

I_{2}=\{i\leq N:\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 4\varepsilon_{N}\}.

(D.20)

In contrast to the case
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
, here we have
(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})=o(\varepsilon_{N})
, while Theorem
4.4
implies that the support of conditional measure
\mu(y|t,X_{t})
of scale
O(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})
. concentrated on
\Phi_{i}(G_{i},5\varepsilon_{N})
. On the other hand, this means that the
O(\varepsilon_{N})
scale is too coarse, and we need to better localize the support.

We solve this problem, by considering the projection of
X_{t}
onto
T_{G_{i}}\Phi^{*}_{i}(0,\varepsilon_{N})
, namely

z^{*}_{i}=z^{*}_{i}(X_{t})=(P^{*}_{i})^{T}\left(X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right)/c_{t},

(D.21)

and by defining the submanifolds
M_{i}
and their counterparts
M^{*}_{i}
as

\displaystyle M_{i}:=\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)\right),

\displaystyle M^{*}_{i}:=\Phi^{*}_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)\right)

(D.22)

that centered around
m_{i}:=\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
and
m^{*}_{i}:=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
.

As we show in
Section

D.2.2
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-2})

for all
i\in I_{2}
the local score
s_{i}(t,X_{t})
verifies

\left\lVert s_{i}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 2\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}n^{-2},\quad\text{where }s_{i}(t,X_{t}):=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\frac{\int_{M_{i}}(c_{t}y-X_{t})p(y|t,X_{t})}{p\left(M_{i}|t,X_{t}\right)}.

So, if we define

s_{tr}(t,X_{t})=\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})s_{i}(t,X_{t})}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})},

(D.23)

then we can prove that

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s_{tr}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\lesssim n^{-2}\sigma_{T_{k}}^{-2}.

(D.24)

The next step is the replacement of
s_{tr}
with its polynomial counterpart
s^{*}_{tr}
, which we define by constructing
s^{*}_{i}
. Denoting by
p_{i}(z)=\left|\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla^{T}\Phi_{i}\right|^{-1}p\left(\Phi_{i}(z)\right)
the density of pushforward of
\mu
restricted on
M_{i}
on the tangent space
T_{G_{i}}M_{i}
we represent

s_{i}(t,X_{t})=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\frac{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\Phi_{i}(z)e^{-\left\lVert c_{t}\Phi_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p_{i}(z)dz}{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}e^{-\left\lVert c_{t}\Phi_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p_{i}(z)dz}-\frac{X_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}},

(D.25)

and approximate it by substituting
\Phi_{i}
and
p_{i}
with their polynomial approximations
\Phi^{*}_{i},p^{*}_{i}
giving us

s^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t}):=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\frac{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)e^{-\left\lVert c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}e^{-\left\lVert c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}-\frac{X_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}.

(D.26)

Defining
s^{*}_{tr}
as a mixture of
s^{*}_{i}
and correspondingly
e^{*}_{tr}
,

s^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t}):=\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})s^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})},e^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t}):=\frac{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})}{\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})},

with
\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}e^{*}_{i}(x)=s^{*}_{i}(x)+\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}
,
the direct calculations performed in
Section

D.2.3
show

\int^{T_{k+1}}_{T_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})-s_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt=O\left(\sigma_{T_{k}}^{-2}\varepsilon_{N}^{2\beta}(\log n)^{2}+(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}n^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}\right)\\
=O\left(T_{k}^{-1}(\log n)^{2\gamma\beta+2}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha+d}}+(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}n^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}\right).

(D.27)

Next, we represent
e^{*}_{i}=f_{i}/g_{i}
, where
f_{i},g_{i}
are regularized such that they can be efficiently approximated using polynomials.

As in the case
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
the main challenge is the term
\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert
in the exponent, and which scales as
O(D)
. To tackle this first note that by definition
M^{*}_{i}
is centered around
m^{*}_{i}=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
. We approximate
M^{*}_{i}
by the tangent space
T_{m^{*}_{i}}M^{*}_{i}
and linearize
\Phi^{*}_{i}
as

\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})+\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})+R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i}),

and we finally define the projection of
x
onto
T_{m^{*}_{i}}M^{*}_{i}

x^{\pr}_{i}:=\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}\left(x-c_{t}m_{i}^{*}\right).

(D.28)

Note that
x^{\pr}_{i}
is not the same as
x_{i,t}=\pr_{c_{t}\mathcal{A}_{i}}x
.

Using that
(x-c_{t}m^{*}_{i})-x^{\pr}_{i}\perp T^{*}_{m^{*}_{i}}M^{*}_{i}
and
x-c_{t}G_{i}-x_{i,t}\perp\mathcal{H}_{i}\supseteq T^{*}_{m^{*}_{i}}M^{*}_{i}
we extract from
\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}m^{*}_{i}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}
a term
\left\lVert X_{t}-X_{t,i}^{\pr}\right\rVert^{2}
depending only
x
and not on
z
. This term carries the ambient
O(D)
contribution and cancels between numerator and denominator in the posterior ratio. More precisely, we write

\displaystyle g_{i}(t,x):=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\exp\left(-\frac{{\left\lVert x^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left(\frac{c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\left\langle x_{i,t}-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\right)p^{*}_{i}(z)dz,

\displaystyle f_{i}(t,x):=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert x^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left(\frac{c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\left\langle x_{i,t}-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\right)p^{*}_{i}(z)dz.

(D.29)

In
Section

D.2.4
we verify that
e^{*}_{i}=f_{i}/g_{i}
and show that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert X^{\pr}_{i}(t)-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}\leq{676}\sigma^{2}_{t}C^{2}(n)

\displaystyle\left|c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}-2c_{t}\left\langle X_{t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right|=O(\sigma_{t}^{3}(d\log n)^{2})

(D.30)

implying
g_{i}(t,X_{t})\geq n^{-2688d}.
So if we construct
\hat{f}_{i},\hat{g}_{i}
satisfying with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\left|g_{i}(t,X_{t})-\hat{g}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right|,\left\lVert f_{i}(t,X_{t})-\hat{f}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq n^{-2370d},

then since
\left\lVert e_{i}\right\rVert\leq 2
by construction

\displaystyle\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{f}_{i}(t,x)}{\hat{g}_{i}(t,x)},\quad\hat{e}_{tr}(t,x)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rho_{i}(t,x)\mathbbm{1}_{\left\lVert\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)\right\rVert\leq 2\sigma^{-2}_{t}}\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)}{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rho_{i}(t,x)},\quad\hat{s}(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\hat{e}_{tr}(t,x)-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}},

satisfy

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{s}(t,X_{t})-s^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\leq n^{-2}.

Finally, in
Section

D.2.5
using (
D.30
) we perform the Taylor expansion of the exponents in (
D.29
) and show that both
\hat{f}_{i}
and
\hat{g}_{i}
satisfying (
D.36
) can be obtained as a composition of polynomials of
\polylog
size, which can be approximated by neural networks also of
\polylog
size.

D.2.2
Score Function Localization

In this section we show that
s(t,X_{t})
is close to
s_{tr}(t,X_{t})
which is defined in (
D.23
).

Applying it with
A=n^{-2}
we conclude that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
if
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
then
i\in I_{2}
and

B_{M}\left(X_{0},2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\subset M_{i}\subset\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(0,7\varepsilon_{N}\right)\right).

Applying Theorem
4.4
with the same fixed tail
probability gives, for all
i\in I_{2}
,

\int_{M_{i}}p(y|t,X_{t})dy\geq\int_{B_{M}\left(X_{0},2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)}p(y|t,X_{t})dy\geq 1-n^{-2}.

We obtain that with probability greater than
1-n^{-2}
, for all
i\in I_{2}

p(M_{i}|t,X_{t})\geq 1-n^{-2}
and using the definition (
D.25
) of
s_{i}(t,X_{t})
together with
e_{i}(t,X_{t})=s_{i}(t,X_{t})+X_{t}/\sigma^{2}_{t}
and
\diam M\leq 1
.
we have that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
, for all
i\in I_{2}

\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})p\left(M_{i}|t,X_{t}\right)-e(t,X_{t})\right\rVert=\left\lVert\int_{M\backslash M_{i}}y\,p(y|t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq n^{-2},

Also, since
\left\lVert s_{i}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})-e(t,X_{t})\right\rVert
, we bound

\displaystyle\quad\left\lVert s_{i}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})\left(1-p\left(M_{i}|t,X_{t}\right)\right)\right\rVert+\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})p\left(M_{i}|t,X_{t}\right)-e(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 2\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}n^{-2}

with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
, for all
i\in I_{2}
.

Finally, recall that

s_{tr}(t,X_{t})=\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})s_{i}(t,X_{t})/(\sum_{i}\rho_{i}(t,X_{t}))
,
is a mixture of
s_{i}
with weights
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})\neq 0
only if
i\in I_{2}
. Therefore with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

\left\lVert s_{tr}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 2\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}n^{-2}.

Almost surely
\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})-e(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert+\left\lVert e(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 2
, so taking the expectation

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s_{tr}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{\sigma^{4}_{t}}n^{-4}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s_{tr}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt

\displaystyle\leq 8\frac{n^{-2}}{\sigma_{T_{k}}^{2}}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\frac{1}{t}dt\leq 8\frac{n^{-2}}{\sigma_{T_{k}}^{2}}\log 2,

which proves (
D.24
).

D.2.3
Polynomial Approximation of
f_{i}
and
g_{i}

The main goal of this section is to substitute
s_{tr}(t,x)
with the score corresponding to the piecewise polynomial approximations of the manifold
M
by
M^{*}=\cup_{i}M_{i}^{*}
with
M_{i}^{*}=\Phi_{i}^{*}(\Phi_{i}^{-1}(M_{i}))=\Phi_{i}^{*}\left(B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)\right)
and for each
i
,
p_{i}=\left|\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla^{T}\Phi_{i}\right|^{-1}p\circ\Phi_{i}
by a polynomial function
p_{i}^{*}
.

Recall that from (
D.38
), we have with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

M_{i}\subset B_{d}(0,7\varepsilon_{N})
so that we can express

e_{i}(t,X_{t})=\frac{\int_{M_{i}}y\,p(y|t,X_{t})}{p\left(M_{i}|t,X_{t}\right)}\\
=\frac{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}(\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i}))e^{-\left\lVert\Phi_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p_{i}(z)dz}{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}e^{-\left\lVert\Phi_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p_{i}(z)dz}+\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})

A natural way to approximate
e_{i}(t,x)
is to substitute
\Phi_{i}
by
\Phi^{*}_{i}
, and
p_{i}(z)
by its Taylor approximation of order
\alpha-1
at
0
which we denote
p^{*}_{i}(z)
. This leads to

e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t}):=\frac{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\left(\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z^{*}_{i})\right)e^{-\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}e^{-\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}+\Phi^{*}(z^{*}_{i})

We now bound
e_{i}^{*}-e_{i}
. First,
since
p_{i}(z)
is
\alpha
-smooth then

\left|p_{i}(z)-p^{*}_{i}(z)\right|\lesssim(\sigma_{t}C(n))^{\alpha}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha}_{N}

and by
Proposition

A.1

p_{i}(z)=\left|\nabla\Phi_{i}\nabla^{T}\Phi_{i}\right|^{-1}p\left(\Phi_{i}(z)\right)\geq 2^{-d}p_{\min}
, so

p_{i}(z)=p^{*}_{i}(z)\left(1+O\left((\sigma_{t}C(n))^{\alpha}\right)\right)=p^{*}_{i}(z)\left(1+O\left((\log n)^{\alpha/2}n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}\right)\right).

By Lemma
5.3
for all
z\in B_{d}(0,7\varepsilon_{N})

\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z)\right\rVert\lesssim\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\leq(\log n)^{2\gamma\beta}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\alpha+d}}.

Let
z\in B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))
, denoting
{y:=\Phi_{i}(z)}
and
y^{*}:=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)
we first bound

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}=c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+2c^{2}_{t}\left\langle X_{0}-y,y-y^{*}\right\rangle+2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle.

By Lemma
5.3
the first term is bounded as

c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert y-y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\leq c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim c^{2}_{t}\cdot\varepsilon^{2\beta}_{N}=o(\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\sigma_{t}c_{t})

By Proposition
5.7
for
n
large enough with probability
1-n^{-2}
the third term is bounded by

2c_{t}\sigma_{t}\cdot\left|\left\langle Z_{D},y-y^{*}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim c_{t}\sigma_{t}\cdot\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\cdot C(n)\leq c_{t}\sigma_{t}\cdot(\log n)^{(\gamma+1/d)\beta+1/2}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\alpha+d}}/2.

At the same time by
Proposition

G.1
,

X_{0}\in B_{M}(m_{i},2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))
, and
since
\Phi_{i}
is
2
-Lipschitz

\left\lVert X_{0}-y\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert X_{0}-m_{i}\right\rVert+\left\lVert m_{i}-y\right\rVert\leq(8+2)(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n),

(D.31)

the second term is bounded by, for
n
large enough,

c^{2}_{t}\left|\left\langle X_{0}-y,y-y^{*}\right\rangle\right|\lesssim c_{t}\sigma_{t}C(n)\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}=o(c_{t}\sigma_{t}\cdot(\log n)^{(\gamma+1/d)\beta+1/2}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\alpha+d}}),

where the constants depend only on
d,C_{log},\beta
.
Summing up we conclude that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}
and
y\in B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))

\frac{\left|\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}\right|}{2\sigma^{2}_{t}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}c_{t}C(n)}{\sigma_{t}}=o(1),

where the latter is due to
(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\geq(\log n)^{\gamma/2}n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\geq n^{-\frac{d\beta}{(d-2)(2\alpha+d)}}(\log n)^{\gamma/2}>>\sqrt{\log n}\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}
, for all
\beta>0
.
As a result

e^{-\frac{\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y^{*}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}=e^{-\frac{\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}y\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}c_{t}C(n)}{\sigma_{t}}\right)\right).

Combining the inequalities above, with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\displaystyle e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})=\frac{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\left(\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)e^{-\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}e^{-\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}+\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\left(\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)e^{-\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}{\int_{B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}e^{-\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-X_{t}\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}p^{*}_{i}(z)dz}+\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})+O\left(\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=e_{i}(t,X_{t})+O\left(\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle+\sup_{z\in B_{d}(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}\left\lVert\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert O\left((\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log n})^{\alpha}+\frac{\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}C(n)}{\sigma_{t}}\right).

Since
\Phi_{i}
is
2
-Lipschitz
\left\lVert\Phi_{i}(z)-\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert\leq 2\left\lVert z-z^{*}_{i}\right\rVert\leq 8\sqrt{d}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)
, applying that
\sigma^{2}_{t}\geq t/2\geq T_{k}/2,\sigma_{t}/c_{t}\leq 4t<4T_{k}
and
C(n)\lesssim\log n
we conclude that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})=e_{i}(t,X_{t})+O\left((\log n)^{\gamma\beta+2}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\alpha+d}}\right)+O\left(T^{1/2}_{k}n^{-\frac{\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{\alpha+1/2}\right).

Taking expectations, and noting that
\left\lVert e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 4T^{-1}_{k},\left\lVert e_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 2T^{-1}_{k}
a.s., we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})-s_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})-e_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(T^{-2}_{k}\varepsilon_{N}^{2\beta}\log n+T^{-1}_{k}n^{2\alpha/(2\alpha+d)}(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}\right).

Integrating over
[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
, since
T_{k+1}=2T_{k}\leq 2n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}

\displaystyle\int^{T_{k+1}}_{T_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})-s_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{T_{k}}^{2}}O\left(\varepsilon_{N}^{2\beta}\log n^{2}+\sigma_{T_{k}}^{2}n^{2\alpha/(2\alpha+d)}(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}\right)

Finally, using that
\rho_{i}(t,x)\neq 0
implies
i\in I_{2}
and by definition of
s^{*}_{tr}
we have

\int^{T_{k+1}}_{T_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})-s_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt=O\left(\sigma_{T_{k}}^{-2}{\varepsilon_{N}^{2\beta}}(\log n)^{2}+n^{2\gamma\alpha}n^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}\right).

which proves (
D.27
).

D.2.4
Polynomial Approximation of
e^{*}_{i}

To construct a neural network approximation of
e^{*}_{tr}
we first approximate
e_{i}^{*}
by a low degree polynomial of at most
O(\polylog n)
variables.
A key point is to treat
e^{-\left\lVert x-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}
in the definition of
e_{i}^{*}
since
\left\lVert x-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}\asymp D
. As explained in
Section

D.2.1
, we introduce the projection operator onto
T_{m^{*}_{i}}M^{*}_{i}
for
m_{i}^{*}=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
,

\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}=\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\left(\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)^{-1}\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),\quad x^{\pr}_{i}:=\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}\left(x-c_{t}m_{i}^{*}\right)

(D.32)

We then write

\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})+\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})+R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i}),

where polynomial
R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})
is the second order polynomial residual and
note that
\operatorname{Im}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})=T_{m^{*}_{i}}M^{*}_{i}
. Since

x^{\pr}_{i}
is the orthogonal projection of

x-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i}^{*})
onto this tangent space,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\lVert x-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert^{2}&=\left\lVert x-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i}^{*})-x^{\pr}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert x^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i}^{*})(z-z_{i}^{*})\right\rVert^{2}\\
&\quad-c_{t}^{2}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z_{i}^{*})\right\rVert^{2}-2c_{t}\left\langle x_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z),R_{i}(z,z_{i}^{*})\right\rangle.\end{aligned}

Indeed, the first two terms come from the orthogonal decomposition
into the tangent space and its orthogonal complement. For the
residual term, we use
R_{i}(z,z_{i}^{*})\in\mathcal{H}_{i}
and

x-x_{i,t}\perp\mathcal{H}_{i}
, which gives

\left\langle x-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i}^{*})-c_{t}\nabla\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i}^{*})(z-z_{i}^{*}),R_{i}(z,z_{i}^{*})\right\rangle=\left\langle x_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z),R_{i}(z,z_{i}^{*})\right\rangle+c_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z_{i}^{*})\right\rVert^{2}.

Therefore, since the first term in the displayed decomposition does
not depend on
z
, we can write
e_{i}^{*}=f_{i}/g_{i}
with
f_{i}
and

g_{i}
defined in (
D.29
).

We now bound from below
g_{i}
.

First we bound the residual exponential term in (
D.29
), using
Lemma

G.6

in
Section

G.2
.
It states that with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
,

\forall i\in I_{2}
and
\forall z\in B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)

\left|c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\left\langle X_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{3}(d\log n)^{2}.

Then, since
T_{k}<n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}

we get that for
n
large enough and with probability greater than
1-n^{-2}

\displaystyle\frac{\left|c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\left\langle X_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right|}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\exp\left(\frac{c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}+2c_{t}\left\langle X_{i,t}-c_{t}\Phi_{i}^{*}(z),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}(\log n)^{2}.

(D.33)

Next, we analyze the main term
{\left\lVert x^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}}
. Substituting
X_{t}
as
x
in (
D.32
) leads to

\displaystyle X^{\pr}_{i}(t)-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})

\displaystyle=\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}\left((X_{t})-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{t}\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}Z_{D}+c_{t}\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}\left(X_{0}-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z_{i}^{*})\right)-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i}).

(D.34)

Recall that
m^{*}_{i}=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
then

\left\lVert m^{*}_{i}-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert m^{*}_{i}-m_{i}\right\rVert+\left\lVert m_{i}-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq O\left(\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\right)+2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\leq 4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n),

where the last inequality follows from
Proposition

G.1
.
We bound the projection
\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}Z_{D}
using
Lemma

G.5
in
Section

G.2
with
\delta=n^{-2}
, so that with probability greater than
1-n^{-2}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\pr_{z_{i}^{*}}Z_{D}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 56dL_{M}\varepsilon_{N}+8\sqrt{d}(1+7L_{M}\varepsilon_{N})\sqrt{2\log 2d+6\log n}\leq 16C(n)

for
n
large enough since
\varepsilon_{N}=o(1)
. Finally, since
\|\nabla\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i}^{*})\|_{op}\leq\sup_{z\in B_{d}(0,7\varepsilon_{N})}\|\nabla\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)\|_{op}\leq 2
by Lemma
5.3
the last term of (
D.34
) is also bound by
8\sqrt{20}\sigma_{t}C(n)
leading to

\displaystyle\left\lVert X^{\pr}_{i}(t)-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\pr_{z^{*}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}+4c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-\Phi_{i}(z_{i}^{*})\right\rVert^{2}+4c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\cdot\sigma^{2}_{t}C^{2}(n)[256+16+64]=1344\sigma^{2}_{t}C^{2}(n).

(D.35)

Moreover, for
n
large enough
p^{*}(z)>\frac{1}{2}\inf_{B_{d}(0,8\varepsilon_{N})}p(z)\geq 2^{-d-1}p_{\min}
, so combining with (
D.33
) we get

\displaystyle g_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}p_{\min}e^{-676C^{2}(n)}\int_{B_{d}(z_{i}^{*},2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n))}dz=\frac{1}{4}p_{\min}e^{-676C^{2}(n)}C_{d}\left(2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)^{d},

where
C_{d}
is the volume of the
d
-dimensional unit ball.
Since
C(n)=\sqrt{d\left(\log n+4C_{\log}\right)+4\log n}
, then for
n
large enough
C^{2}(n)<4d\log n
. So, since
\sigma_{t}/c_{t}>n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}>n^{-1}
for
n
large enough with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
, for all
i\in I_{2}
,

g_{i}(t,X_{t})\geq n^{-2688d}.

Suppose
\hat{f}_{i}
and
\hat{g}_{i}
are approximations of
f_{i},g_{i}
such that with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\displaystyle\left|g_{i}(t,X_{t})-\hat{g}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right|\leq n^{-2690d},\quad\left\lVert f_{i}(t,X_{t})-\hat{f}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq n^{-2690d}.

(D.36)

then with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
, for all
i\in I_{2}

\left\lVert\hat{e}_{i}(t,X_{t})-e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert e^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert n^{-2}\leq 4n^{-2},\quad\text{with }\,\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)=\frac{\hat{f}_{i}(t,x)}{\hat{g}_{i}(t,x)}.

This implies that

\hat{e}_{tr}(t,x)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rho_{i}(t,x)\hat{e}_{i}(t,x)/(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rho_{i}(t,x))
verifies

\left\lVert\hat{e}_{tr}(t,X_{t})-e^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 4n^{-2}.

So, defining
\hat{e}_{\clip}(t,x)=\hat{e}(t,x)\mathbbm{1}_{\left\lVert\hat{e}(t,x)\right\rVert\leq 1}
and using that
\left\lVert e^{*}(t,x)\right\rVert\leq 1
a.s.

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{e}_{\clip}(t,X_{t})-e^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim\left(n^{-2}+n^{-4}\right)\lesssim n^{-2}.

Integrating we obtain

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{e}_{\clip}(t,X_{t})-e^{*}_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\lesssim n^{-2}\leq T_{k}n^{-1},

where the constant depends only on
d,\beta,C_{log}
.
So, defining
\hat{s}_{\clip}(t,x):=\frac{c_{t}\hat{e}_{\clip}(t,x)-x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}
and combining with (
D.27
)

\int^{t_{k+1}}_{t_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{s}_{\clip}(t,X_{t})-s_{tr}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt=O\left(\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}(\log n)^{2\gamma\beta}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha+d}}+(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}n^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}\right).

D.2.5
Neural Network Approximation

In this Section we show that we can construct neural networks for
\hat{f}_{i},\hat{g}_{i}
satisfying (
D.36
). To do that we first approximate them by polynomials whose coefficients are themselves polynomials of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t},c_{t},c_{t}^{-1},\sigma_{t},\sigma_{t}^{-1}
.

First, as in the case
T_{k}\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
, using the linear isometry
P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}:\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{i}
that identifies
\mathcal{H}_{i}
with
\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
, we can represent

\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)=G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z),
where
\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}
is a polynomial of degree
\beta-1
with coefficients
\widetilde{P}^{*}_{i}:=P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P^{*}_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
,
\widetilde{a}^{*}_{i,S}\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
belonging to
\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
. Define
\widetilde{x}_{i}=P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\left(x-c_{t}G_{i}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
,
and
\widetilde{x}^{\pr}_{i}=P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}x^{\pr}_{i}
so that

\widetilde{x}^{\pr}_{i}=\widetilde{\pr}_{z^{*}_{i}}\left(\widetilde{x}_{i,t}-c_{t}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)=\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\left(\nabla^{T}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)^{-1}\nabla^{T}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z_{i}^{*})(\widetilde{x}_{i,t}-c_{t}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})).

And since
P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}
is an isometry

\displaystyle\left\lVert x^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle={\left\lVert\widetilde{x}^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}},

\displaystyle\left\lVert R(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\widetilde{R}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\left\langle X_{i,t}-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i}),R(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widetilde{X}_{i,t}(t)-c_{t}\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i}),\widetilde{R}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle.

Therefore
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
can be re-expressed in terms of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t},\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{*},\widetilde{R}
etc.
Then using a Taylor approximation of
e^{u}
near
0
together with
(
D.35
) and (
D.33
), we have that for
n
large enough with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

\displaystyle\left|e^{-{\left\lVert\widetilde{x}^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}-\sum_{k=1}^{K_{1}}\frac{1}{k!}\left(-{\left\lVert\widetilde{x}^{\pr}_{i}-c_{t}\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}}/2\sigma^{2}_{t}\right)^{k}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(\frac{e\cdot 2368d\log n}{K_{1}}\right)^{K_{1}}\leq{e^{-e^{2}\cdot 2368d\log n}<n^{-2370d}},

by choosing
K_{1}=e^{2}\cdot 2368d\log n=O(d\log n)
. And similarly

\displaystyle\bigg|e^{\left(c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\widetilde{R}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}-c_{t}\left\langle\widetilde{X}_{i,t}(t)-c_{t}\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i}),\widetilde{R}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right)/2\sigma^{2}_{t}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{K_{2}}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\left(c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert\widetilde{R}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}-c_{t}\left\langle\widetilde{X}_{i,t}(t)-c_{t}\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z-z^{*}_{i}),\widetilde{R}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right)/2\sigma^{2}_{t}\right)^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\frac{en^{-\frac{1}{2(2\alpha+d)}}}{K_{2}}\right)^{K_{2}}\quad\leq n^{-2370d}

by choosing
K_{2}=2(2\alpha+d)2370d=O(d\alpha+d))
. We write
Q_{1}(z-z_{i}^{*})
the polynomial approximation of the first term and
Q_{2}(z-z_{i}^{*})
the polynomial approximation of the second (as functions of
z
).
Q_{1}
has degree
2K_{1}
and
Q_{2}

2K_{2}
. Moreover
p_{i}^{*}
is a polynomial function of degree
\alpha
; therefore

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{i}(t,x):=\int_{\|z-z_{i}^{*}\|\leq 4\sigma_{t}C(n)/c_{t}}Q_{1}(z-z_{i}^{*})Q_{2}(z-z_{i}^{*})p_{i}^{*}(z)dz=\int_{\|z\|\leq 4\sigma_{t}C(n)/c_{t}}Q_{1}(z)Q_{2}(z)Q_{3}(z)dz

where
Q_{3}
is a polynomial function of
z
of degree
\alpha
and we have
p_{i}^{*}\leq 2p_{max}

|\bar{g}_{i}(t,x)-g_{i}(t,x)|\leq 4n^{-2370d}\Vol B_{d}(0,4\sigma_{t}C(n)/c_{t})\lesssim T_{k}^{d}(\log n)^{d}n^{-2370d}=o(n^{-2370d})

Similarly, since
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{*}
is also polynomial with degree
\beta
and
\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{*}(z)\|\leq 2
over the ball of integration,
|\bar{f}_{i}(t,x)-f_{i}(t,x)|o(n^{-2370d})
with

\displaystyle\bar{f}_{i}(t,x):=\int_{\|z\|\leq 4\sigma_{t}C(n)/c_{t}}Q_{4}(z)Q_{1}(z)Q_{2}(z)Q_{3}(z)dz,\quad Q_{4}(z)=\Phi_{i}^{*}(z+z_{i}^{*}).

We write

Q_{j}(z)=\sum_{|S|\leq 2K_{j}}a_{S,Q_{j}}z^{S}
with
\beta\geq 2K_{3}\geq\beta-1
and
\alpha-1\leq 2K_{4}\leq\alpha
and note that
Q_{j}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
for
j=1,2,3
and
Q_{4}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
. Then by construction
a_{S,Q_{1}}
are polynomial functions of degree less than
O(K_{1})
of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t}^{pr},\sigma_{t}^{-2},c_{t},z_{i}^{*}
since
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{*}=\widetilde{P}_{i}^{*}+\sum_{S^{\prime}}\widetilde{a}_{i,S}^{*}z^{S^{\prime}}
. Also
a_{S,Q_{2}}
are polynomial functions of degree less than
O(K_{2})
of the first
\beta
derivatives of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{i}^{*}
at
z_{i}^{*}
, of
c_{t},\sigma_{t}^{-1},\widetilde{x}_{i,t}
so that they are polynomial functions of degree less than
O(K_{2})
of
c_{t},\sigma_{t}^{-1},\widetilde{x}_{i,t},z_{i}^{*}
. Similarly for
a_{S,Q_{3}}
which as a degree of order
O(\alpha d)
. Finally
a_{S,Q_{4}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
, whose components are polynomial functions of degree
\beta
of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t},z_{i}^{*}
.
Since for all vector of indices
S

\int_{\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq 4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)}z^{S}dz=(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{S}\left(4\sqrt{20}C(n))\right)^{S}\int_{\left\lVert z\right\rVert\leq 1}z^{S}=c_{S,n}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{S}

we can write

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S_{1},\cdots,S_{3}}\prod_{l=1}^{3}a_{S_{l},Q_{l}}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{\sum_{l}S_{l}}C_{\sum_{l}S_{l},n}

\displaystyle\bar{f}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S_{1},\cdots,S_{4}}\prod_{l=1}^{4}a_{S_{l},Q_{l}}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{\sum_{l}S_{l}}C_{\sum_{l}S_{l},n}.

We now show that
\widetilde{x}_{i,t}^{\pr}
and
z_{i}^{*}
are polynomial functions of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t}
and
c_{t}^{-1}
. Recall that
x_{i,t}=\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(x-ctG_{i})+c_{t}G_{i}
and since
\operatorname{Im}P^{*}_{i}\subset\mathcal{H}_{i}
then
z^{*}_{i}:=\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}(x-c_{t}G_{i})/c_{t}=\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}(x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i})/c_{t}

and

\left(\widetilde{P}^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}\widetilde{x}_{i,t}/c_{t}=\left(P^{*}_{i}\right)^{T}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P^{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\left(x_{i,t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right)=z^{*}_{i}.

Therefore
z^{*}_{i}
is a polynomial of order
2
of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t},c_{t}^{-1}
.
Now recall that

\widetilde{x}^{\pr}_{i}=\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\left(\nabla^{T}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)^{-1}\nabla^{T}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(\widetilde{x}_{i,t}-c_{t}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}))

and that
\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z)=\widetilde{P}^{*}_{i}z+\sum\widetilde{a}^{*}_{i,S}z^{S}
; with
\widetilde{a}^{*}_{i,S}\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
and
|S|_{1}\leq\beta-1
. Therefore
\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z_{i}^{*})
is a polynomial of degree smaller than
\beta-2
of
z_{i}^{*}
, hence it is a polynomial of degree
2(\beta-2)
of
\widetilde{x}_{i,t},c_{t}^{-1}
.
As a consequence we obtain that

•

the entries of the
d\times d
matrix
\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
are polynomials in
c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t}
of order at most
4(\beta-2)
. In other words
\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})=Q_{d,d}(c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t})
, for some polynomial
Q_{1}
with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
;

•

the entries of the
d\times 1
vector
\nabla^{T}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(\widetilde{x}_{i,t}-c_{t}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}))
are polynomials in
c^{-1}_{t},x_{i,t}
of order at most
2\beta
. In other words
\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(\widetilde{x}_{i,t}-c_{t}\widetilde{\Phi}^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}))=Q_{d}(c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t})
, for some polynomial
Q_{d}
with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
;

•

Cramer’s rule states that if
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is invertible then

A^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det A}((-1)^{i+j}\det M_{ji})_{i,j\leq d},

where the matrix
M_{ij}
is the
ij
-minor – matrix. Also the
determinant of a
d\times d
matrix is a polynomial of order
d
in its entries
Therefore, the inverse matrix can be represented as

\left(\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right)^{-1}=\frac{Q_{\operatorname{adj}}(c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t})}{Q_{\det}(c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t})},

where
Q_{\operatorname{adj}}
is a polynomial in
c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t}
of order
2(d-1)(\beta-2)
with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, and
Q_{\det}
is a polynomial in
c^{-1}_{t},\widetilde{x}_{i,t}
of order
2d(\beta-2)
with scalar coefficients.

Combining the above we obtain that
\widetilde{x}_{i,t}^{\pr}
is a polynomial of degree 4 of
Q_{d},Q_{d,d},Q_{\det}^{-1},Q_{\operatorname{adj}}
.

Also, applying
Lemma

G.5
we note that with probability
1-n^{-2}
for all
i\in I_{2}

\left\lVert\widetilde{X}_{i,t}(t)\right\rVert=\left\lVert X_{i,t}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\left(c_{t}X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}-c_{t}G_{i}\right)\right\rVert\leq c_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\lesssim O\left(\log n\right),

and using that
1/2\leq\left\lVert\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\nabla^{T}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert\leq 2
we conclude that all variables
\widetilde{x}_{i,t},\sigma^{-1}_{t},\sigma_{t},c_{t},c^{-1}_{t},Q_{d},Q_{d\times d},Q_{\operatorname{adj}}
are bounded by
n
.

We can now construct the neural network approximations of
\bar{f}_{i}
and
\bar{g}_{i}
.
Using Lemmas
F.2
–
F.4
, neural networks can be chosen
\phi_{\bar{g}},\phi_{\bar{f}},\phi_{Q_{d}},\phi_{Q_{d\times d}},\phi_{Q_{\operatorname{adj}}},\phi_{Q^{-1}_{\det}}\in\Psi(L_{1},W_{1},S_{1},B_{1})
, where
L_{1},W_{1},S_{1}=\polylog n
and
B_{1}=e^{\polylog n}
.

Finally, applying
Lemma

F.1
and then
Lemma

F.4
we concatenate these neural networks and get two neural networks
\phi_{g_{i}},\phi_{e_{i}}\in\Psi(L,W,S,B)
, where
L,W,S=\polylog n
and
B=e^{\polylog n}
that approximate
g_{i}
and
e_{i}=f_{i}/g_{i}
respectively with an error
n^{-2370d}
.

Constructing these networks for all
i\leq N
we show that for
T_{k}\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
there is a network
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
s.t.

\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\leq O\left(\sigma^{-2}_{T_{k}}(\log n)^{4\gamma\beta}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\alpha+d}}+(\log n)^{2\alpha+1}n^{-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+d}}\right).

D.3
Tail Bounds

In this section, we first prove propositions describing the sets of indices for which
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
. Then we prove the tail bound statement of Theorem
3.3
. All the statements in the section hold for an arbitrary chosen initial point
X_{0}\in M
and with high probability in
Z_{D}
.

Proposition D.1
.

Assume
T_{k}\geq n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}
, and fix
t\geq T_{k}
and
A>0
. For

t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
define

I_{1}(t):=\left\{i\leq N:\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq\frac{1}{3}(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}\right\},

and

I_{2}(t):=\left\{i\leq N:\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 3(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}\right\}.

Then for all sufficiently large
n
and
A\leq\frac{1}{1200}\log\log n
with probability at least

1-n^{-A}
,

I_{1}(t)\subseteq\{i:\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})=1\},\qquad\{i:\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0\}\subseteq I_{2}(t).

Proof.

Both statements are corollaries of Lemma
4.5
obtained
by substituting the
\varepsilon_{N}
-dense set
\mathcal{G}
. The lemma states that
with probability at least
1-n^{-A}
, for all
i\leq N
simultaneously

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}c_{t}^{-2}\Delta_{i}(X_{t})-128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}B_{t}\leq\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 9\varepsilon_{N}^{2}+2c_{t}^{-2}\Delta_{i}(X_{t})+128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}B_{t},

(D.37)

where

\Delta_{i}(X_{t}):=\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{\min}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2},\qquad B_{t}:=d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4dC_{\log}+A\log n.

Note that
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})=\rho(\Delta_{i}(X_{t})/2C(T_{k},n))
. Since

t\geq n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}
, for large enough
n
we have
192B_{t}\leq\frac{1}{6}\log n\log\log n
.

We start by proving the first inclusion

I_{1}(t)\subseteq\{i:\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})=1\}
. By definition of
\rho_{i}
, it is
enough to prove that
\Delta_{i}(X_{t})\leq C(T_{k},n)
.

Let
i\in I_{1}(t)
, the first inequality in (
D.37
) combined with definition of
I_{1}(t)
implies

\Delta_{i}(X_{t})\leq\frac{3}{2}c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}+192\sigma^{2}_{t}B_{t}\leq\frac{1}{6}\sigma_{t}^{2}\log n\log\log n+192\sigma^{2}_{t}B_{t}\leq\frac{1}{3}\sigma^{2}_{t}\log n\log\log n.

Finally, using that
\sigma^{2}_{t}\leq 2\sigma^{2}_{T_{k}}
we conclude that for
i\in I_{1}(t)

\Delta_{i}(X_{t})\leq\frac{2}{3}\sigma^{2}_{T_{k}}\log n\log\log n=\frac{2}{3}C(T_{k},n).

This proves the first inclusion.

Next, we prove that
\{i:\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0\}\subseteq I_{2}(t)
. Let
i
satisfy
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
, by definition of
\rho_{i}
we have

\Delta_{i}\leq 2C(T_{k},n)=2\sigma^{2}_{T_{k}}\log n\log\log n\leq 2\sigma^{2}_{t}\log n\log\log n

Substituting this into (
D.37
) we get

\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 9\varepsilon_{N}^{2}+4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\log n\log\log n+128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}B_{t}

Since
128B_{t}\leq\frac{1}{9}\log n\log\log n
and by (
D.4
)

\varepsilon_{N}=o(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})
for
n
large enough,

\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 5(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\log n\log\log n\leq 9(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\log n\log\log n.

That proves the second inclusion.
∎

The next proposition is a small time counterpart of the proposition above.

Proposition D.2
.

Fix
t\in[\underline{T},n^{-\frac{2}{2\alpha+d}}]
and
A>0
. Then, for all sufficiently large
n
with
\log n>A
, with probability at least
1-n^{-A}
, the following holds.

(i)

Every active index satisfies

\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad i\in I_{2},\qquad I_{2}:=\{i\leq N:\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 4\varepsilon_{N}\}.

In particular, for every such index
i
,

X_{0}\in\Phi_{i}(B_{d}(0,4\varepsilon_{N})),\qquad\operatorname{dist}(X_{0},G_{i}+\mathcal{H}_{i})\lesssim\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}.

(ii)

Let

C(n,A)=\sqrt{d\left(\log n+4C_{\log}\right)+4A\log n}.

Then, for all sufficiently large
n
, every active index (
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
) satisfies

B_{M}\left(X_{0},2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\subset\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\right)\subset\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(0,7\varepsilon_{N}\right)\right).

(D.38)

Proof.

(i)

Since
\mathcal{G}
is
\varepsilon_{N}/2
-dense in
M
,
Lemma
4.5
, applied with failure probability

n^{-A}/2
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{N}/2
, gives simultaneously for all

i\leq N

\displaystyle\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9}{4}\varepsilon_{N}^{2}+2c_{t}^{-2}\left(\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{\min}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+128(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{2}\left(d\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})+4dC_{\log}+A\log n+\log 2\right).

If
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
, then

\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert X_{t}-c_{t}G_{\min}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}<2C(T_{k},n)=6\varepsilon_{N}^{2}.

Since
c_{t}=e^{-t}
, for large enough

n
for any
t<n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}
we have
c_{t}^{-2}=1+O(t)\leq 25/24
. Since
A<\log n
and
\log_{+}(c_{t}/\sigma_{t})\lesssim\log n
, the
logarithmic factor in the last term is bounded by a fixed multiple of

(\log n)^{2}
, at the same time by the choice of
\gamma

\sigma_{t}/c_{t}\leq\varepsilon_{N}(\log n)^{-3},

so for
n
large enough, the last term is bounded by
\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{N}^{2}
. Therefore

\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left(\frac{9}{4}+\frac{25}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right)\varepsilon_{N}^{2}<16\varepsilon_{N}^{2},

which gives that
i\in I_{2}
.

By construction,
\Phi_{i}
is the inverse of the projection on the tangent space at
G_{i}
, so if
z_{i}
is the projection of

X_{0}-G_{i}
on
T_{G_{i}}M
then
X_{0}=\Phi_{i}(z_{i})
and
\|z_{i}\|\leq\|X_{0}-G_{i}\|\leq 4\varepsilon_{N}
. Finally, by construction, the approximation
\Phi^{*}_{i}
satisfy

\operatorname{dist}(X_{0},G_{i}+\mathcal{H}_{i})\leq\left\lVert\Phi_{i}(z_{i})-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{i})\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}.

(ii)

Let
m_{i}=\Phi_{i}(z^{*}_{i})
. Proposition
G.1
,
with the union bound over
i\leq N
and failure probability
n^{-A}/2
, gives that for all
i\in I_{2}

\left\lVert m_{i}-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d+\log n+2A\log n}\leq 2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A),

where the last inequality holds for all sufficiently large
n
, as
(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\geq\varepsilon^{\beta}_{N}\log n
. That shows

\displaystyle B_{M}\left(X_{0},2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)

\displaystyle\subset B_{M}\left(m_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)

\displaystyle\subset\Phi_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\right).

Finally, since
z^{*}_{i}
is a projection on
T_{G_{i}}M
of
m_{i}

\left\lVert z^{*}_{i}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert m_{i}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert m_{i}-X_{0}\right\rVert+\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)+4\varepsilon_{N}.

So, since for
n
large enough
{2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\leq\varepsilon_{N}}
we conclude

B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\subset B_{d}\left(0,4\varepsilon_{N}+6(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\subset B_{d}\left(0,7\varepsilon_{N}\right),

By applying
\Phi_{i}
, we finish the proof.

∎

By applying these propositions, we prove

Lemma D.3
.

Assume
T_{k}\geq n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}
. For every fixed

t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
and
A
satisfying
A\leq\frac{1}{1200}\log\log n
, with probability at least
1-2n^{-A}
, every active
index
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
satisfies

\left\lVert c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq 4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

and

\left\lVert c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-X_{i,t}\right\rVert\leq 8\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

for all
n\geq C_{0}(d,C_{\log})
.

Proof.

By construction

M_{i}^{*}=\Phi_{i}^{*}(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{N}))\subset G_{i}+\mathcal{H}_{i}
and

\Phi_{i}^{*}(0)=G_{i}
. Since
\Phi_{i}^{*}
is
2+O(\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-1})
-Lipschitz, for
n
large enough
every
y^{*}\in M_{i}^{*}
satisfies
\|y^{*}-G_{i}\|\leq 3\varepsilon_{N}
. The point

e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})
is a weighted average over
M_{i}^{*}
and
\varepsilon_{N}=o(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})
, hence

\left\lVert e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 3\varepsilon_{N}\leq\sigma_{t}/c_{t}.

Combining Proposition
D.1
and
Proposition
C.2
both applied
with
\delta=n^{-A}
we get that for
n
large enough with probability at least
1-2n^{-A}

\left\lVert X_{0}-G_{i}\right\rVert\leq 3(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}

and

\max_{i\leq N}\left\lVert\pr_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 4\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

Using
X_{t}=c_{t}X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}
and
e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})\in G_{i}+\mathcal{H}_{i}
,

\displaystyle X_{i,t}-c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})

\displaystyle=c_{t}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{T}(X_{0}-G_{i})+\sigma_{t}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{T}Z_{D}-c_{t}(e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-G_{i}).

Combining with the inequalities above, we conclude

\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq 8\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

While, by dropping
Z_{D}
term we get

\left\lVert c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq 4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log n\log\log n}.

∎

The following lemma is a small-time counterpart of the lemma above.

Lemma D.4
.

Assume
\underline{T}\leq T_{k}<n^{-2/(2\alpha+d)}
.
For every fixed
t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
and
A\leq\log n
, with probability at least

1-O(n^{-A})
, every active index
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
satisfies

\left\lVert c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq 241\sigma_{t}\log n

and

\left\lVert c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-X_{i,t}\right\rVert\leq 243\sigma_{t}\log n

for all
n\geq C_{0}(d,C_{\log})
.

Proof.

By construction
e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})
is the integral over
M^{*}_{i}=\Phi^{*}_{i}\left(B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)\right)
. So it is enough to bound
\left\lVert c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(t,X_{t})-X_{i,t}\right\rVert
for all

z\in B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\right)
.

Since
\Phi_{i}^{*}
is
2+O(\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta-1})
-Lipschitz, for
n
large enough

\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)\right\rVert\leq 12(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\leq 120(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\log n.

By Proposition
D.2
with probability at least
1-n^{-A}
it holds
X_{0}\in M_{i}
, since
\left\lVert\Phi_{i}-\Phi_{i}^{*}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}

\left\lVert X_{0}-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+12(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n,A)\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+120(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\log n.

Finally, applying Proposition
C.2
, for
n
large enough, with probability at least
1-n^{-A}

\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{(C_{\dim}+2)\log n+2\log 2n^{A}}\leq 2\log n.

Therefore, representing

\displaystyle X_{i,t}-c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})

\displaystyle=c_{t}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{T}(X_{0}-G_{i})+\sigma_{t}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{T}Z_{D}-c_{t}(e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})-G_{i}).

and using that
c_{t}\leq 1
and
\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}\lesssim\underline{T}\lesssim\sigma_{t}
we conclude

\left\lVert X_{i,t}-c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+242\sigma_{t}\log n\leq 243\sigma_{t}\log n.

Similarly, by dropping the term with
Z_{D}

\left\lVert X_{0}-c_{t}e_{i}^{*}(t,X_{t})\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+240\sigma_{t}\log n\leq 241\sigma_{t}\log n.

∎

By combining these lemmas, we get the tail bound in Theorem
3.3
as an immediate consequence.

Corollary D.5
.

For every fixed
t\in[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
and
A\leq\frac{1}{1200}\log n\log\log n
, on event

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-A}
every

\phi_{s}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
satisfies

{\left\lVert\sigma_{t}\phi_{s}(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 244\log n.}

Consequently the ERM
\hat{s}_{k}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
satisfies
the tail bound in Theorem
3.3
.

Proof.

For
\phi_{s}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
, define

\phi_{e}(t,x):=\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{c_{t}}\phi_{s}(t,x)+\frac{x}{c_{t}},

We need to bound

\left\lVert\sigma_{t}\phi_{s}(t,X_{t})+Z_{D}\right\rVert=\frac{\left\lVert c_{t}\phi_{e}(t,X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{t}}.

By construction,
\phi_{e}(t,X_{t})
is a convex combination of functions

e_{i}^{\phi}(t,X_{t}):=G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\phi_{i}(t,X_{t})
with indices satisfying
\rho_{i}(t,X_{t})>0
, so it is enough to bound
\left\lVert c_{t}e^{\phi}_{i}(t,X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert.

Conditioned on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}
we have
\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}\leq C_{\dim}\log n
, so combined with the definition of class
\mathcal{S}_{k}
we have

{\left\lVert c_{t}e_{i}^{\phi}(t,X_{t})-X_{i,t}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}\,\sigma_{t}\log n\lesssim\sigma_{t}\log n\sqrt{\log n}.}

Applying Propositions
D.3
and
D.4
we obtain that

\left\lVert c_{t}X_{i,t}-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq 243\sigma_{t}\log n.

Therefore

\frac{\left\lVert c_{t}e^{\phi}_{i}(t,X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{t}}\leq 244\sigma_{t}\log n

∎

D.4
Generalization error & Proof of Theorem
3.3

Recall that we approximate the score
s(t,x)
by neural networks of the form

{\phi_{s}(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\frac{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\left(G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\phi_{e_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\right)}{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})}-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}},\quad\widetilde{x}_{i,t}=P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{T}(x-c_{t}G_{i}),}

where
\phi_{e_{i}}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}
and
\phi_{w_{i}}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{i}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
are neural networks of
\polylog
size belonging to the class
\mathcal{S}_{k}
defined in (
3.5
). In particular, on the relevant domain,

\mathcal{S}_{k}=\Big\{\phi_{s}:\phi_{e_{i}},\phi_{w_{i}}\in\Psi\left(L,W,B,S\right),\quad\left\lVert\phi_{e_{i}}\right\rVert\leq C_{2},\\
\left\|\frac{c_{t}\phi_{e_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})-\widetilde{x}_{i,t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\log n,\quad\left\lVert\phi_{w_{i}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\geq n^{-2}\Big\},

The network parameters satisfy

L=O\left(\polylog n\right),\left\lVert W\right\rVert_{\infty}=O\left(\polylog n\right),S=O\left(\polylog n\right),B=e^{O\left(\polylog n\right)}.

The estimator
\hat{s}
of the score
s(t,x)
on interval
[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
is defined as

{\hat{s}_{k}\in}\argmin_{\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1}),\quad\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{y_{i}}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1}).

and the loss function
\ell_{y}
for
y\in M
is defined in (
2.11
) as

\ell_{y}(\hat{s},T_{k},T_{k+1}):=\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{D}}\|\hat{s}\left(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}\right)+Z_{D}/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}dt.

Theorem C.4 in
[
31
]
, states (Note that the original proof has an error which we fix in
Section

D.5
)

\displaystyle\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{s}_{k}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\lesssim\inf_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sup_{y\in M}\sup_{\phi}\ell_{y}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1})}{n}\left(\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L},\|\cdot\|_{L_{\infty}(M)},\delta)+1\right)+\delta.

(D.39)

We have shown in
Section

D.2
that the first term is of order
O(T_{k}^{-1}n^{-2\beta/({2\alpha+d})}(\log n)^{4\gamma\beta}+n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+d)}(\log n)^{2\alpha+1})
. We now study the second term.
We follow
[
31
]
, but taking into account the dependency on ambient dimension
D
and the special form (
3.5
) of our estimators.

We first bound
\sup_{y\in M}\ell_{y}\left(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1}\right)
uniformly over
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
.
To do this let us first define

{\phi_{e}(t,x):=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{t}}{c_{t}}\phi_{s}(t,x)+\frac{x}{c_{t}}=\frac{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\left(G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\phi_{e_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\right)}{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})},}

the estimator of the conditional expectation
e(t,x)
defined in (
2.15
). For fixed
y\in M
, set
X_{t}(y):=c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}
. Then

\displaystyle\ell_{y}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1})

\displaystyle={\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\frac{c^{2}_{t}}{\sigma^{4}_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{D}}\|\phi_{e}(t,X_{t}(y))-y\|^{2}dt}.

(D.40)

Applying Corollary
D.5
with
A=4
we get that with probability at least
1-2n^{-4}

\left\lVert\phi_{e}(t,X_{t}(y))-y\right\rVert\leq 244(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\log n.

(D.41)

Since
\left\lVert\phi_{e_{i}}\right\rVert\leq C_{2}
and by the choice of
C_{2}\geq 2\diam M
we have that almost surely
\left\lVert\phi_{e}-y\right\rVert\leq C_{2}+\diam M\leq 2C_{2}
.
Substituting into (
D.40
) gives, for all
y\in M
and all
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
,

{\ell_{y}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1})\leq\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\left(244\log n\cdot\sigma_{t}^{-2}+C_{2}n^{-4}\cdot\sigma_{t}^{-4}\right)dt\leq C_{\ell}\log^{3}n,}

(D.42)

where we used that
\sigma_{t}^{-2}\leq\frac{1}{2t}+1
and
T_{k}\lesssim\log n
. This bounds the first factor in the variance term in (
D.39
).

To complete the proof we thus only now need to control the covering number of
\mathcal{L}=\{y\mapsto\ell_{y}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1});\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}\}
and show that for
\delta\geq 1/n

\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L},\|\cdot\|_{L_{\infty}(M)},\delta)\lesssim N(\polylog n)(\log\delta^{-1}).

(D.43)

To do this we follow the proof of
[
31
]
, Lemma C.2.
First note that using Lemma 3 of
[
38
]
,
the covering number of
\Psi(L,W,S,B)
is bounded by

\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\Psi(L,W,S,B),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\leq 2SL\log(\delta^{-1}L(B\vee 1)(W+1)).

(D.44)

We now explain why this allows us to bound the number of
\mathcal{S}_{k}
by
\left\lVert\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{2}\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})}
- balls, where
\left\lVert\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{2}\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})}=\sup_{(x,t)\in[-C,C]^{D+1}}\left\lVert\phi(t,x)\right\rVert_{2}
required to cover
\mathcal{S}_{k}
.
Indeed for all
k
and
\phi^{1},\phi^{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
,
by definition of
\mathcal{S}_{k}
we have for
j=1,2
,

\left\lVert\phi^{j}_{e_{i}}\right\rVert\leq C_{2},\quad\left\lVert\frac{c_{t}\phi^{j}_{e_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})-\widetilde{x}_{i,t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\log n,\quad\text{and}\quad\phi^{j}_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\geq n^{-2}.

Moreover,
\sigma^{2}_{t}>n^{-1}
, so that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi^{1}-\phi^{2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert\frac{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi^{1}_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\left(G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\phi^{1}_{e_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\right)}{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi^{1}_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})}-\frac{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi^{2}_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\left(G_{i}+P_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}\phi^{2}_{e_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})\right)}{\sum_{i}^{N}\tilde{\rho}_{i}(t,x)\phi^{2}_{w_{i}}(t,\widetilde{x}_{i,t})}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\sigma^{-2}_{t}\cdot 2C_{2}n^{2}\left\lVert\phi^{1}_{w_{i}}-\phi^{2}_{w_{i}}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\phi^{1}_{e_{i}}(t,x)-\phi^{2}_{e_{i}}(t,x)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\quad\leq n^{3}C_{2}\left(\max_{i,t}\left\lVert\phi^{1}_{e_{i}}-\phi^{2}_{e_{i}}\right\rVert+\max_{i,t}\left\lVert\phi^{1}_{w_{i}}-\phi^{2}_{w_{i}}\right\rVert\right),

which implies that for all
\delta>0
and all
C>0

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{S}_{k},\left\lVert\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{2}\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})},\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}(\delta n^{-3}/2C_{2},\Psi(L,W,S,B),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2n^{d/(2\alpha+d)}(\polylog n)\log(C\delta^{-1}).

To prove (
D.43
), we then bound for
\left\lVert\left\lVert\phi^{1}-\phi^{2}\right\rVert_{2}\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}([-C_{5}\sqrt{\log n},C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}]^{D+1})}\leq\delta^{\prime}
with
\phi^{1},\phi^{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\left|\ell_{y}(\phi^{1})-\ell_{y}(\phi^{2})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi^{1}\left(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}\right)+Z_{D}/\sigma_{t}\right\rVert^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi^{2}\left(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}\right)+Z_{D}/\sigma_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\right)dt\right|

\displaystyle=\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\frac{c_{t}^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{y}(t)\sim\mathcal{N}(y,\sigma_{t}^{2}Id_{D})}[\|\phi_{e}^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|^{2}-\|\phi_{e}^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|^{2}]dt

where the last equality comes from (
D.40
) with
\phi_{e}^{j}(t,x)=\sigma_{t}^{2}\phi^{j}(t,x)/c_{t}+x/c_{t}
. We bound, using (
D.41
)

\displaystyle\|\phi_{e}^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|^{2}-\|\phi_{e}^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\left(\|\phi_{e}^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|-\|\phi_{e}^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|\right)\left(\|\phi_{e}^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|+\|\phi_{e}^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\|\phi_{e}^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-\phi_{e}^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))\|(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})(\log n)^{3/2}\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{t}^{3}}{c_{t}^{2}}\|\phi^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-\phi^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))\|(\log n)^{3/2}.

When

X_{y}(t)\in[-C_{5}\sqrt{\log n},C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}]^{D+1}
then

\|\phi^{1}(t,X_{y}(t))-\phi^{2}(t,X_{y}(t))\|\leq\left\lVert\left\lVert\phi^{1}-\phi^{2}\right\rVert_{2}\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}([-C_{5}\sqrt{\log n},C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}]^{D+1})}\leq\delta^{\prime}.

Also
