\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{y}(t)\sim\mathcal{N}(y,\sigma_{t}^{2}\Id_{D})}

\displaystyle\left[\mathbbm{1}_{X_{y}(t)\notin[-C_{5}\sqrt{\log n},C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}]^{D}}\|\phi_{e}^{j}(t,X_{y}(t))-y\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}(\log n)^{3}\rP[\mathcal{N}(y,\sigma_{t}^{2}\Id_{D})\notin[-C_{5}\sqrt{\log n},C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}]^{D}]

Since for all
y\in M
,
\|y\|_{\infty}\leq C_{\log}
, for all
C>0

\rP[\mathcal{N}(y,\sigma_{t}^{2}Id_{D})\notin[-C_{5}\sqrt{\log n},C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}]^{D}]\leq\rP\left(\left\lVert Z_{D}\right\rVert_{\infty}\geq C_{5}\sqrt{\log n}/2\right)\leq n^{-C}

since
\log D\lesssim\log n
and by choosing
C_{5}
large enough.
We finally have that

\displaystyle\left|\ell_{y}(\phi^{1})-\ell_{y}(\phi^{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\polylog(n)\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\sigma_{t}^{-1}dt[\delta^{\prime}+n^{-C}\polylog(n)]\lesssim\delta^{\prime}\polylog(n)

and choosing
\delta^{\prime}\asymp\delta/\polylog(n)
leads to
\left|\ell_{y}(\phi^{1})-\ell_{y}(\phi^{2})\right|\leq\delta
, which in turns implies
(
D.43
).

D.5
Correction of
[
31
]
, Theorem C.4

Theorem D.6
.

For all
k\leq K
let
\hat{\phi}_{k}
be a minimizer of the empirical risk, i.e.
\hat{\phi}_{k}:=\argmin_{\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}}\sum_{y_{i}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell_{y_{i}}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1})
.
Then for any
\delta_{n}>0
there is a constant
C_{b}>0
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{Y}}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{\phi}_{k}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\\
\leq 3\inf_{\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt\\
+\frac{C_{b}\log^{6}n}{n}\left(1\vee\log\mathcal{N}\left(\mathcal{L},\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}(M)},\delta_{n}\right)\right)+12\delta_{n}

(D.45)

Remark
.

The constant
3
in (
D.45
) can be replaced with
1+\delta
for any
\delta>0
at the expense of constants in front of the other two terms.

Proof.

We start by introducing some notation. We fix an index
k
and suppress it in what follows.
We recall that the de-noising score matching loss
\ell
at
y\in M
is defined as

\ell_{y}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1}):=\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}dt.

We write
\ell_{\phi}(y):=\ell_{y}(\phi,T_{k},T_{k+1})
to emphasize the dependence on
y\in M
.
The score-matching risk
R(\phi,s)
(
2.7
) is

R(\phi,s):=\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\phi(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}dt.

Note that by (
2.10
)

0\leq R(\phi,s):=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}l_{y}(\phi)-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}l_{y}(s).

Finally, we introduce the empirical risk and its deviation from the true risk

R_{n}(\phi)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{\phi}(y_{i}),\quad\Delta_{n}(\phi)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\ell_{\phi}(y)-R_{n}(\phi).

Fix
\delta_{n}>0
and let
\{\phi_{j}\}_{j\leq\mathcal{N}_{n}}
be a minimal
\delta_{n}
-cover of
{\mathcal{L}}=\{\ell_{\phi},\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\}
with respect to
L_{\infty}(M)
,
in other words
\mathcal{N}_{n}:=\mathcal{N}\left(\mathcal{L},\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{L_{\infty}(M)},\delta_{n}\right)
.
The case
\log\mathcal{N}_{n}<1
is trivial, so we assume that
\log\mathcal{N}_{n}\geq 1
.
Slightly abusing notation for all
j\leq\mathcal{N}_{n}
we write index
j
instead of
\phi_{j}
, namely
f(j)=f_{j}:=f(\phi_{j})
, e.g.
\ell_{j}=\ell_{\phi_{j}}
.

Since
\hat{\phi}
is the minimizer of the empirical risk for any
\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}

\displaystyle R(\hat{\phi},s)

\displaystyle=R_{n}(\hat{\phi})+\Delta_{n}(\hat{\phi})-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\ell_{s}(y)

\displaystyle\leq R_{n}(\phi)-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\ell_{s}(y)+\Delta_{n}(\hat{\phi})

\displaystyle=R(\phi,s)-[\Delta_{n}(\phi)-\Delta_{n}(s)]+[\Delta_{n}(\hat{\phi})-\Delta_{n}(s)].

(D.46)

Let
\phi^{*}:=\argmin_{\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}}R(\phi,s)
,
since
\{\phi_{j}\}_{j\leq\mathcal{N}_{n}}
form
\delta_{n}
dense net there are
\hat{j}
and
j^{*}

such that

\|\ell_{\hat{\phi}}-\ell_{\hat{j}}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n},\quad\left\lVert\ell_{\phi^{*}}-\ell_{j^{*}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n}.

Substituting
\phi^{*}
into (
D.46
) we conclude

\displaystyle R(\hat{\phi},s)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\phi\in{\mathcal{S}_{k}}}R(\phi,s)-[\Delta_{n}(j^{*})-\Delta_{n}(s)]+[\Delta_{n}(\hat{j})-\Delta_{n}(s)]+5\delta_{n}.

(D.47)

So, it is enough to control
\Delta_{n}(\hat{j})-\Delta_{n}(s)
and

\Delta_{n}(j^{*})-\Delta_{n}(s)
. We condition on the training sample

\mathcal{Y}
and introduce an independent ghost sample

\mathcal{X}=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}\sim\mu^{\otimes n}
. For each

j\leq\mathcal{N}_{n}
,

{\Delta_{n}(j)-\Delta_{n}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[(\ell_{j}(x_{i})-\ell_{j}(y_{i}))-(\ell_{s}(x_{i})-\ell_{s}(y_{i}))].}

Set

g_{j}(x,y):=(\ell_{j}(x)-\ell_{j}(y))-(\ell_{s}(x)-\ell_{s}(y))
.
Then
\mathbb{E}_{x,y\sim\mu}g_{j}(x,y)=0
, and the variance can be bounded by Jensen’s inequality as

\begin{split}&\Var_{x,y\sim\mu}g_{j}({x,y})=2\Var_{y\sim\mu}\left[\ell_{j}(y)-\ell_{s}(y)\right]\\
&\quad\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\mu}\Bigg[\bigg(\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{D}}\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}dt\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\\
&\quad\leq 2(T_{k+1}-T_{k})\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left[\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}\right]^{2}dt.\end{split}

Writing

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}-\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\langle\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}),s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rangle,

and recalling that
T_{k+1}=2T_{k}
we obtain the following bound

\displaystyle\Var_{x,y\sim\mu}g_{j}(x,y)\leq 8T_{k}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{4}dt

\displaystyle\quad+32T_{k}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}dt.

•

By Tweedie’s formula
s(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}\mathbb{E}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]-X_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}
, and since
\sigma^{2}_{t}\geq\sigma^{2}_{T_{k}}\geq\min(1,T_{k})/2
, a.s.

\left\lVert s(t,x)+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[X_{0}|X_{t}]-X_{0}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\leq 2\max\left(1,T^{-1}_{k}\right),

while by Theorem
4.4
, since
T_{k}\geq n^{-2}
with probability at least
1-n^{-4}
for a constant
C_{s}
that depends only on
C_{\log}
and
d

\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sigma^{-1}_{t}\cdot 20\sqrt{80d\left(C_{\log}+\log n\right)}\leq C_{s}\max\left(1,T^{-1/2}_{k}\right)\sqrt{\log n}.

•

The construction (
3.5
) of
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
mimics Tweedie’s representation of the score. Any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}
can be represented as
\phi(t,x)=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\phi_{e}(t,x)-\frac{x}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}
where
\left\lVert\phi_{e}(t,x)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1
. Moreover,
as we show in (
D.41
), for each fixed
y\in M
this holds with probability at least
1-n^{-4}
w.r.t.
Z_{D}
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert=\frac{c_{t}}{\sigma^{2}_{t}}\left\lVert\phi_{e}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-y\right\rVert\leq 2\cdot 244\sigma^{-1}_{t}\log n^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq 1000\max\left(1,T^{-1/2}_{k}\right)\log n^{3/2}.

•

Summarizing, with probability at least
1-2n^{-4}

\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-\phi(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert\leq(1000+C_{s})\max\left(1,T^{-1/2}_{k}\right)\log^{3/2}n.

This implies that for
\log n\gtrsim T_{k}\gtrsim n^{-1}

\displaystyle 8T_{k}\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{4}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-4}T_{k}^{2}\max\left(1,T^{-4}_{k}\right)+T_{k}\max\left(1,T^{-1}_{k}\right)\log^{3}n\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{2}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-2}+\log^{4}n\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{2}dt=n^{-2}+\log^{4}nR(\phi_{j},s).

and similarly

\displaystyle 16(T_{k+1}-T_{k})\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-2}+\log^{4}n\int_{T_{k}}^{T_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}_{y,Z_{D}}\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})-\phi_{j}(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})\right\rVert^{2}dt=n^{-2}+\log^{4}nR(\phi_{j},s).

Therefore, there is a constant
C_{g}
that depends only on
C_{\log}
and
d
such that

\Var_{x,y\sim\mu}g_{j}(x,y)\leq C_{g}\left(n^{-2}+\log^{4}n\cdot R(\phi_{j},s)\right).

Finally, we compute an a.s. bound on
g_{j}(x,y)
, combining (
D.42
) and (
4.4
)
there is a constant
C_{\sup}
that depends only on
C_{\log}
and
d

\left|g_{j}(x,y)\right|\leq 2\sup_{y\in M}\left|{\ell_{s}(y)}\right|+2\sup_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\sup_{y\in M}\left|\ell_{j}(y)\right|\lesssim\log^{3}n+\log^{2}n\leq C_{\sup}\log^{3}n.

Remark
.

This argument also works in the original setting
[
31
, Theorem C.4]
, in this case the inequalities have form

\displaystyle\left\lVert s(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert\lesssim 2\max\left(1,T^{-1/2}_{k}\right)\sqrt{D\log n},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi(t,c_{t}y+\sigma_{t}Z_{D})+\sigma^{-1}_{t}Z_{D}\right\rVert\lesssim 2\max\left(1,T^{-1/2}_{k}\right)\sqrt{D\log n}.

Under
\rP_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{X}}
the pairs
(x_{i},y_{i})
are independent with common law
\mu\otimes\mu
. Then, by Bernstein’s inequality, there is a positive constant
C_{b}<1/5
depending only on
d,C_{\log}

{\rP_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{X}}\left(\left|\sum_{i}g_{j}(x_{i},y_{i})\right|>t\right)}\leq 2\exp\left(-C_{b}\frac{t^{2}}{n^{-1}+n\log^{4}n\cdot R(\phi_{j},s)+t\log^{3}n}\right).

So, for
r_{j}=\max\left(\sqrt{n^{-1}\log\mathcal{N}_{n}},\sqrt{R(\phi_{j},s)}\right)

\displaystyle{\rP_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{X}}\left(\left|\frac{\sum_{i}g_{j}(x_{i},y_{i})}{r_{j}}\right|>t\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-C_{b}\frac{t^{2}}{r_{j}^{-2}n^{-1}+r_{j}^{-2}n\log^{4}n\cdot R(\phi_{j},s)+r_{j}^{-1}t\cdot\log^{3}n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-C_{b}\frac{t^{2}}{2n\log^{4}n+(t/r_{j})\cdot\log^{3}n}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-C_{b}\frac{t^{2}}{\log^{4}n\cdot\left(2n+t/r_{j}\right)}\right).

For
t\geq 2\log n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}
, since
r_{j}\geq\sqrt{n^{-1}\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}

we have

-t=-\frac{t}{2}-\frac{t}{2}\leq-\log n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}-\frac{t}{2}\frac{\sqrt{n^{-1}\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{r_{j}}=-\frac{1}{2}\log n\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}}\left(2n+t/r_{j}\right)

implying that

-\frac{t^{2}}{(2n+t/r_{j})\log^{4}n}\leq-\frac{t\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{2\sqrt{n}\log^{3}n}.

Therefore, if we introduce

T_{n}(j)={\frac{\sum_{i}g_{j}(x_{i},y_{i})}{r_{j}}},

substituting this bound we get that for
t\geq 2\log n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}

\displaystyle{\rP_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{X}}\left(\left|\sup_{j}T_{n}(j)\right|\geq t\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{N}_{n}\sup_{j}\exp\left(-C_{b}\frac{t^{2}}{2n\log^{4}n+(t/r_{j})\cdot\log^{3}n}\right)\leq 2\mathcal{N}_{n}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{b}}{2}\frac{t\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}\cdot\log^{3}n}\right).

We are now ready to bound the expectation. Taking
A=2C^{-1}_{b}\log^{3}n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}\geq 2\log n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}
for
n
large enough

\mathbb{E}\left|\sup_{j}T_{n}(j)\right|=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sup_{j}T_{n}(j)\right|\geq t\right)dt\leq A+\int_{A}^{\infty}2\mathcal{N}_{n}\exp\left(-\frac{C}{2}\frac{t\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}\cdot\log^{3}n}\right)dt\\
=A+2\mathcal{N}_{n}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{b}}{2}\frac{A\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}\cdot\log^{3}n}\right)\frac{2}{C_{b}}\frac{\sqrt{n}\cdot\log^{3}n}{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}\leq 4C^{-1}_{b}\log^{3}n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}.

Next we bound
\mathbb{E}|\sup_{j}T_{n}^{2}(j)|
using a similar approach. We have

\mathbb{E}\left|\sup_{j}T^{2}_{n}(j)\right|=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sup_{j}T_{n}(j)\right|\geq\sqrt{t}\right)dt\leq A+\int_{A}^{\infty}2\mathcal{N}_{n}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{b}}{2}\frac{\sqrt{t}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}\cdot\log^{3}n}\right)dt\\
=A+8\mathcal{N}_{n}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{b}}{2}\frac{\sqrt{A}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}\log^{3}n}\right)\frac{n(\log^{6}n)}{C_{b}^{2}\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}\left(\frac{C_{b}}{2}\frac{\sqrt{A}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}\log^{3}n}+1\right)

and choosing
A=4C^{-2}_{b}\left(n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}\right)\log^{6}n\geq\left(2\log n\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}\right)^{2}

we get

\mathbb{E}\left|\sup_{j}T^{2}_{n}(j)\right|\lesssim 4C^{-2}_{b}\left(n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}\right)\log^{6}n+8\frac{n(\log^{6}n)}{C_{b}^{2}\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}\left(\log\mathcal{N}_{n}+1\right)\leq 20C^{-2}_{b}n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}\log^{6}n.

We are finally, ready to bound
\Delta_{n}(j)-\Delta_{n}(s)
after noting that

r_{j}\leq\sqrt{(\log\mathcal{N}_{n})/n}+\sqrt{R(\phi_{j},s)}
.
Let
J=J(\mathcal{Y})
be a random index potentially depending on the sample
\mathcal{Y}
.
Then notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{n}(J)-\Delta_{n}(s)]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[r_{J}T_{n}(J)]\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{j}|T_{n}(j)|]}{n}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\sqrt{R(\phi_{J},s)}\max_{j}|T_{n}(j)|\right)}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}}{n^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[\max_{j}|T_{n}(j)|]+\frac{1}{n}\left[\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{J},s)\right]^{1/2}\left[\mathbb{E}\max_{j}|T_{n}(j)|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 5C^{-1}_{b}\frac{\sqrt{(\log\mathcal{N}_{n})/n}}{n}\sqrt{n\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}\log^{3}n+\left[\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{J},s)\right]^{1/2}5C^{-1}_{b}\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}}\log^{3}n.

\displaystyle\leq 5C^{-1}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{3}n+5C^{-1}_{b}\left[\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{J},s)\right]^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}}\log^{3}n

and using Young’s inequality

\displaystyle\leq 5C^{-1}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{3}n+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{J},s)+\frac{25C^{-2}_{b}}{2}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{J},s)+50C^{-2}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n.

Substituting
J=\hat{j}
and recalling that by construction
\left\lVert\ell_{\phi_{\hat{j}}}-\ell_{\hat{\phi}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{n}(\hat{j})-\Delta_{n}(s)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{\hat{j}},s)+50C^{-2}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}R(\hat{\phi},s)+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n}+50C^{-2}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n.

While substituting
J=j^{*}
and recalling that
\left\lVert\ell_{\phi_{j^{*}}}-\ell_{\phi^{*}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\delta_{n}
and
\phi^{*}=\argmin_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}R(\phi,s)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{n}(j^{*})-\Delta_{n}(s)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}R(\phi_{j^{*}},s)+50C^{-2}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\min_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}R(\phi,s)+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{n}+50C^{-2}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n.

Finally, the above combined with (
D.47
) gives us the desired bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}R(\hat{\phi},s)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}R(\phi,s)-\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{n}(j^{*})-\Delta_{n}(s)]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{n}(\hat{j})-\Delta_{n}(s)]+5\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}R(\hat{\phi},s)+\frac{3}{2}\min_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}R(\phi,s)+6\delta_{n}+50C^{-2}_{b}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}_{n}}{n}\log^{6}n.

∎

Appendix E
Proofs for Section 6

This appendix contains the details of the proofs of Proposition 6.2, Theorem 6.1 and Corollary
3.2

omitted in Section 6.Recall that in this part
L
denotes the number of splits in the discretization and not the number of layers in the neural network.

Proof of Proposition 6.2.

(i)

We need to show that

\tau_{(i)}-\tau_{(i+1)}\leq\kappa\min(1,\tau_{(i+1)}),\qquad 1\leq j\leq M.

If
\tau_{(i)}
and
\tau_{(i+1)}
belong to the same block

[T_{K-k},T_{K-k+1}]
, then

\tau_{(i)}-\tau_{(i+1)}=\Delta_{k}=\frac{\kappa}{4}\min(1,T_{k+1})\leq\kappa\min(1,\tau_{(i+1)}).

Otherwise, for some
k\leq K
we have that
\tau_{(i)}=T_{k}-(L_{k}-1)\Delta_{k}-\Delta_{k}u_{k}
is the last point of a subpartition of
[T_{k+1},T_{k}]
, while
\tau_{(i+1)}=T_{k+1}-\Delta_{k+1}u_{k+1}
is the first element of subpartition of the next block
[T_{k+2},T_{k+1}]
. So, by the definition of
L_{k}

\left\lVert\tau_{(i)}-\tau_{(i+1)}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\tau_{(i)}-T_{k+1}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\tau_{(i+1)}-T_{k+1}\right\rVert\leq\Delta_{k}+\Delta_{k+1}\\
=(\kappa/4)\left(\min(1,T_{k+1})+\min(1,T_{k+2})\right)\leq(\kappa/4)\min(1+1,T_{k+1}+T_{k+2})\\
=(\kappa/4)\min(2,3T_{k+2})\leq\kappa\min(1,T_{k+2})\leq\kappa\min(1,\tau_{i+1}).

(ii)

We need to show

\tau_{(1)}>\overline{T}-\kappa,\quad\tau_{(L)}<(1+\kappa)T,\quad\tau_{i_{k}}/\tau_{i_{k+1}}<4;

First inequality: since
T_{0}:=\overline{T}

\overline{T}-\tau_{(1)}=u_{0}\Delta_{0}\leq\Delta_{0}=(\kappa/4)\min(1,\overline{T})\leq\kappa.

Second inequality: Since
T_{K+1}:=\underline{T}

\tau_{(L)}-\underline{T}\leq\Delta_{K}\leq(\kappa/4)\min(1,T_{K+1})\leq\kappa\underline{T}.

Third inequality: by the definition of
i_{k}
we have
\tau_{i_{k}}\in[T_{k+1},T_{k+1}+\Delta_{k}]
, so using that
\kappa<1

\tau_{i_{k}}\leq T_{k+1}+\Delta_{k}=2T_{k+2}+(\kappa/4)\min(1,2T_{k+2})\leq 4T_{k+2}\leq 4\tau_{i_{k+1}}.

(iii)

We need to show

L\leq(K+1)+\frac{4}{\kappa}\left(\overline{T}+\left\lceil\log_{2}\underline{T}^{-1}\right\rceil+1\right)=O\left(\kappa^{-1}(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})\right)

Consider an interval
[T_{k+1},T_{k}]
. Number of points on the interval
[T_{k},T_{k+1}]
is
L_{k}
and satisfy

T_{k}-\Delta_{k}u_{k}-(L_{k}-1)\Delta_{k}\geq T_{k+1},

so

L_{k}\leq\frac{T_{k+1}-\Delta_{k}u_{k}+\Delta_{k}}{\Delta_{k}}\leq 1+\frac{4}{\kappa}\frac{T_{k+1}}{\min(1,T_{k+1})}.

Let
k_{1}
be the smallest index satisfying
T_{k_{1}}>1
.
Then

L=\sum_{k=1}^{K}L_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{k_{1}}L_{k}+\sum_{k=k_{1}+1}^{K}L_{k}\leq K+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{1}}\frac{4}{\kappa}T_{k+1}+\sum_{k=k_{1}+1}^{K}\frac{4}{\kappa}=K+\frac{4}{\kappa}\left(K-k_{1}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{1}}T_{k+1}\right),

since
T_{k}/T_{k+1}=2
and
T_{k_{1}+1}\leq 1
we have
K-k_{1}\leq\lceil\log_{2}T^{-1}_{K+1}\rceil=\lceil\log_{2}\underline{T}^{-1}\rceil
, at the same time
\sum_{k=1}^{k_{1}}T_{k+1}=\sum 2^{-(k+1)}\overline{T}\leq\overline{T}
. Substituting we conclude

L\leq K+\frac{4}{\kappa}\left(\lceil\log_{2}\underline{T}^{-1}\rceil+\overline{T}\right).

Noting that
K=\log_{2}(\overline{T}-\underline{T})
we conclude

L=O\left(\kappa^{-1}(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})\right)

(iv)

Let
g(t)=\sigma^{2}_{i}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\hat{s}(t,X_{t})-s(t,X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

Then, recalling that
\gamma_{i}=\tau_{(i)}-\tau_{(i+1)}
we need to show that

\mathbb{E}_{\tau}\sum_{i=1}^{M-1}\gamma_{i}g(\tau_{(i)})\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}g(t)\,dt.

If
\tau_{(i+1)}=\tau_{k,\ell}
for
\ell>1
then
\gamma_{i}=\Delta_{k}
, while if
\tau_{(i+1)}=\tau_{k,1}
then
{\gamma_{i}\leq\Delta_{k}+\Delta_{k-1}\leq 2\Delta_{k-1}}
, so

\sum\gamma_{i}g(\tau_{(i)})\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\Delta_{k}g(\tau_{k,\ell})=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\Delta_{k}g(T_{k}-u_{k}\Delta_{k}-(\ell-1)\Delta_{k})

Taking expectation with respect to the shifts and using Tonelli’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u_{k}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\Delta_{k}g(\tau_{k,\ell})=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\int_{0}^{1}\Delta_{k}g(T_{k}-u_{k}\Delta_{k}-(\ell-1)\Delta_{k})\mathbbm{1}\{T_{k}-u_{k}\Delta_{k}-(\ell-1)\Delta_{k}\geq T_{k+1}\}\,du

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\int_{(\ell-1)\Delta_{k}}^{\ell\Delta_{k}}g(T_{k}+s)\mathbbm{1}\{T_{k}-s\geq T_{k+1}\}\,ds=\int_{0}^{T_{k}-T_{k+1}}g(T_{k}-s)\,ds=\int_{T_{k+1}}^{T_{k}}g(t)\,dt.

By summing over
k
we get the desired result.

∎

E.1
Added details in the proof of Theorem 6.1

Initialization Error.
We note that the processes
\bar{Y}
and
\bar{Y}^{(1)}
differ only due to their initialization, so by applying
[
7
, Proposition 9]
, Pinsker’s and the data-processing inequalities

W_{1}\left(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}\right)\leq 2C_{1}\TV\left(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}\right)\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{\KL\left(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}}\,||\,\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}\right)}\\
\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{\KL\left(\bar{Y}_{0}\,||\,\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{0}\right)}\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{\KL(p_{\tau_{(1)}}\,||\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id\right))}\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{D}e^{-\tau_{(1)}}\leq,

where we used that
\bar{Y}_{t_{L+1}}=\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},\bar{Y}_{t_{1}:t_{L}})
,
\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{t_{L+1}}=\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{t_{1}:t_{L}})
and
\left\lVert\bar{e}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{1}
for all
t
.

Since
\tau_{(1)}\geq\overline{T}-1
a.s. in
u

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}\left(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}\right)\lesssim\sqrt{D}e^{-\overline{T}}.

(E.1)

Error in the last step.
Next we bound
W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(K+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}},X_{0})
. We note that
\bar{Y}^{(K+1)}_{t_{L+1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle dist.}}{{=}}X_{\tau_{(L)}}
as it follows the backward dynamic (
2.4
) on the interval
[0,\tau_{(1)}-\tau(L)]
and the difference is only in the last step. In other words, while the true process sends
X_{\tau_{(L)}}\rightarrow X_{0}
, the discretized one sends
X_{\tau_{(L)}}\rightarrow\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},X_{\tau_{(L)}})=\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}}^{(K+1)}
.

Using the fact that with probability at least
1-\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{L^{2}}
we have that
{\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},X_{\tau_{(L)}})=\hat{e}(\tau_{(L)},X_{\tau_{(L)}})}
and
\left\lVert\hat{e}(\tau_{(L)},X_{\tau_{(L)}})-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq R_{\tau_{(L)}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{L^{2}}\right)
, we conclude that with probability at least
1-2\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{M^{2}}

\left\lVert\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},X_{\tau_{(L)}})-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq R_{\tau_{(L)}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right).

Finally, using that
\left\lVert\bar{e}\right\rVert\leq C_{1}
almost surely we bound

\displaystyle W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(K+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}},X_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left\lVert X_{0}-\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},X_{\tau_{(L)}})\right\rVert\leq R_{\tau_{(L)}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{L^{2}}\right)+2C_{1}\underline{T}\delta/L^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\sigma_{\tau_{(L)}}/c_{\tau_{(L)}})\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log\frac{2L^{2}}{\underline{T}\delta}}+2\frac{C_{1}\underline{T}\delta}{L^{2}}.

Since
\tau_{(L)}\leq 2\underline{T}
a.s. in
u

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(K+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}},X_{0})\leq\sqrt{\underline{T}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log\frac{2L^{2}}{\underline{T}\delta}}+2\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{L^{2}}

(E.2)

Discretization Error

Finally, we bound
W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}})
.

By taking the same Brownian motion on the interval
[0,t_{i_{k}}]
, by definition (6.12) the processes
\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t}
and
\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t}
coincide on this interval and in particular
\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{i_{k}}}=\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t_{i_{k}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle dist.}}{{=}}X_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}
.

Moreover, by construction (see definition (6.12))
\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}}=\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{1}:t_{L}})
and
\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}=\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t_{1}:t_{L}})
, so

\left\lVert\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}}-\bar{e}(\tau_{(i_{k})},\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{1}:t_{L}})-\bar{e}(\tau_{(i_{k})},\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert\leq 2R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right)\\
\left\lVert\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}-\bar{e}(\tau_{(i_{k})},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t_{1}:t_{L}})-\bar{e}(\tau_{(i_{k})},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert\leq 2R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right)

Therefore the conditional distributions of
\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}}
and
\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}
given
Y_{[0,t_{i_{k}}]}
have a support with diameter bounded by
2R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right)
.

Then writing
W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}|Y_{[0,t_{i_{k}}]})
to denote the Wasserstein distance between the conditional distributions of
\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}}
and
\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}
given the past trajectory
Y_{[0,t_{i_{k}}]}
, we have

\displaystyle W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}|Y_{[0,t_{i_{k}}]})\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\TV\left(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(1)}}\big|Y_{[0,t_{i_{k}}]}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2L^{2}}\right)\TV\left(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{[0,t_{L}]},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{[0,t_{L}]}\right)\right),

where we have used that
W_{1}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\leq diam(\mathcal{X})\TV\left(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right)
for any distributions
\mu_{j},j\in\{1,2\}
supported on
\mathcal{X}
, the fact that
for random variables
X,Y,Z
we have

\mathbb{E}\TV(X,Y|Z)=\mathbb{E}\TV((X,Z),(Y,Z)),

as well as the fact that
y_{t_{1}:t_{L}}\rightarrow\bar{e}(\tau(L),y_{t_{1}:t_{L}})
is measurable.

Next, we bound the
\TV
distance using first Pinsker’s inequality and then Girsanov’s theorem on the interval
[0,\tau_{(L)}]
. By construction of the processes
\bar{Y}^{(k)}
we obtain,

\displaystyle\TV^{2}\left(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{[0,t_{L}]},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{[0,t_{L}]}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\KL\left(\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{[0,t_{L}]}\,||\,\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{[0,t_{L}]}\right)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(Girsanov)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{u}\int_{t_{i_{k}}}^{t_{i_{k+1}}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s(\tau_{(1)}-t,Y_{t})-\bar{s}(\tau_{(1)}-t,Y_{t}|\tau_{(i_{k})},Y_{t_{1}:t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert^{2}\,dt.

Note that, unlike
[
31
]
, we do not have to introduce a truncated auxiliary process to ensure (Girsanov), since Novikov’s condition holds a.s. by construction of the process.

Finally, using
[
33
, Theorem 10]
, for all
u_{1},\cdots,u_{K}
, since expectation is with respect to
P_{t}

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i_{k}}}^{t_{i_{k+1}}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s(\tau_{(1)}-t,Y_{t})-\bar{s}(\tau_{(1)}-t,Y_{t}|\tau_{(i_{k})},Y_{t_{1}:t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i=i_{k}}^{i_{k+1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s(\tau_{(1)}-t,Y_{t})-\bar{s}(\tau_{(1)}-t,Y_{t}|\tau_{(i_{k})},Y_{t_{1}:t_{i_{k}}})\right\rVert^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{i=i_{k}}^{i_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert s(\tau_{(i)},Y_{t_{i}})-\bar{s}(\tau_{(i)},Y_{t_{i}})\right\rVert^{2}+\kappa+d\kappa^{2}L(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sigma^{-2}_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}(\varepsilon^{2}_{denoiser}(u)+\delta)+\kappa d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})

where to get the last inequality we used that
\tau_{(i)}/\tau_{(i_{k+1})}<4
for all
i\geq i_{k}
, the bound (6.3) in Proposition 6.2 as well as (6.9).

Substituting we get

W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(M)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(M)}})\leq 4R_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}\left(\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{2M^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\sigma^{-2}_{\tau_{(i_{k})}}(\varepsilon^{2}_{denoiser}(u)+\delta)+\kappa d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})}\\
\lesssim C_{\mathrm{tail}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log\frac{2M^{2}}{\underline{T}\delta}}\sqrt{\varepsilon^{2}_{denoiser}(u)+\delta+\kappa d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})},

where to get the last inequality we used (6.5) and that
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
for all
t
.

By taking expectation in
u
and applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(M)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(M)}})\lesssim\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log\frac{2L^{2}}{\underline{T}\delta}}\sqrt{\varepsilon^{2}_{denoiser}+\delta+\kappa d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})}

(E.3)

Combining the terms.

Overall combining (
E.1
), (
E.2
) and (
E.3
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}

\displaystyle(Y_{\tau_{(L)}},\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(L)}})\leq\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}({Y}_{\tau_{(L)}},\bar{Y}^{(K+1)}_{\tau_{(L)}})+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}(\bar{Y}^{(k)}_{\tau_{(L)}},\bar{Y}^{(k+1)}_{\tau_{(L)}})+\mathbb{E}_{u}W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(L)}},\bar{Y}^{(1)}_{\tau_{(L)}})

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim+\sqrt{D}e^{-\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\underline{T}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log\frac{2L^{2}}{\underline{T}\delta}}+2\frac{\underline{T}\delta}{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+K\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log\frac{2L^{2}}{\underline{T}\delta}}\sqrt{\varepsilon^{2}_{denoiser}+\delta+\kappa d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})}

Now we do the last substitutions, we note that
K=O(\log(\overline{T}-\underline{T}))
and
L=O\left(\kappa^{-1}(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})\right)
, so substituting
\kappa=\delta=\varepsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{denoiser}}
we get the desired bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{u}

\displaystyle W_{1}(Y_{\tau_{(1)}},\bar{Y}_{\tau_{(1)}})\lesssim\sqrt{D}e^{-\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\underline{T}}\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(\varepsilon^{-1}_{\mathrm{denoiser}}\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\overline{T})}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}\log(\overline{T}\cdot\underline{T}^{-1})\log\underline{T}^{-1}\sqrt{C_{W}+\log(\varepsilon^{-1}_{\mathrm{denoiser}}\cdot\underline{T}^{-1}\cdot\overline{T})}\sqrt{d(\log\underline{T}^{-1}+C_{\log})(\log{\underline{T}^{-1}}+\overline{T})}.

E.2
Proof of Corollary
3.2

Conditioned on event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}
that depends only on
\mathcal{Y}
, Theorem
3.1
gives Assumption
6
with
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}=O\left(n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\polylog n\right)
,
C_{W}\asymp\log n
and
\eta=O\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{denoiser}}^{-4}\underline{T}^{-1}(\log\underline{T}+\overline{T}\right)=O\left(n^{\frac{6(\alpha+1)}{2\alpha+d}}\polylog n\right)
. Therefore, applying Theorem
6.1
we get that given
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}

W_{1}(\mu,\hat{\mu})\lesssim n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\polylog n.

When event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}
does not hold, which happens with probability at most
O\left(\polylog n\cdot n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\right)
, we note that the last step of the sampling scheme has form

y_{t_{L}}\mapsto\bar{e}(\tau_{(L)},y_{1:t_{L}})=\hat{e}(\tau_{(i)},y_{i})\quad\text{for some }i\leq L\text{ by~(6.9)}.

By construction, see (
3.5
), we have
\dist\left(M,\hat{e}(\tau_{(i)},y_{i})\right)\leq C_{2}
, therefore for all
y\in M

\left\lVert y-\hat{e}(\tau_{(i)},y_{i})\right\rVert\leq C_{2}+\diam M=O(1).

So a.s.
W_{1}(\mu,\hat{\mu})=O(1)
, and as result, since
\rP(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k})=1-O\left(\polylog n\cdot n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{Y}\sim\mu^{\otimes n}}W_{1}(\mu,\hat{\mu})

\displaystyle=\rP(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k})\cdot O\left(\polylog n\cdot n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\right)+(1-\rP(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{geom},k}))\cdot O(1)

\displaystyle=O\left(\polylog n\cdot n^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\alpha+d}}\right).

That finishes the proof of Corollary
3.2
.

Appendix F
Auxiliary Results on Neural Networks

Below we list the results on the neural networks from
[
31
]
that were used to build the approximation in the Euclidean case.

Lemma F.1

(
[
31
]
, Lemma F.1, Concatenation of neural networks.)
.

For any neural networks

\phi^{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\phi^{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}},\dots,\phi^{k}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d_{k+1}}

with
\phi^{i}\in\Psi(L^{i},W^{i},S^{i},B^{i})

(i=1,2,\dots,d)
, there exists a neural network
\phi\in\Phi(L,W,S,B)
satisfying
\phi(x)=\phi^{k}\circ\phi^{k-1}\circ\dots\circ\phi^{1}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
, with

L=\sum_{i=1}^{k}L^{i},\quad W\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{k}W^{i},\quad S\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}S^{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(\|A^{i}_{L_{i}}\|_{0}+\|b^{i}_{L_{i}}\|_{0}+\|A^{i+1}_{1}\|_{0})\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{k}S^{i},

and
B\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq k}B^{i}
.

Here
A_{j}^{i}
is the parameter matrix and
b_{j}^{i}
is the bias vector at the
j
th layer of the
i
th neural network
\phi^{i}
.

Lemma F.2

(
[
31
]
, Lemma F.3, Parallelization of neural networks.)
.

For any neural networks
\phi^{1},\phi^{2},\dots,\phi^{k}
with
\phi^{i}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}_{i}}
and

\phi^{i}\in\Psi(L^{i},W^{i},S^{i},B^{i})\quad(i=1,2,\dots,d),

there exists a neural network
\phi\in\Psi(L,W,S,B)
satisfying

\phi(x)=\left[\phi^{1}(x_{1})^{\top}\ \phi^{2}(x_{2})^{\top}\ \cdots\ \phi^{k}(x_{k})^{\top}\right]^{\top}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}+d_{2}+\dots+d_{k}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}_{1}+d^{\prime}_{2}+\dots+d^{\prime}_{k}}

for all
x=(x_{1}^{\top}\ x_{2}^{\top}\ \cdots\ x_{k}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}+d_{2}+\dots+d_{k}}
(here
x_{i}
can be shared), with

L=L_{i},\quad\|W\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|W^{i}\|_{\infty},\quad S\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}S^{i},\quad\text{and}\quad B\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq k}B^{i}\quad(\text{when }L=L_{i}\text{ holds for all }i),

L=\max_{1\leq i\leq k}L^{i},\quad\|W\|_{\infty}\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|W^{i}\|_{\infty},\quad S\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{k}(S^{i}+LW^{i}_{L}),\quad\text{and}\quad B\leq\max\{\max_{1\leq i\leq k}B^{i},1\}\quad(\text{otherwise}).

Moreover, there exists a network
\phi_{\operatorname{sum}}(x)\in\Phi(L,W,S,B)
that realizes
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\phi^{i}(x)
, with

L=\max_{1\leq i\leq k}L^{i}+1,\quad\|W\|_{\infty}\leq 4\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|W^{i}\|_{\infty},\quad S\leq 4\sum_{i=1}^{k}(S^{i}+LW^{i}_{L})+2WL,\quad\text{and}\quad B\leq\max\{\max_{1\leq i\leq k}B^{i},1\}.

Lemma F.3

(
[
31
]
, Lemma F.6, Approximation of monomials)
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
C\geq 1
,
0<\epsilon_{\text{error}}\leq 1
. For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a neural network
\phi_{\operatorname{mult}}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{d})\in\Psi(L,W,S,B)
with

L=\mathcal{O}(\log d(\log\epsilon^{-1}+d\log C)),\quad\|W\|_{\infty}=48d,\quad S=\mathcal{O}(d\log\epsilon^{-1}+d\log C),\quad B=C^{d}

such that

\left|\phi_{\text{mult}}(x^{1},x^{2},\dots,x^{d})-\prod_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}x_{d^{\prime}}\right|\leq\epsilon+dC^{d-1}\epsilon_{\text{error}},\quad\text{for all }x\in[-C,C]^{d}\text{ and }x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\text{ with }\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{\text{error}}.

Lemma F.4

(

[
31
]
, Lemma F.7, Approximating the reciprocal function)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<1
, there exists
\phi_{\text{rec}}\in\Psi(L,W,S,B)
with
L\leq O(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1})
,
\|W\|_{\infty}=O(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1})
,
S=O(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1})
, and
B=O(\epsilon^{-2})
such that

\left|\phi_{\text{rec}}(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{x}\right|\leq\epsilon+\frac{|x^{\prime}-x|}{\epsilon^{2}},\quad\text{for all }x\in[\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}]\text{ and }x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}.

Lemma F.5

(
[
31
]
, Section B.1)
.

Constants
c_{t}
, and
\sigma_{t}
with an error less than
\varepsilon
can be computed by neural networks
\phi_{c}(t),\phi_{\sigma}(t)
of
\polylog\varepsilon^{-1}
size.

Proposition F.6
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\in R
, then there are neural networks
\phi_{\max},\phi_{\min}\in\Psi(L,B,S,W)
where
L=O\left(\log n\right)
,
\left\lVert W\right\rVert_{\infty},S=O(n)
and
B=O(1)
such that

\displaystyle\phi_{\max}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

\displaystyle=\max\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right),

\displaystyle\phi_{\min}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})

\displaystyle=\min\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right).

Proof.

Since
\min\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)=-\max\left(-x_{1},\ldots,-x_{n}\right)

it is enough to build
\phi_{\max}
.
Let
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\max\left(a,b\right)=a+\max\left(0,b-a\right)=\relu\left(a\right)+\relu\left(-a\right)+\relu\left(a-b\right).

So,
\max
can be implemented as
1
-layer network of constant size. Representing

\max\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)=\max\left(\max\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n/2}\right),\max\left(x_{n/2+1},\ldots,x_{n}\right)\right)

we get the required statement by induction.
∎

Appendix G
Bounds on Tangent Spaces

G.1
Projection on Tangent Space as a Denoiser

In this section, we prove the auxiliary results used to build approximation for
T_{k}<n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha+d}}
.

Proposition G.1
.

Work on the geometric event where the local chart approximation bounds
from Section
5.1
hold. Let

X_{t}=c_{t}X_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}
and, for a fixed index
i
, assume

X_{0}\in\Phi_{i}(B_{d}(0,5\varepsilon_{N}))
. Define

z_{i}^{*}(X_{t}):=c_{t}^{-1}(P_{i}^{*})^{T}(X_{t}-c_{t}G_{i}),\qquad m_{i}(X_{t}):=\Phi_{i}(z_{i}^{*}(X_{t})).

Then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\rP_{Z_{D}}\left(\left\lVert m_{i}(X_{t})-X_{0}\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

Consequently, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the same bound
holds simultaneously for all
i\leq N
after replacing the square root
by
\sqrt{d+\log n+2\log\delta^{-1}}
. In the small-time regime
used in Appendix
D
,
this displacement is
o(\varepsilon_{N})
, so for
n
large enough

z_{i}^{*}(X_{t})
remains in the local chart domain.

Proof.

Fix
i
and write
X_{0}=\Phi_{i}(z_{0})
with

z_{0}\in B_{d}(0,5\varepsilon_{N})
. Since

\Phi_{i}^{*}(z)-G_{i}-P_{i}^{*}z\perp\operatorname{Im}P_{i}^{*}
and
P_{i}^{*}
is an
isometric embedding,

(P_{i}^{*})^{T}(c_{t}\Phi_{i}(z_{0})+\sigma_{t}Z_{D}-c_{t}G_{i})=c_{t}(P_{i}^{*})^{T}(\Phi_{i}(z_{0})-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{0}))+c_{t}z_{0}+\sigma_{t}(P_{i}^{*})^{T}Z_{D}.

The chart approximation gives

\left\lVert\Phi_{i}(z_{0})-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{0})\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}
.
Therefore

\left\lVert z_{i}^{*}(X_{t})-z_{0}\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\left\lVert(P_{i}^{*})^{T}Z_{D}\right\rVert.

Since
(P_{i}^{*})^{T}Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Id_{d})
, the standard Gaussian norm
tail bound implies

\left\lVert z_{i}^{*}(X_{t})-z_{0}\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d+2\log\delta^{-1}}

with probability at least
1-\delta
. The map
\Phi_{i}
is

2
-Lipschitz on the relevant ball, so

\left\lVert m_{i}(X_{t})-\Phi_{i}(z_{0})\right\rVert\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{\beta}+2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d+2\log\delta^{-1}}.

Since
\Phi_{i}(z_{0})=X_{0}
, this proves the fixed-index claim on the
event where
z_{i}^{*}(X_{t})
is in the local chart domain. The final
sentence of the proposition verifies this condition in the
small-time application.
Applying the same argument with
\delta/N
and using
N\leq n

gives the simultaneous version.
∎

G.2
Correlation Between Tangent Vectors and Gaussian Noise

The following lemma is the second order counterpart of
proposition

B.1
.

Lemma G.2
.

Let
f(z):B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{D}
satisfy
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{\beta}\left(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\right)}\leq L
for
\beta\geq 2
and let
Z_{D}\simeq\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id_{D}\right)
. Let
h(z_{1},z_{2})=f(z_{1})-f(z_{2})-\nabla f(z_{2})(z_{1}-z_{2})
– an error at
z_{1}
of the linear approximation of
f
at point
z_{2}
, then

\rP\left(\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},h(z_{1},z_{2})\right\rangle\right|\leq L\varepsilon^{2}\left(4\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log\delta}\right)\right)\geq 1-\delta,

Proof.

The function
h(z_{1},z_{2}):B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\times B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\subset B_{2d}(0,2\varepsilon)\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{D}
. Since
h(0,0)=0
to apply
proposition

B.1
it is enough to estimate the Lipschitz constant of
h(z_{1},z_{2})
.

First, let us prove an auxiliary bound on
\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{\infty}
. Since
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{\beta}\left(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\right)}\leq L
integrating along the segment connecting
z_{1}
and
z_{2}

h(z_{1},z_{2})=f(z_{1})-f(z_{2})-\nabla f(z_{2})(z_{1}-z_{2})=\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla f(az_{1}+(1-a)z_{2})-\nabla f(z_{2}),z_{1}-z_{2}\right\rangle da,

note
B(0,\varepsilon)
is a convex set, so

\left\lVert h(z_{1},z_{2})\right\rVert\leq\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\nabla f(az_{1}+(1-a)z_{2})-\nabla f(z_{2})\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert z_{1}-z_{2}\right\rVert\leq L\varepsilon^{2}.

(G.1)

To bound the Lipschitz constant we introduce another pair of points
z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2}\in B(0,\varepsilon)
and estimate

\left\lVert h(z_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})\right\rVert=\left\lVert h(z_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})+h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})\right\rVert\\
\leq\left\lVert h(z_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})\right\rVert+\left\lVert h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})\right\rVert.

We deal with each term separately. We represent the first term as

\displaystyle h(z_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=f(z_{1})-f(z^{\prime}_{1})-\nabla f(z_{2})(z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla f\left(az_{1}+(1-a)z^{\prime}_{1}\right)-\nabla f(z_{2}),z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}\right\rangle da,

so

\left\lVert h(z_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert\int_{0}^{1}\left\lVert\nabla f\left(az_{1}+(1-a)z^{\prime}_{1}\right)-\nabla f(z_{2})\right\rVert_{op}da\leq L\varepsilon\left\lVert z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert.

(G.2)

The second term
h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})
is represented in a similar way

h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})=\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla_{z_{a}}h\left(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{a}\right),z_{2}-z^{\prime}_{2}\right\rangle da,

where
z_{a}=az_{2}+(1-a)z^{\prime}_{2}
. The gradient can be computed directly

\nabla_{z_{2}}h(z_{1},z_{2})=\nabla_{z_{2}}\left(f(z_{2})-\nabla_{z_{2}}f(z_{2})(z_{2}-z_{1})\right)=-\left(\nabla_{z_{2}}^{2}f(z_{2})\right)(z_{2}-z_{1}),

so we get a bound

\left\lVert h(z^{\prime}_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert z_{2}-z^{\prime}_{2}\right\rVert\int_{0}^{1}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}f(z_{a})\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert z_{a}-z^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert da\leq L\varepsilon\left\lVert z_{2}-z^{\prime}_{2}\right\rVert.

(G.3)

Summing (
G.2
) and (
G.3
) up we conclude

\left\lVert h(z_{1},z_{2})-h(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})\right\rVert\leq L\varepsilon\left(\left\lVert z_{2}-z_{2}^{\prime}\right\rVert+\left\lVert z_{1}-z^{\prime}_{1}\right\rVert\right)\leq L\sqrt{2}\left\lVert(z_{1},z_{2})-(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2})\right\rVert.

Applying
proposition

B.1
we derive the lemma.
∎

Corollary G.3
.

Let
\Phi_{1}^{*},\ldots,\Phi^{*}_{N}:B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{D}
be
L^{*}
-lipschitz functions then for any positive
\delta<1
with probability at least
1-\delta
for all
i\leq N

\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{1})-\Phi_{i}^{*}(z_{2})-\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z_{1})(z_{1}-z_{2})\right\rangle\right|\leq 2L^{*}\varepsilon^{2}\left(4\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log N+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right).

Proof.

Apply
lemma

G.2
for each
\Phi^{*}_{i}
with
\delta/N
and combine the inequalities.
∎

The next lemma is a local bound of the projection on the tangent space of
Z_{D}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Id\right)
.

Lemma G.4
.

For function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{D}
s.t.
f(0)=0
and
\nabla^{T}f(0)\nabla f(0)=\Id_{d}
and point
z
we define

\pr_{z}=\nabla f(z)\left(\nabla f^{T}(z)\nabla f(z)\right)^{-1}\nabla^{T}f(z),

the projection map onto tangent to the image of
f
at
f(z)
. Let
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{\beta}(B(0,\varepsilon)}\leq L
, where
\beta\geq 3
then for any
\varepsilon<L^{-1}/8

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lVert\pr_{z}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 4dL\varepsilon+4\sqrt{d}(1+L\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log 4d+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta

Proof.

We note that (ii) and (iii) in
proposition

A.1
holds for any
f
satisfying
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{\beta}(B(0,\varepsilon)}\leq L
and
\nabla^{T}f(0)\nabla f(0)=\Id_{d}
, so we have: (i)
\left\lVert\nabla f(z)\right\rVert_{op}<2
; (ii)
\left\lVert\nabla^{T}f(z)\nabla f(z)-\Id_{d}\right\rVert_{op}\leq 1/2
. The latter implies
\left\lVert\left(\nabla^{T}f(z)\nabla f(z)\right)^{-1}\right\rVert_{op}\leq 2
and we conclude

\displaystyle\left\lVert\pr_{z}Z_{D}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\nabla f(z)\left(\nabla f^{T}(z)\nabla f(z)\right)^{-1}\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\nabla f(z)\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 4\left\lVert\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\right\rVert.

So, it is enough to prove that

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lVert\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq dL\varepsilon+\sqrt{d}(1+L\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log 2d+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

The vector
\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and we bound it coordinate-wise. Let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{d}
be an orthonormal basis in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
f_{v}=\frac{d}{dv}f(z)
then
\nabla^{T}f(z)=\left(f^{T}_{v_{1}}(z),\ldots,f^{T}_{v_{d}}(z)\right)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times d}
.
For
z,z^{\prime}\in B(0,\varepsilon)
using that
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{C^{\beta}(0,\varepsilon)}\leq L
we bound

\left\lVert f_{v_{i}}(z)-f_{v_{i}}(z^{\prime})\right\rVert\leq L\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert.

Applying
proposition

B.1
we get

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f_{v_{i}}(z)-f_{v_{i}}(0)\right\rangle\right|\leq L\varepsilon\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2\log(4d\delta^{-1})}\right)\right)\geq 1-\delta/2d.

Since
\nabla^{T}f(0)\nabla f(0)=\Id_{d}
, we note that
\left\lVert f_{v_{i}}(0)\right\rVert=1
. So
\left\langle Z_{D},v_{i}\right\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,1\right)
and

\rP\left(\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f_{v_{i}}(0)\right\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{2\log(4d\delta^{-1})}\right)\geq 1-\delta/2d.

Summing up

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f_{v_{i}}(z)\right\rangle\right|\leq L\varepsilon\sqrt{d}+(1+L\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log(4d\delta^{-1})}\right)\geq 1-\delta/d.

Combining inequalities for all
v_{i}

\rP\left(\forall i:\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f_{v_{i}}(z)\right\rangle\right|\leq L\varepsilon\sqrt{d}+(1+L\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log 4d+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

Finally the inequality

\left\lVert\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\right\rVert^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f_{v_{i}}(z)\right\rangle\right|^{2}\leq d\cdot\sup_{i}\left|\left\langle Z_{D},f_{v_{i}}(z)\right\rangle\right|^{2}

gives

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lVert\nabla^{T}f(z)Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq dL\varepsilon+\sqrt{d}(1+L\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log 4d+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

∎

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Let us take
\varepsilon<r_{0}
-dense set
\mathcal{G}=\{G_{1},\ldots,G_{N}\}
by Proposition
2.1
we can guarantee
N\leq(\varepsilon/2)^{-d}\Vol M
.
Applying Lemma
G.4
with
\delta/N
and
f(z)=\Phi_{G_{i}}(z)

\rP\left(\sup_{z\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\left\lVert\pr_{z}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 4dL_{M}\varepsilon+4\sqrt{d}(1+L_{M}\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log 2d+2\log N+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta/N.

We note that since locally
M
is a graph of
\Phi_{G_{i}}
we have
\pr_{z}\equiv\pr_{\Phi_{G_{i}}(z)}M
, so combining over all
i\leq N
, since
M=\bigcup_{i}\Phi_{G_{i}}\left(B_{d}(0,\varepsilon)\right)
we conclude

\rP\left(\sup_{y\in M}\left\lVert\pr_{T_{y}M}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 4dL_{M}\varepsilon+4\sqrt{d}(1+L_{M}\varepsilon)\sqrt{2\log 2d+2\log\varepsilon^{-1}+2\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\\
\geq 1-\delta.

Taking
\varepsilon=(2d)^{-1}r_{0}

\rP\left(\sup_{y\in M}\left\lVert\pr_{T_{y}M}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 8\sqrt{d}\sqrt{4\log 2d+2\log r^{-1}_{0}+2\log_{+}\Vol M+2\log\delta^{-1}}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

we finish the proof.
∎

The following result is a counterpart of Lemma above for
M^{*}
.

Lemma G.5
.

For all
\delta>0
with probability at least
1-\delta
for all
i\leq I_{2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\pr_{z_{i}^{*}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq 8dL_{M}\varepsilon_{N}+8\sqrt{d}(1+7L_{M}\varepsilon_{N})\sqrt{2\log 2d+2\log n+2\log(1/\delta)}

Proof.

Note that by Lemma
5.3
for
n
large enough
\left\lVert\Phi^{*}_{i}\right\rVert_{C^{2}\left(B_{d}(0,7\varepsilon_{N})\right)}\leq L_{M}+L^{*}(7\varepsilon)^{\beta-2}_{N}\leq 2L_{M}
for all
i\leq N
. Then

\left\lVert\pr_{z_{i}^{*}}Z_{D}\right\rVert\leq\sup_{z\in B_{d}(0,7\varepsilon_{N})}\left\lVert\pr_{z}Z_{D}\right\rVert

Apply
lemma

G.4
for each
\Phi^{*}_{1},\ldots,\Phi^{*}_{N}
with
\delta/N
and combine the resulting inequalities using that
N\leq n
.
∎

Lemma G.6
.

For all
i\in I_{2}
and
z\in B_{d}\left(z^{*}_{i},4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\right)
with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}+c_{t}\left\langle X(t)-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{3}(d\log n)^{2}.

Proof.

By
proposition

G.1
and choice of
z
with probability at least
1-n^{-2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert X(0)-\Phi(z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert\leq 2(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\lesssim(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d\log n},

\displaystyle\left\lVert z-z^{*}_{i}\right\rVert\leq 4(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{20}C(n)\lesssim(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d\log n},

since
C(n)\lesssim\sqrt{d\log n}
.
We estimate the terms separately. Recall that

R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})=\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})-\Phi^{*}_{i}(z)-\nabla\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})(z^{*}_{i}-z),

so by (
G.1
) for
n
large enough

c^{2}_{t}\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left(2L^{*}\left\lVert z-z^{*}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\right)^{2}\lesssim(\sigma_{t}/c_{t})^{4}d^{2}\log^{2}n<\sigma_{t}^{2}.

(G.4)

We move the second term

\left\langle X(0)-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle=c_{t}\left\langle X(0)-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle+\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle.

The first term is bounded similarly using (
G.1
)

c_{t}\left|\left\langle X(0)-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i}),R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right|\leq c_{t}\left\lVert X(0)-c_{t}\Phi^{*}_{i}(z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert\left\lVert R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rVert\lesssim c_{t}\left((\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d\log n}\right)^{3}.

While the second term by
corollary

G.3
with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
is bounded by

\left|\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left\langle Z_{D},R_{i}(z,z^{*}_{i})\right\rangle\right|\lesssim\sigma_{t}c_{t}\left((\sigma_{t}/c_{t})\sqrt{d\log n}\right)^{2}\lesssim c^{-1}_{t}\sigma^{3}_{t}d\log n.

Summing the inequalities up and noting that
1>c_{t}\geq 1/2
we finish the proof.
∎
